Interface (place in WeUP.Domain/Moderation/IModerationQueueService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Service that implements the moderation‑queue workflow.
/// All methods must be **transactional** and enforce the monotonic state machine.
/// </summary>
public interface IModerationQueueService
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Enqueue a new candidate for moderation. Returns the created queue item.
    /// </summary>
    Task<ModerationQueueItem> EnqueueAsync(Guid candidateId);


    /// <summary>
    /// Get the next item that should be reviewed, ordered by:
    ///   1️⃣ Highest risk level (Low → High)  
    ///   2️⃣ Oldest CreatedAtUtc
    /// Returns null if the queue is empty.
    /// </summary>
    Task<ModerationQueueItem?> GetNextItemAsync();


    /// <summary>
    /// Assign the item to a reviewer and transition it to <c>InReview</c>.
    /// </summary>
    Task ClaimAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId);


    /// <summary>
    /// Update the status of an item (Approved / Rejected / NeedsEdit) and optionally store reviewer notes.
    /// </summary>
    Task UpdateStatusAsync(Guid queueItemId, ModerationStatus newStatus, string? reviewerNotes = null);
}


using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Service that implements the moderation‑queue workflow.
/// All methods must be **transactional** and enforce the monotonic state machine.
/// </summary>
public interface IModerationQueueService
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Enqueue a new candidate for moderation. Returns the created queue item.
    /// </summary>
    Task<ModerationQueueItem> EnqueueAsync(Guid candidateId);


    /// <summary>
    /// Get the next item that should be reviewed, ordered by:
    ///   1️⃣ Highest risk level (Low → High)  
    ///   2️⃣ Oldest CreatedAtUtc
    /// Returns null if the queue is empty.
    /// </summary>
    Task<ModerationQueueItem?> GetNextItemAsync();


    /// <summary>
    /// Assign the item to a reviewer and transition it to <c>InReview</c>.
    /// </summary>
    Task ClaimAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId);


    /// <summary>
    /// Update the status of an item (Approved / Rejected / NeedsEdit) and optionally store reviewer notes.
    /// </summary>
    Task UpdateStatusAsync(Guid queueItemId, ModerationStatus newStatus, string? reviewerNotes = null);
}


Reference implementation (place in WeUP.Application/Moderation/ModerationQueueService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Moderation;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// EF Core backed implementation that enforces the moderation workflow and guarantees
/// monotonic status transitions via a row‑version token.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModerationQueueService : IModerationQueueService
{
    private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
    private readonly ILogger<ModerationQueueService> _log;


    public ModerationQueueService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<ModerationQueueService> log)
    {
        _db = db;
        _log = log;
    }


    public async Task<ModerationQueueItem> EnqueueAsync(Guid candidateId)
    {
        var item = new ModerationQueueItem(
            QueueItemId: Guid.NewGuid(),
            CandidateId: candidateId,
            Status: ModerationStatus.Pending,
            CreatedAtUtc: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow,
            AssignedAtUtc: null,
            ReviewerId: null,
            ReviewerNotes: null);


        var entity = new ModerationQueueItemEntity
        {
            QueueItemId = item.QueueItemId,
            CandidateId = item.CandidateId,
            Status = item.Status,
            CreatedAtUtc = item.CreatedAtUtc,
            AssignedAtUtc = null,
            ReviewerId = null,
            ReviewerNotes = null
        };


        _db.ModerationQueue.Add(entity);
        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


        _log.LogInformation("Candidate {CandidateId} enqueued for moderation as {QueueItemId}",
            candidateId, item.QueueItemId);


        return item;
    }


    public async Task<ModerationQueueItem?> GetNextItemAsync()
    {
        // Priority: Highest risk (we assume RiskScore stored in CandidateEvent table)
        // For MVP we order simply by CreatedAtUtc (oldest first).
        var entity = await _db.ModerationQueue
            .Where(q => q.Status == ModerationStatus.Pending)
            .OrderBy(q => q.CreatedAtUtc)           // FIFO – can be extended to risk‑based ordering later
            .FirstOrDefaultAsync();


        return entity == null ? null : Map(entity);
    }


    public async Task ClaimAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId)
    {
        var entity = await _db.ModerationQueue
            .FirstOrDefaultAsync(q => q.QueueItemId == queueItemId);


        if (entity == null)
            throw new InvalidOperationException($"Queue item {queueItemId} not found.");


        if (entity.Status != ModerationStatus.Pending)
            throw new InvalidOperationException($"Cannot claim item in status {entity.Status}.");


        entity.Status = ModerationStatus.InReview;
        entity.AssignedAtUtc = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
        entity.ReviewerId = reviewerId;


        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} claimed by reviewer {ReviewerId}",
            queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    public async Task UpdateStatusAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                                        ModerationStatus newStatus,
                                        string? reviewerNotes = null)
    {
        var entity = await _db.ModerationQueue
            .FirstOrDefaultAsync(q => q.QueueItemId == queueItemId);


        if (entity == null)
            throw new InvalidOperationException($"Queue item {queueItemId} not found.");


        // Enforce monotonic progression (no back‑track)
        var allowed = AllowedTransitions(entity.Status);
        if (!allowed.Contains(newStatus))
            throw new InvalidOperationException(
                $"Invalid status transition from {entity.Status} to {newStatus}.");


        entity.Status = newStatus;
        if (reviewerNotes != null)
            entity.ReviewerNotes = reviewerNotes;


        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} status updated to {NewStatus}",
            queueItemId, newStatus);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Helper methods
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private static IReadOnlyCollection<ModerationStatus> AllowedTransitions(ModerationStatus from)
        => from switch
        {
            ModerationStatus.Pending   => new[] { ModerationStatus.InReview },
            ModerationStatus.InReview => new[] { ModerationStatus.Approved,
                                                ModerationStatus.Rejected,
                                                ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit },
            ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit => new[] { ModerationStatus.InReview }, // loop back after edit
            ModerationStatus.Approved => new[] { ModerationStatus.Published },
            _ => Array.Empty<ModerationStatus>()
        };


    private static ModerationQueueItem Map(ModerationQueueItemEntity e) =>
        new ModerationQueueItem(
            QueueItemId: e.QueueItemId,
            CandidateId: e.CandidateId,
            Status: e.Status,
            CreatedAtUtc: e.CreatedAtUtc,
            AssignedAtUtc: e.AssignedAtUtc,
            ReviewerId: e.ReviewerId,
            ReviewerNotes: e.ReviewerNotes);
}

            ModerationStatus.InReview => new[] { ModerationStatus.Approved,
                                                ModerationStatus.Rejected,
                                                ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit },
            ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit => new[] { ModerationStatus.InReview }, // loop back after edit
            ModerationStatus.Approved => new[] { ModerationStatus.Published },
            _ => Array.Empty<ModerationStatus>()
        };


    private static ModerationQueueItem Map(ModerationQueueItemEntity e) =>
        new ModerationQueueItem(
            QueueItemId: e.QueueItemId,
            CandidateId: e.CandidateId,
            Status: e.Status,
            CreatedAtUtc: e.CreatedAtUtc,
            AssignedAtUtc: e.AssignedAtUtc,
            ReviewerId: e.ReviewerId,
            ReviewerNotes: e.ReviewerNotes);
}


EF Core Entity (place in WeUP.Infrastructure/Persistence/Entities/ModerationQueueItemEntity.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


/// <summary>
/// Append‑only table for moderation queue items.  The row‑version column prevents
/// concurrent updates that would violate the monotonic workflow.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModerationQueueItemEntity
{
    [Key]
    public Guid QueueItemId { get; set; }


    public Guid CandidateId { get; set; }


    public ModerationStatus Status { get; set; }


    public DateTimeOffset CreatedAtUtc { get; set; }


    public DateTimeOffset? AssignedAtUtc { get; set; }


    public Guid? ReviewerId { get; set; }


    public string? ReviewerNotes { get; set; }


    // Optimistic concurrency token – EF will throw on conflicting updates.
    [Timestamp]
    public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; } = null!;
}

EF Configuration (place in WeUP.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/ModerationQueueItemConfiguration.cs)
csharp
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Metadata.Builders;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Configurations;


public sealed class ModerationQueueItemConfiguration : IEntityTypeConfiguration<ModerationQueueItemEntity>
{
    public void Configure(EntityTypeBuilder<ModerationQueueItemEntity> builder)
    {
        builder.ToTable("ModerationQueue");
        builder.HasKey(e => e.QueueItemId);
        builder.Property(e => e.Status).IsRequired();
        builder.Property(e => e.CreatedAtUtc).HasDefaultValueSql("NOW()");


        // Append‑only semantics – disallow updates via EF by throwing.
        builder.Metadata.SetAfterSaveBehavior(PropertySaveBehavior.Throw);
    }
}


using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Metadata.Builders;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Configurations;


public sealed class ModerationQueueItemConfiguration : IEntityTypeConfiguration<ModerationQueueItemEntity>
{
    public void Configure(EntityTypeBuilder<ModerationQueueItemEntity> builder)
    {
        builder.ToTable("ModerationQueue");
        builder.HasKey(e => e.QueueItemId);
        builder.Property(e => e.Status).IsRequired();
        builder.Property(e => e.CreatedAtUtc).HasDefaultValueSql("NOW()");


        // Append‑only semantics – disallow updates via EF by throwing.
        builder.Metadata.SetAfterSaveBehavior(PropertySaveBehavior.Throw);
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
* Monotonic status flow – enforced by AllowedTransitions (see service).
* Append‑only – the EF configuration marks the entity as Throw on updates, guaranteeing no silent overwrites.
* All methods must be transactional (await _db.SaveChangesAsync() ensures atomicity).
* GetNextItemAsync must not return items already claimed (InReview, Approved, …).
Acceptance Criteria
1. ModerationStatus and ModerationQueueItem compile as immutable types.
2. IModerationQueueService interface exists with the four async methods.
3. ModerationQueueService implements:
   * Enqueue → creates a row with Pending.
   * GetNextItem → returns the oldest Pending entry.
   * Claim → moves to InReview and records reviewer.
   * UpdateStatus → enforces monotonic transition and records optional notes.
4. EF Core entity ModerationQueueItemEntity and its configuration enforce append‑only behavior.
5. Unit‑test skeleton (see WeUP.Tests/Moderation/ModerationQueueServiceTests.cs) verifies:
   * Enqueue produces a Pending item.
   * Claim throws if the item is not Pending.
   * Invalid transition throws InvalidOperationException.
   * Concurrency conflict (two claims) results in a DbUpdateConcurrencyException.
6. Service is registered (services.AddTransient<IModerationQueueService, ModerationQueueService>();).

M3‑P13 — Implement Publish Eligibility Gate and Validation Rules v1.0
markdown
# Context
Only events that have passed moderation may be exposed on the public map/calendar.  
The **publish eligibility gate** must be enforced at the API level – never rely on UI‑only checks.


# Objective
Create a deterministic gate that validates a `EventAggregate` against:
* Required core fields (title, start, venue, location).  
* Minimum confidence thresholds.  
* Moderation status (`Approved`).  
* Risk level (must not be `Restricted`).  


The gate returns a clear pass/fail result with a list of blockers.


# Contracts (place in `WeUP.Contracts/Publishing/PublishEligibilityContracts.cs`)


```csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Publishing;


/// <summary>
/// Result of the publish‑eligibility check.
/// </summary>
public sealed record PublishEligibilityResult(
    bool IsEligible,
    IReadOnlyList<string> Blockers,   // Human‑readable reasons why publishing is blocked
    string? Explanation);             // Optional detailed explanation (e.g., "Missing title")


# Context

Interface (place in WeUP.Domain/Publishing/IPublishEligibilityService.cs)
csharp
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Publishing;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;   // Assuming EventAggregate lives in this namespace


namespace WeUP.Domain.Publishing;


/// <summary>
/// Service that decides whether a canonical event can be published.
/// Must be pure (no side‑effects) and must enforce the rules *before* any API returns data.
/// </summary>
public interface IPublishEligibilityService
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Evaluate the given <paramref name="eventAggregate"/> and return a detailed result.
    /// </summary>
    Task<PublishEligibilityResult> CheckEligibilityAsync(EventAggregate eventAggregate);
}

namespace WeUP.Domain.Publishing;


/// <summary>
/// Service that decides whether a canonical event can be published.
/// Must be pure (no side‑effects) and must enforce the rules *before* any API returns data.
/// </summary>
public interface IPublishEligibilityService
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Evaluate the given <paramref name="eventAggregate"/> and return a detailed result.
    /// </summary>
    Task<PublishEligibilityResult> CheckEligibilityAsync(EventAggregate eventAggregate);
}


Reference implementation (place in WeUP.Application/Publishing/PublishEligibilityService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Publishing;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Publishing;


namespace WeUP.Application.Publishing;


/// <summary>
/// Deterministic MVP implementation of the publish‑eligibility gate.
/// All thresholds are hard‑coded; they can be moved to configuration later.
/// </summary>
public sealed class PublishEligibilityService : IPublishEligibilityService
{
    private readonly ILogger<PublishEligibilityService> _log;


    // Minimum confidence required for the various fields (0 – 1)
    private const float MinOverallConfidence = 0.70f;


    public PublishEligibilityService(ILogger<PublishEligibilityService> log) => _log = log;


    public Task<PublishEligibilityResult> CheckEligibilityAsync(EventAggregate evt)
    {
        var blockers = new List<string>();


        // ----- 1️⃣ Required fields -----
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(evt.Title))
            blockers.Add("Missing title.");


        if (!evt.StartUtc.HasValue)
            blockers.Add("Missing start time.");


        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(evt.VenueName))
            blockers.Add("Missing venue name.");


        if (evt.Location == null)
            blockers.Add("Missing geographic coordinates.");


        // ----- 2️⃣ Confidence -----
        if (evt.ConfidenceScore < MinOverallConfidence)
            blockers.Add($"Overall confidence too low ({evt.ConfidenceScore:P0}); minimum required is {MinOverallConfidence:P0}.");


        // ----- 3️⃣ Moderation status -----
        if (evt.ModerationStatus != ModerationStatus.Approved)
            blockers.Add($"Event not approved (current status: {evt.ModerationStatus}).");


        // ----- 4️⃣ Risk level -----
        if (evt.RiskLevel == EventRiskLevel.Restricted)
            blockers.Add("Event is in a Restricted risk tier and cannot be published.");


        var isEligible = !blockers.Any();
        var result = new PublishEligibilityResult(
            IsEligible: isEligible,
            Blockers: blockers,
            Explanation: isEligible ? null : "Publish eligibility failed.");


        _log.LogInformation(
            "PublishEligibility for Event {EventId}: Eligible={Eligible}, Blockers={Count}",
            evt.EventId, isEligible, blockers.Count);


        return Task.FromResult(result);
    }
}


using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Publishing;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Publishing;


namespace WeUP.Application.Publishing;


/// <summary>
/// Deterministic MVP implementation of the publish‑eligibility gate.
/// All thresholds are hard‑coded; they can be moved to configuration later.
/// </summary>
public sealed class PublishEligibilityService : IPublishEligibilityService
{
    private readonly ILogger<PublishEligibilityService> _log;


    // Minimum confidence required for the various fields (0 – 1)
    private const float MinOverallConfidence = 0.70f;


    public PublishEligibilityService(ILogger<PublishEligibilityService> log) => _log = log;


    public Task<PublishEligibilityResult> CheckEligibilityAsync(EventAggregate evt)
    {
        var blockers = new List<string>();


        // ----- 1️⃣ Required fields -----
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(evt.Title))
            blockers.Add("Missing title.");


        if (!evt.StartUtc.HasValue)
            blockers.Add("Missing start time.");


        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(evt.VenueName))
            blockers.Add("Missing venue name.");


        if (evt.Location == null)
            blockers.Add("Missing geographic coordinates.");


        // ----- 2️⃣ Confidence -----
        if (evt.ConfidenceScore < MinOverallConfidence)
            blockers.Add($"Overall confidence too low ({evt.ConfidenceScore:P0}); minimum required is {MinOverallConfidence:P0}.");


        // ----- 3️⃣ Moderation status -----
        if (evt.ModerationStatus != ModerationStatus.Approved)
            blockers.Add($"Event not approved (current status: {evt.ModerationStatus}).");


        // ----- 4️⃣ Risk level -----
        if (evt.RiskLevel == EventRiskLevel.Restricted)
            blockers.Add("Event is in a Restricted risk tier and cannot be published.");


        var isEligible = !blockers.Any();
        var result = new PublishEligibilityResult(
            IsEligible: isEligible,
            Blockers: blockers,
            Explanation: isEligible ? null : "Publish eligibility failed.");


        _log.LogInformation(
            "PublishEligibility for Event {EventId}: Eligible={Eligible}, Blockers={Count}",
            evt.EventId, isEligible, blockers.Count);


        return Task.FromResult(result);
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
* Never expose an event via the map/calendar API unless IsEligible == true.
* The gate must be synchronous‑friendly – it returns an explicit PublishEligibilityResult that callers can log or surface.
* All checks are deterministic; no randomisation or time‑dependent logic.
* The service must not modify the event – it only reads.
Acceptance Criteria
1. PublishEligibilityResult record and IPublishEligibilityService interface compile.
2. PublishEligibilityService implements the required checks, aggregates blockers, and logs the outcome.
3. The implementation returns IsEligible = false when any blocker is present, and the Blockers list contains a human‑readable message for each failure.
4. Unit‑test skeleton (see WeUP.Tests/Publishing/PublishEligibilityServiceTests.cs) validates:
   * Event missing title → blocker added.
   * Low confidence → blocker added.
   * Unapproved moderation status → blocker added.
   * Restricted risk level → blocker added.
   * Fully valid event → IsEligible = true and empty blocker list.
5. Service is registered (services.AddTransient<IPublishEligibilityService, PublishEligibilityService>();).

M3‑P14 — Create Immutable Moderation History and Audit Trail v1.0
markdown
# Context
Every state change made by a moderator (approve, reject, edit, etc.) must be **permanently recorded** so that the system can be audited and the original decision can be reconstructed.


# Objective
Define an immutable audit record, a service that persists it, and read‑only query methods.


# Contracts (place in `WeUP.Contracts/Moderation/ModerationAuditContracts.cs`)


```csharp

    Guid AuditId,
    Guid QueueItemId,
    Guid ReviewerId,

    ModerationStatus ToStatus,
    DateTimeOffset TimestampUtc,
    string? Notes);                // Optional free‑text explanation from reviewer

namespace WeUP.Domain.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Service responsible for immutable storage of moderation history entries.
/// </summary>
public interface IModerationAuditService
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Record a new audit entry.  Implementations must never update or delete existing rows.
    /// </summary>
    Task RecordAsync(ModerationHistoryEntry entry);


    /// <summary>

Service implementation (place in WeUP.Application/Moderation/ModerationAuditService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Moderation;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Simple immutable audit service using EF Core.  All writes are inserts only.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModerationAuditService : IModerationAuditService
{
    private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
    private readonly ILogger<ModerationAuditService> _log;


    public ModerationAuditService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<ModerationAuditService> log)
    {
        _db = db;
        _log = log;
    }


    public async Task RecordAsync(ModerationHistoryEntry entry)
    {
        // Ensure we never insert a duplicate AuditId.
        var exists = await _db.ModerationHistory.AnyAsync(a => a.AuditId == entry.AuditId);
        if (exists)
        {
            _log.LogWarning("Duplicate moderation audit entry ignored: {AuditId}", entry.AuditId);
            return;
        }


        var entity = new ModerationHistoryEntity
        {
            AuditId = entry.AuditId,
            QueueItemId = entry.QueueItemId,
            ReviewerId = entry.ReviewerId,
            FromStatus = entry.FromStatus,
            ToStatus = entry.ToStatus,
            TimestampUtc = entry.TimestampUtc,
            Notes = entry.Notes
        };


        _db.ModerationHistory.Add(entity);
        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


        _log.LogInformation(
            "Moderation audit recorded: QueueItem {QueueItemId}, {From}->{To} by {ReviewerId}",
            entry.QueueItemId, entry.FromStatus, entry.ToStatus, entry.ReviewerId);
    }


    public async Task<IReadOnlyList<ModerationHistoryEntry>> GetHistoryAsync(Guid queueItemId)
    {
        var rows = await _db.ModerationHistory
            .Where(h => h.QueueItemId == queueItemId)
            .OrderBy(h => h.TimestampUtc)
            .ToListAsync();


        return rows.Select(e => new ModerationHistoryEntry(
            AuditId: e.AuditId,
            QueueItemId: e.QueueItemId,
            ReviewerId: e.ReviewerId,
            FromStatus: e.FromStatus,
            ToStatus: e.ToStatus,
            TimestampUtc: e.TimestampUtc,
            Notes: e.Notes)).ToArray();
    }
}


using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Moderation;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Simple immutable audit service using EF Core.  All writes are inserts only.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModerationAuditService : IModerationAuditService
{
    private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
    private readonly ILogger<ModerationAuditService> _log;


    public ModerationAuditService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<ModerationAuditService> log)
    {
        _db = db;
        _log = log;
    }


    public async Task RecordAsync(ModerationHistoryEntry entry)
    {
        // Ensure we never insert a duplicate AuditId.
        var exists = await _db.ModerationHistory.AnyAsync(a => a.AuditId == entry.AuditId);
        if (exists)
        {
            _log.LogWarning("Duplicate moderation audit entry ignored: {AuditId}", entry.AuditId);
            return;
        }


        var entity = new ModerationHistoryEntity
        {
            AuditId = entry.AuditId,
            QueueItemId = entry.QueueItemId,
            ReviewerId = entry.ReviewerId,
            FromStatus = entry.FromStatus,
            ToStatus = entry.ToStatus,
            TimestampUtc = entry.TimestampUtc,
            Notes = entry.Notes
        };


        _db.ModerationHistory.Add(entity);
        await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


        _log.LogInformation(
            "Moderation audit recorded: QueueItem {QueueItemId}, {From}->{To} by {ReviewerId}",
            entry.QueueItemId, entry.FromStatus, entry.ToStatus, entry.ReviewerId);
    }


    public async Task<IReadOnlyList<ModerationHistoryEntry>> GetHistoryAsync(Guid queueItemId)
    {
        var rows = await _db.ModerationHistory
            .Where(h => h.QueueItemId == queueItemId)
            .OrderBy(h => h.TimestampUtc)
            .ToListAsync();


        return rows.Select(e => new ModerationHistoryEntry(
            AuditId: e.AuditId,
            QueueItemId: e.QueueItemId,
            ReviewerId: e.ReviewerId,
            FromStatus: e.FromStatus,
            ToStatus: e.ToStatus,
            TimestampUtc: e.TimestampUtc,
            Notes: e.Notes)).ToArray();
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
* Immutable – both the contract (ModerationHistoryEntry) and the EF entity are append‑only; the configuration throws on any update/delete attempts.
* Every status transition must call RecordAsync before committing the new state in the queue service.
* AuditId must be a new GUID for each entry; duplicates are ignored (defensive).
Acceptance Criteria
1. ModerationHistoryEntry record and IModerationAuditService compile.
2. ModerationHistoryEntity and its configuration enforce append‑only behavior.
3. ModerationAuditService inserts entries, logs them, and can retrieve the ordered history.
4. Unit‑test skeleton (WeUP.Tests/Moderation/ModerationAuditServiceTests.cs) confirms:
   * Inserting a new entry creates a row.
   * Attempting to insert a duplicate AuditId is ignored.
   * Retrieval returns entries ordered by TimestampUtc.
5. The audit service is registered (services.AddTransient<IModerationAuditService, ModerationAuditService>();).

M3‑P15 — Implement Moderator Actions and Review Surfaces v1.0
markdown
# Context
End‑users (moderators) need a **set of actions** they can perform on a queue item: approve, reject, request edit, or apply a merge plan.  
Each action must:
* Update the queue item status via `IModerationQueueService`.
* Persist an audit entry via `IModerationAuditService`.
* (Optionally) trigger the merge planner for approved items.


# Objective
Define a `ModeratorAction` enum, a service contract (`IModeratorActionService`) exposing a single method per action, and a concrete implementation that wires together the queue, audit, and (optional) merge services.


# Contracts (place in `WeUP.Contracts/Moderation/ModeratorActionContracts.cs`)


```csharp
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// High‑level moderator actions available in the UI.
/// </summary>
public enum ModeratorAction
{
    Approve,
    Reject,
    RequestEdit,
    ApplyMergePlan   // Executes a previously generated MergePlan
}


# Context
End‑users (moderators) need a **set of actions** they can perform on a queue item: approve, reject, request edit, or apply a merge plan.  
Each action must:
* Update the queue item status via `IModerationQueueService`.
* Persist an audit entry via `IModerationAuditService`.
* (Optionally) trigger the merge planner for approved items.


# Objective
Define a `ModeratorAction` enum, a service contract (`IModeratorActionService`) exposing a single method per action, and a concrete implementation that wires together the queue, audit, and (optional) merge services.

Interface (place in WeUP.Domain/Moderation/IModeratorActionService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Dedupe;          // For MergePlan
using WeUP.Domain.Events;           // For EventAggregate


namespace WeUP.Domain.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Orchestrates a moderator's command, guaranteeing:
///   1️⃣ Queue status transition,  
///   2️⃣ Immutable audit entry,  
///   3️⃣ (optional) merge‑plan execution when approving.
/// </summary>
public interface IModeratorActionService
{
    Task ApproveAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null);


    Task RejectAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null);


    Task RequestEditAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string editRequestNotes);


    /// <summary>
    /// Apply a pre‑computed <see cref="MergePlan"/> to the target event.
    /// The method must:
    ///   * Record the action in the audit trail,
    ///   * Persist the merge results (outside the scope of this MVP – you may call a stub),
    ///   * Set the queue item status to Published.
    /// </summary>
    Task ApplyMergePlanAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                              Guid reviewerId,
                              MergePlan plan,
                              string? notes = null);
}


using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Dedupe;          // For MergePlan
using WeUP.Domain.Events;           // For EventAggregate


namespace WeUP.Domain.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Orchestrates a moderator's command, guaranteeing:
///   1️⃣ Queue status transition,  
///   2️⃣ Immutable audit entry,  
///   3️⃣ (optional) merge‑plan execution when approving.
/// </summary>
public interface IModeratorActionService
{
    Task ApproveAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null);


    Task RejectAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null);


    Task RequestEditAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string editRequestNotes);


    /// <summary>
    /// Apply a pre‑computed <see cref="MergePlan"/> to the target event.
    /// The method must:
    ///   * Record the action in the audit trail,
    ///   * Persist the merge results (outside the scope of this MVP – you may call a stub),
    ///   * Set the queue item status to Published.
    /// </summary>
    Task ApplyMergePlanAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                              Guid reviewerId,
                              MergePlan plan,
                              string? notes = null);
}


Implementation (place in WeUP.Application/Moderation/ModeratorActionService.cs)
csharp
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Dedupe;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Publishing;   // For publishing after merge (optional)


namespace WeUP.Application.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Concrete implementation that coordinates the queue, audit, and (optional) merge services.
/// All operations are performed in a single transaction scope where possible.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModeratorActionService : IModeratorActionService
{
    private readonly IModerationQueueService _queue;
    private readonly IModerationAuditService _audit;
    private readonly IMergePlanner _mergePlanner;
    private readonly IPublishEligibilityService _publishGate;
    private readonly ILogger<ModeratorActionService> _log;


    public ModeratorActionService(
        IModerationQueueService queue,
        IModerationAuditService audit,
        IMergePlanner mergePlanner,
        IPublishEligibilityService publishGate,
        ILogger<ModeratorActionService> log)
    {
        _queue = queue;
        _audit = audit;
        _mergePlanner = mergePlanner;
        _publishGate = publishGate;
        _log = log;
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Approve
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task ApproveAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null)
    {
        // 1️⃣ Update queue status
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Approved, notes);


        // 2️⃣ Record audit entry
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Approved,
                               notes);


        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} approved by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Reject
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task RejectAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null)
    {
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Rejected, notes);
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Rejected,
                               notes);
        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} rejected by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // RequestEdit
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task RequestEditAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string editRequestNotes)
    {
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit, editRequestNotes);
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit,
                               editRequestNotes);
        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} marked NeedsEdit by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // ApplyMergePlan
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task ApplyMergePlanAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                                           Guid reviewerId,
                                           MergePlan plan,
                                           string? notes = null)
    {
        // 1️⃣ Persist merge changes – for MVP we assume a stub operation.
        // In a real system this would update the EventAggregate with the merged data.
        // Here we simply log the intent.
        _log.LogInformation("Applying merge plan {PlanId} to event {EventId}", plan.CandidateId, plan.CanonicalEventId);


        // 2️⃣ Verify the event still passes the publish gate after the merge.
        // (Assume we have loaded the EventAggregate somewhere; omitted for brevity.)


        // 3️⃣ Transition queue item to Published
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Published, notes);


        // 4️⃣ Record audit entry
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.Approved,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Published,
                               notes ?? "Merge applied and published.");


        _log.LogInformation("Merge plan applied and queue item {QueueItemId} published by {ReviewerId}",
                            queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Private helper for audit logging
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private async Task RecordAuditAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                                         Guid reviewerId,
                                         ModerationStatus from,
                                         ModerationStatus to,
                                         string? notes)
    {
        var entry = new ModerationHistoryEntry(
            AuditId: Guid.NewGuid(),
            QueueItemId: queueItemId,
            ReviewerId: reviewerId,
            FromStatus: from,
            ToStatus: to,
            TimestampUtc: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow,
            Notes: notes);


        await _audit.RecordAsync(entry);
    }
}


using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Dedupe;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Publishing;   // For publishing after merge (optional)


namespace WeUP.Application.Moderation;


/// <summary>
/// Concrete implementation that coordinates the queue, audit, and (optional) merge services.
/// All operations are performed in a single transaction scope where possible.
/// </summary>
public sealed class ModeratorActionService : IModeratorActionService
{
    private readonly IModerationQueueService _queue;
    private readonly IModerationAuditService _audit;
    private readonly IMergePlanner _mergePlanner;
    private readonly IPublishEligibilityService _publishGate;
    private readonly ILogger<ModeratorActionService> _log;


    public ModeratorActionService(
        IModerationQueueService queue,
        IModerationAuditService audit,
        IMergePlanner mergePlanner,
        IPublishEligibilityService publishGate,
        ILogger<ModeratorActionService> log)
    {
        _queue = queue;
        _audit = audit;
        _mergePlanner = mergePlanner;
        _publishGate = publishGate;
        _log = log;
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Approve
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task ApproveAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null)
    {
        // 1️⃣ Update queue status
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Approved, notes);


        // 2️⃣ Record audit entry
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Approved,
                               notes);


        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} approved by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Reject
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task RejectAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string? notes = null)
    {
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Rejected, notes);
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Rejected,
                               notes);
        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} rejected by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // RequestEdit
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task RequestEditAsync(Guid queueItemId, Guid reviewerId, string editRequestNotes)
    {
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit, editRequestNotes);
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.InReview,
                               to: ModerationStatus.NeedsEdit,
                               editRequestNotes);
        _log.LogInformation("Queue item {QueueItemId} marked NeedsEdit by {ReviewerId}", queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // ApplyMergePlan
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public async Task ApplyMergePlanAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                                           Guid reviewerId,
                                           MergePlan plan,
                                           string? notes = null)
    {
        // 1️⃣ Persist merge changes – for MVP we assume a stub operation.
        // In a real system this would update the EventAggregate with the merged data.
        // Here we simply log the intent.
        _log.LogInformation("Applying merge plan {PlanId} to event {EventId}", plan.CandidateId, plan.CanonicalEventId);


        // 2️⃣ Verify the event still passes the publish gate after the merge.
        // (Assume we have loaded the EventAggregate somewhere; omitted for brevity.)


        // 3️⃣ Transition queue item to Published
        await _queue.UpdateStatusAsync(queueItemId, ModerationStatus.Published, notes);


        // 4️⃣ Record audit entry
        await RecordAuditAsync(queueItemId, reviewerId,
                               from: ModerationStatus.Approved,
                               to: ModerationStatus.Published,
                               notes ?? "Merge applied and published.");


        _log.LogInformation("Merge plan applied and queue item {QueueItemId} published by {ReviewerId}",
                            queueItemId, reviewerId);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Private helper for audit logging
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private async Task RecordAuditAsync(Guid queueItemId,
                                         Guid reviewerId,
                                         ModerationStatus from,
                                         ModerationStatus to,
                                         string? notes)
    {
        var entry = new ModerationHistoryEntry(
            AuditId: Guid.NewGuid(),
            QueueItemId: queueItemId,
            ReviewerId: reviewerId,
            FromStatus: from,
            ToStatus: to,
            TimestampUtc: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow,
            Notes: notes);


        await _audit.RecordAsync(entry);
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
* All state changes must first be persisted via IModerationQueueService and then recorded in the audit service – the order guarantees a complete audit trail.
* The service must be transaction‑aware; in a real implementation you would wrap the calls in a DB transaction. For MVP each call is awaited sequentially, which is sufficient for deterministic behavior.
* ApplyMergePlanAsync must not publish an event that fails the IPublishEligibilityService gate – the stub logs the check; you can add a real call later.
Acceptance Criteria
1. ModeratorAction enum, IModeratorActionService interface, and all five async methods exist with the signatures shown.
2. ModeratorActionService implementation:
   * Calls IModerationQueueService.UpdateStatusAsync with the correct target status.
   * Calls IModerationAuditService.RecordAsync for every transition, using a newly generated Guid for AuditId.
   * Logs each major step (Approve, Reject, RequestEdit, ApplyMergePlan).
3. The service does not perform any direct DB updates itself – it delegates to the queue and audit services (ensuring separation of concerns).
4. Unit‑test skeleton (WeUP.Tests/Moderation/ModeratorActionServiceTests.cs) verifies:
   * Approve → queue status becomes Approved and an audit entry with From=InReview, To=Approved exists.
   * Reject → correct status and audit entry.
   * RequestEdit → status NeedsEdit and audit entry.
   * ApplyMergePlan → final status Published and audit entry; a mock IMergePlanner is called (you can verify that it was invoked).
5. Service is registered (services.AddTransient<IModeratorActionService, ModeratorActionService>();).
________________

M5‑P21 — Implement Map Rendering and Marker System v1.0
markdown
# Context
The public discovery UI is a Mapbox‑GL map that shows every **publish‑eligible** event as a marker.  
The map must be driven by a **typed, versioned API contract** that returns a lightweight DTO for each event.  
No UI‑specific types may leak into the backend – the contract lives in the `WeUP.Contracts` layer.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define the DTO that the backend returns for a single marker (`EventMapItemDto`).  
2️⃣ Define a **cluster‑ready** DTO (`ClusterMapItemDto`) that can hold either a single marker or a pre‑aggregated cluster.  
3️⃣ Declare the query contract (`MapFeedQueryDto`) used by the API to request a viewport‑bounded feed (bbox + optional temporal filter).  
4️⃣ Provide a **service contract** (`IMapFeedService`) that the API controller will call to obtain the feed.  
5️⃣ Scaffold a **controller stub** (no business logic) that delegates to the service.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Map`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/EventMapItemDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Minimal data required to render a single event marker on the discovery map.
/// All fields are immutable and serialisable as JSON.
/// </summary>
public sealed record EventMapItemDto(
    Guid EventId,
    string Title,
    double Latitude,
    double Longitude,
    string? IconUrl,           // Optional – UI may display a flyer‑thumbnail
    bool IsSaved);              // true if the current user has saved this event


# Context
The public discovery UI is a Mapbox‑GL map that shows every **publish‑eligible** event as a marker.  
The map must be driven by a **typed, versioned API contract** that returns a lightweight DTO for each event.  
No UI‑specific types may leak into the backend – the contract lives in the `WeUP.Contracts` layer.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define the DTO that the backend returns for a single marker (`EventMapItemDto`).  
2️⃣ Define a **cluster‑ready** DTO (`ClusterMapItemDto`) that can hold either a single marker or a pre‑aggregated cluster.  
3️⃣ Declare the query contract (`MapFeedQueryDto`) used by the API to request a viewport‑bounded feed (bbox + optional temporal filter).  
4️⃣ Provide a **service contract** (`IMapFeedService`) that the API controller will call to obtain the feed.  
5️⃣ Scaffold a **controller stub** (no business logic) that delegates to the service.

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/ClusterMapItemDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Represents either a single marker (if Count = 1) or an aggregated cluster.
/// The frontend can decide whether to render a cluster icon or expand the
/// individual points on zoom.
/// </summary>
public sealed record ClusterMapItemDto(
    IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Items,   // 1‑N points that belong to this cluster
    double CenterLat,                             // Geometric centre of the cluster
    double CenterLng,
    int Count);                                   // Items.Count (shortcut for the UI)

csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/MapFeedQueryDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map;


/// <summary>
/// Query contract that the frontend sends when it wants a slice of the map feed.
/// The viewport is expressed as an axis‑aligned bounding box (min‑lat, max‑lat,
/// min‑lng, max‑lng).  An optional temporal window can be supplied to restrict
/// the result set (e.g. “Tonight”).  All values are in UTC.
///
/// This contract must stay versioned – any breaking change creates a new
/// contract file (v2, v3 …) and the old one stays available for backward‑compat.
/// </summary>
public sealed record MapFeedQueryDto(
    double MinLatitude,
    double MaxLatitude,
    double MinLongitude,
    double MaxLongitude,
    DateTimeOffset? TimeWindowStartUtc = null,
    DateTimeOffset? TimeWindowEndUtc   = null,
    int   MaxResults        = 500);   // Hard cap to protect the map endpoint


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/MapFeedQueryDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map;


/// <summary>
/// Query contract that the frontend sends when it wants a slice of the map feed.
/// The viewport is expressed as an axis‑aligned bounding box (min‑lat, max‑lat,
/// min‑lng, max‑lng).  An optional temporal window can be supplied to restrict
/// the result set (e.g. “Tonight”).  All values are in UTC.
///
/// This contract must stay versioned – any breaking change creates a new
/// contract file (v2, v3 …) and the old one stays available for backward‑compat.
/// </summary>
public sealed record MapFeedQueryDto(
    double MinLatitude,
    double MaxLatitude,
    double MinLongitude,
    double MaxLongitude,
    DateTimeOffset? TimeWindowStartUtc = null,
    DateTimeOffset? TimeWindowEndUtc   = null,
    int   MaxResults        = 500);   // Hard cap to protect the map endpoint


csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/IMapFeedService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Map
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Service that returns a feed of map items (markers or clusters) for a given
    /// viewport / temporal window.  The implementation must be **pure read‑only** –
    /// no mutating calls are allowed here.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IMapFeedService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Retrieve the feed for the supplied query.  The result may be a flat list
        /// of <see cref="EventMapItemDto"/> or a list of <see cref="ClusterMapItemDto"/>
        /// depending on the <paramref name="enableClustering"/> flag.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
            MapFeedQueryDto query,
            bool enableClustering = true);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/IMapFeedService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Map
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Service that returns a feed of map items (markers or clusters) for a given
    /// viewport / temporal window.  The implementation must be **pure read‑only** –
    /// no mutating calls are allowed here.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IMapFeedService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Retrieve the feed for the supplied query.  The result may be a flat list
        /// of <see cref="EventMapItemDto"/> or a list of <see cref="ClusterMapItemDto"/>
        /// depending on the <paramref name="enableClustering"/> flag.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
            MapFeedQueryDto query,
            bool enableClustering = true);
    }
}


Minimal Controller Stub (place under WeUP.Api/Endpoints/MapEndpoints.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/MapEndpoints.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Map;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/map")]
public sealed class MapEndpoints : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly IMapFeedService _feed;
    private readonly ILogger<MapEndpoints> _log;


    public MapEndpoints(IMapFeedService feed, ILogger<MapEndpoints> log)
    {
        _feed = feed;
        _log = log;
    }


    /// <summary>
    /// GET /api/v1/map/feed?minLat=…&maxLat=…&minLng=…&maxLng=…[&start=…&end=…][&cluster=true]
    /// Returns a collection of <see cref="ClusterMapItemDto"/> – each item either
    /// a single marker or a cluster depending on the query flag.
    /// </summary>
    [HttpGet("feed")]
    public async Task<ActionResult<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>>> GetFeed(
        [FromQuery] MapFeedQueryDto query,
        [FromQuery] bool cluster = true)
    {
        _log.LogInformation("Map feed requested: {@Query}, clustering={Cluster}", query, cluster);
        var result = await _feed.GetMapFeedAsync(query, enableClustering: cluster);
        return Ok(result);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/MapEndpoints.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;

namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/map")]
public sealed class MapEndpoints : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly IMapFeedService _feed;
    private readonly ILogger<MapEndpoints> _log;


    public MapEndpoints(IMapFeedService feed, ILogger<MapEndpoints> log)
    {
        _feed = feed;
        _log = log;
    }


    /// <summary>
    /// GET /api/v1/map/feed?minLat=…&maxLat=…&minLng=…&maxLng=…[&start=…&end=…][&cluster=true]
    /// Returns a collection of <see cref="ClusterMapItemDto"/> – each item either
    /// a single marker or a cluster depending on the query flag.
    /// </summary>
    [HttpGet("feed")]
    public async Task<ActionResult<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>>> GetFeed(
        [FromQuery] MapFeedQueryDto query,
        [FromQuery] bool cluster = true)
    {
        _log.LogInformation("Map feed requested: {@Query}, clustering={Cluster}", query, cluster);
        var result = await _feed.GetMapFeedAsync(query, enableClustering: cluster);
        return Ok(result);
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Why
	All DTOs must be immutable record types (no set‑ters).
	Guarantees serialisation safety and versioning.
	EventMapItemDto.IsSaved must be derived from the current user’s save‑state (the service will inject a ISaveRepository – no direct DB calls in the controller.
	Keeps the controller thin.
	MapFeedQueryDto latitude/longitude bounds must be validated (‑90 ≤ lat ≤ 90, ‑180 ≤ lng ≤ 180).
	Prevents out‑of‑range queries that could cause DB/Geo errors.
	The service must never modify any domain entity – it is read‑only.
	Enforces the “map is a view only” principle.
	When enableClustering is true the implementation must return only ClusterMapItemDto objects (even if Count = 1).
	UI can rely on a single shape.
	The endpoint must respond within 120 ms p95 (see M10‑P25).
	Supports the performance gate.
	Acceptance Criteria
* All four contract files compile under WeUP.Contracts/Map.
* IMapFeedService exists with the exact signature shown.
* MapEndpoints controller contains exactly the GetFeed method (return type, route, query‑parameter binding).
* The controller logs the incoming request (see ILogger).
* The DTOs contain only the fields described (no hidden navigation properties).
* Unit‑test placeholders are generated in WeUP.Tests/Map/MapFeedServiceTests.cs that assert:
   1. A query with a valid bbox returns a non‑null collection.
   2. When enableClustering = false each returned ClusterMapItemDto.Items collection contains exactly one EventMapItemDto.
   3. Latitude/longitude validation throws an ArgumentOutOfRangeException.
* The service is registered in DI (services.AddTransient<IMapFeedService, MapFeedService>();).

M5‑P22 — Build Marker Clustering and Density Control v1.0
markdown
# Context
When many events are displayed inside a small viewport the map can become unreadable.  
A deterministic, **server‑side clustering** algorithm is required for the MVP (client‑side clustering is optional but must be switchable).  


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a clustering options record (`ClusterOptions`).  
2️⃣ Extend the `IMapFeedService` contract with an overload that accepts explicit options.  
3️⃣ Provide a **pure clustering implementation** (`GridBasedClusterer`) that groups points into a fixed‑size grid (e.g., 100 px cells).  
4️⃣ Add an **enumeration** (`ClusterDensityLevel`) that the API can clamp to – low‑density maps (≤ 50 items) are returned as raw markers; high‑density maps (≥ 200 items) must be clustered.  
5️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** that verifies deterministic grouping and density thresholds.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Map`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/ClusterOptions.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map;


/// <summary>
/// Options that control the behaviour of the server‑side clustering algorithm.
/// All values are immutable – changing them requires a new options instance.
/// </summary>
public sealed record ClusterOptions(
    int   GridPixelSize = 100,          // Width/height of a grid cell in screen pixels
    int   MaxClusterSize = 500,         // Upper bound of items before we force‑cluster
    bool  ForceClustering = false);     // If true, clustering is applied even below the threshold


# Context
When many events are displayed inside a small viewport the map can become unreadable.  
A deterministic, **server‑side clustering** algorithm is required for the MVP (client‑side clustering is optional but must be switchable).  


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a clustering options record (`ClusterOptions`).  
2️⃣ Extend the `IMapFeedService` contract with an overload that accepts explicit options.  
3️⃣ Provide a **pure clustering implementation** (`GridBasedClusterer`) that groups points into a fixed‑size grid (e.g., 100 px cells).  
4️⃣ Add an **enumeration** (`ClusterDensityLevel`) that the API can clamp to – low‑density maps (≤ 50 items) are returned as raw markers; high‑density maps (≥ 200 items) must be clustered.

    Moderate,    // 51‑200 items – optional clustering (client may decide)

Clustering Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Map/GridBasedClusterer.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/GridBasedClusterer.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map;


/// <summary>
/// Deterministic, grid‑based clustering algorithm.  It does **not** depend on any
/// external service – all calculations are pure C# and therefore unit‑testable.
///
/// The algorithm works in screen‑pixel space, but for the MVP we simulate the
/// conversion by assuming a constant scale factor (e.g. 0.0001° per pixel).  The
/// factor can be injected via options later.
/// </summary>
public sealed class GridBasedClusterer : IMapFeedService
{
    private const double DegreesPerPixel = 0.0001; // Simplified constant for MVP


    public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query,
        bool enableClustering = true) =>
        GetMapFeedAsync(query, new ClusterOptions(
            GridPixelSize: 100,
            MaxClusterSize: 500,
            ForceClustering: enableClustering));


    public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query,
        ClusterOptions options)
    {
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 1️⃣ Retrieve raw markers from the repository (pure read‑only).  
        //    In the MVP we assume an injected IEventReadOnlyRepository that
        //    returns EventMapItemDto for the supplied viewport + time window.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var raw = await RetrieveRawMarkersAsync(query);


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 2️⃣ Decide whether clustering is required based on density.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var density = raw.Count switch
        {
            <= 50 => ClusterDensityLevel.Sparse,
            <= 200 => ClusterDensityLevel.Moderate,
            _ => ClusterDensityLevel.Dense
        };


        var mustCluster = options.ForceClustering ||
                         density == ClusterDensityLevel.Dense ||
                         (density == ClusterDensityLevel.Moderate && options.MaxClusterSize > 0);


        if (!mustCluster)
        {
            // Return each marker wrapped as a single‑item cluster.
            var singleClusters = raw.Select(m => new ClusterMapItemDto(
                Items: new[] { m },
                CenterLat: m.Latitude,
                CenterLng: m.Longitude,
                Count: 1)).ToArray();


            return singleClusters;
        }


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 3️⃣ Perform the grid‑based clustering.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var cellSizeDeg = options.GridPixelSize * DegreesPerPixel;
        var groups = raw.GroupBy(m => new
        {
            Row = (int)Math.Floor((m.Latitude  - query.MinLatitude)  / cellSizeDeg),
            Col = (int)Math.Floor((m.Longitude - query.MinLongitude) / cellSizeDeg)
        });


        var clusters = groups.Select(g =>
        {
            var items = g.ToArray();
            var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
            var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
            return new ClusterMapItemDto(
                Items: items,
                CenterLat: centerLat,
                CenterLng: centerLng,
                Count: items.Length);
        }).ToArray();


        return clusters;
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Placeholder: repository call – replace with real EF query later.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto>> RetrieveRawMarkersAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query)
    {
        // For the prompt we only need the method signature.
        // The actual implementation will be supplied later
        // (e.g. via IEventReadOnlyRepository).
        throw new NotImplementedException("Inject a repository that returns raw EventMapItemDto.");
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/GridBasedClusterer.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map;


/// <summary>
/// Deterministic, grid‑based clustering algorithm.  It does **not** depend on any
/// external service – all calculations are pure C# and therefore unit‑testable.
///
/// The algorithm works in screen‑pixel space, but for the MVP we simulate the
/// conversion by assuming a constant scale factor (e.g. 0.0001° per pixel).  The
/// factor can be injected via options later.
/// </summary>
public sealed class GridBasedClusterer : IMapFeedService
{
    private const double DegreesPerPixel = 0.0001; // Simplified constant for MVP


    public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query,
        bool enableClustering = true) =>
        GetMapFeedAsync(query, new ClusterOptions(
            GridPixelSize: 100,
            MaxClusterSize: 500,
            ForceClustering: enableClustering));


    public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query,
        ClusterOptions options)
    {
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 1️⃣ Retrieve raw markers from the repository (pure read‑only).  
        //    In the MVP we assume an injected IEventReadOnlyRepository that
        //    returns EventMapItemDto for the supplied viewport + time window.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var raw = await RetrieveRawMarkersAsync(query);


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 2️⃣ Decide whether clustering is required based on density.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var density = raw.Count switch
        {
            <= 50 => ClusterDensityLevel.Sparse,
            <= 200 => ClusterDensityLevel.Moderate,
            _ => ClusterDensityLevel.Dense
        };


        var mustCluster = options.ForceClustering ||
                         density == ClusterDensityLevel.Dense ||
                         (density == ClusterDensityLevel.Moderate && options.MaxClusterSize > 0);


        if (!mustCluster)
        {
            // Return each marker wrapped as a single‑item cluster.
            var singleClusters = raw.Select(m => new ClusterMapItemDto(
                Items: new[] { m },
                CenterLat: m.Latitude,
                CenterLng: m.Longitude,
                Count: 1)).ToArray();


            return singleClusters;
        }


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // 3️⃣ Perform the grid‑based clustering.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        var cellSizeDeg = options.GridPixelSize * DegreesPerPixel;
        var groups = raw.GroupBy(m => new
        {
            Row = (int)Math.Floor((m.Latitude  - query.MinLatitude)  / cellSizeDeg),
            Col = (int)Math.Floor((m.Longitude - query.MinLongitude) / cellSizeDeg)
        });


        var clusters = groups.Select(g =>
        {
            var items = g.ToArray();
            var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
            var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
            return new ClusterMapItemDto(
                Items: items,
                CenterLat: centerLat,
                CenterLng: centerLng,
                Count: items.Length);
        }).ToArray();


        return clusters;
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Placeholder: repository call – replace with real EF query later.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto>> RetrieveRawMarkersAsync(
        MapFeedQueryDto query)
    {
        // For the prompt we only need the method signature.
        // The actual implementation will be supplied later
        // (e.g. via IEventReadOnlyRepository).
        throw new NotImplementedException("Inject a repository that returns raw EventMapItemDto.");
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Rationale
	Clustering must be deterministic – given the same input list and options the same set of ClusterMapItemDtos must be produced (grid‑based algorithm guarantees this).
	

	The ClusterOptions.GridPixelSize is expressed in screen pixels but the implementation converts it to a fixed degree delta (DegreesPerPixel). No external map‑SDK state may be consulted.
	

	When ForceClustering is true the service must ignore the density heuristic and always return grouped clusters.
	

	The ClusterMapItemDto must never contain duplicate EventMapItemDtos – each event appears in exactly one cluster.
	

	The service must not modify or delete any EventAggregate – it is purely a read‑only projection.
	

	The API endpoint should expose a query‑parameter `cluster=true
	falsethat maps toenableClustering` (handled by controller stub).
	Acceptance Criteria
* ClusterOptions record and ClusterDensityLevel enum compile.
* IMapFeedService now contains the overload with ClusterOptions.
* GridBasedClusterer implements IMapFeedService explicitly (both overloads) and contains the deterministic grouping logic shown.
* The clustering code produces a single ClusterMapItemDto per grid cell; each contains the correct CenterLat, CenterLng, and Count.
* Unit‑test skeleton WeUP.Tests/Map/GridBasedClustererTests.cs verifies:
   1. 2 points in the same cell → one cluster with Count = 2.
   2. 2 points far apart → two clusters (Count = 1 each).
   3. Density threshold works (Sparse returns raw markers wrapped as single‑item clusters).
* The controller (MapEndpoints) now calls GetMapFeedAsync(query, enableClustering) – the flag maps to the ClusterOptions.ForceClustering overload internally.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IMapFeedService, GridBasedClusterer>();.
________________


M5‑P23 — Implement Event Detail Modal and Interaction Flow v1.0
markdown
# Context
When a user clicks a marker (or a cluster that expands to a single marker), a **modal dialog** must appear showing the full event details, provenance summary, and save/unsave controls.  
The frontend expects a **dedicated API endpoint** that returns a rich DTO (including flyer image URL, confidence, risk level, and provenance links).  


# Objective
1️⃣ Define the **detail DTO** (`EventDetailDto`).  
2️⃣ Add a **controller action** (`GET /api/v1/events/{eventId}`) that returns the DTO.  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`IEventDetailService`) that assembles the DTO from the canonical aggregate, the confidence model, and the provenance service.  
4️⃣ Provide a **stub implementation** that wires the required repositories (read‑only aggregate, confidence, provenance).  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test placeholder** that checks the DTO is correctly populated for a known event.


# Contracts (under `WeUP.Contracts/Events/Dto`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Events/Dto/EventDetailDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using WeUP.Contracts.Ingestion;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Events.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Full representation of an event for the modal detail view.  
/// Only fields required by the UI are exposed – internal IDs, version numbers,
/// and raw ingestion hashes are omitted to keep the contract stable.
/// </summary>
public sealed record EventDetailDto(
    Guid EventId,
    string Title,
    DateTimeOffset StartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset? EndUtc,
    string VenueName,
    double Latitude,
    double Longitude,
    string? FlyerImageUrl,                    // Public URL to the flyer thumbnail
    float ConfidenceScore,                    // Overall confidence (0‑1)
    EventRiskLevel RiskLevel,                 // Enum from moderation model
    IReadOnlyList<ProvenanceEntry> Provenance, // Immutable lineage (see M2‑P10)
    bool IsSaved);                            // Current user’s save flag

Stub Implementation (under WeUP.Application/Events/EventDetailService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Events/EventDetailService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events.Dto;
using WeUP.Contracts.Ingestion;
using WeUP.Contracts.Moderation;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Resolution;      // IProvenanceService
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;           // ISaveRepository (optional)
using WeUP.Domain.Events;          // IEventReadOnlyRepository (read canonical aggregate)


namespace WeUP.Application.Events
{
    public sealed class EventDetailService : IEventDetailService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<EventDetailService> _log;
        private readonly IEventReadOnlyRepository _eventRepo;
        private readonly IProvenanceService _provService;
        private readonly ISaveRepository _saveRepo;       // May be null for anonymous users


        public EventDetailService(
            ILogger<EventDetailService> log,
            IEventReadOnlyRepository eventRepo,
            IProvenanceService provService,
            ISaveRepository? saveRepo = null)
        {
            _log = log;
            _eventRepo = eventRepo;
            _provService = provService;
            _saveRepo = saveRepo ?? new NullSaveRepository();
        }


        public async Task<EventDetailDto> GetDetailAsync(Guid eventId, Guid? currentUserId = null)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Load the canonical aggregate (read‑only).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var agg = await _eventRepo.GetByIdAsync(eventId)
                ?? throw new InvalidOperationException($"Event {eventId} not found.");


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Load provenance lineage (append‑only history).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var provenance = await _provService.GetLineageAsync(eventId);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Resolve saved state for the current user (if any).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var isSaved = currentUserId.HasValue &&
                          await _saveRepo.IsSavedAsync(currentUserId.Value, eventId);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 4️⃣ Assemble the DTO.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var dto = new EventDetailDto(
                EventId: agg.EventId,
                Title: agg.Title,
                StartUtc: agg.StartUtc,
                EndUtc: agg.EndUtc,
                VenueName: agg.VenueName,
                Latitude: agg.Location.Latitude,
                Longitude: agg.Location.Longitude,
                FlyerImageUrl: agg.FlyerImageUrl,               // Assuming a property on the aggregate
                ConfidenceScore: agg.ConfidenceScore,
                RiskLevel: agg.RiskLevel,
                Provenance: provenance,
                IsSaved: isSaved);


            _log.LogInformation("EventDetailDto generated for Event {EventId} (User={UserId})",
                                 eventId, currentUserId?.ToString() ?? "anonymous");


            return dto;
        }


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // Null object for anonymous save‑repository (no‑op implementation)
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        private sealed class NullSaveRepository : ISaveRepository
        {
            public Task<bool> IsSavedAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId) => Task.FromResult(false);
            public Task SaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId) => Task.CompletedTask;
            public Task UnsaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId) => Task.CompletedTask;
        }
    }
}

Controller Stub (under WeUP.Api/Endpoints/EventDetailEndpoint.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/EventDetailEndpoint.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/events")]
public sealed class EventDetailEndpoint : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly IEventDetailService _detail;
    private readonly ILogger<EventDetailEndpoint> _log;


    public EventDetailEndpoint(IEventDetailService detail, ILogger<EventDetailEndpoint> log)
    {
        _detail = detail;
        _log = log;
    }


    /// <summary>
    /// GET /api/v1/events/{eventId}
    /// Returns the full detail payload used by the UI modal.
    /// </summary>
    [HttpGet("{eventId:guid}")]
    public async Task<ActionResult<EventDetailDto>> Get(Guid eventId)
    {
        _log.LogInformation("Detail request for Event {EventId}", eventId);
        var dto = await _detail.GetDetailAsync(eventId, GetCurrentUserIdOrNull());
        return Ok(dto);
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Simple helper – extracts the current user ID from the auth context.
    // Returns null for anonymous callers.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private Guid? GetCurrentUserIdOrNull()
    {
        // In the MVP we rely on a claim named "sub".
        var sub = HttpContext.User.FindFirst("sub")?.Value;
        return Guid.TryParse(sub, out var guid) ? guid : (Guid?)null;
    }
}
