Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All rows in MergeHistoryEntity are append‑only – the EF configuration throws on any UPDATE/DELETE.
	Guarantees immutable audit trail.
	MergeHistoryEntry.Id must be a brand‑new GUID for each record; the service silently ignores duplicates.
	Prevents replay attacks / accidental double writes.
	The service must order results by ChangedAtUtc ascending (chronological order).
	Required for UI timeline view.
	The merge planner (M2‑P09) must call IMergeHistoryService.RecordAsync for every field action it decides on. (Out‑of‑scope for this bundle but note the contract.)
	

	No external I/O besides the DB – the service is pure persistence.
	Keeps it testable.
	Acceptance Criteria
* MergeHistoryEntry record and IMergeHistoryService compile.
* MergeHistoryEntity and its EF configuration enforce append‑only semantics.
* MergeHistoryService.RecordAsync inserts a new row, ignores duplicate IDs, and logs the operation.
* MergeHistoryService.GetLineageAsync returns an ordered list of entries for a given event.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Resolution/MergeHistoryServiceTests.cs verifies:
   1. A first record is stored.
   2. A second record with the same Id is ignored.
   3. Retrieval returns entries ordered by ChangedAtUtc.
* IEventDetailService now includes GetMergeHistoryAsync and the concrete EventDetailService delegates to IMergeHistoryService.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IMergeHistoryService, MergeHistoryService>();.

M6‑P26 — Implement Calendar Overlay Layer Architecture v1.0
markdown
# Context
The discovery surface has two synchronized views: a **Map** (M5) and a **Calendar overlay** that shows events in a time‑grid (day/week view).  The overlay must be driven by a strongly‑typed backend contract and must expose a lightweight service that the front‑end can query for a given temporal window and optional market/venue filter.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **DTO** that represents a single calendar cell (`CalendarEventItemDto`).  
2️⃣ Define a **query contract** (`CalendarQueryDto`) that callers use to request a slice of the calendar.  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ICalendarOverlayService`) that returns a list of calendar items for the query.  
4️⃣ Provide a **pure reference implementation** that fetches `EventAggregate`s, maps them to the DTO and optionally groups them by day.  
5️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test placeholder**.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Calendar`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/Dto/CalendarEventItemDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Minimal data required to render a single event in the calendar overlay.
/// </summary>
public sealed record CalendarEventItemDto(
    Guid EventId,
    string Title,
    DateTimeOffset StartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset? EndUtc,
    string VenueName,
    double Latitude,
    double Longitude,
    EventRiskLevel RiskLevel,
    bool IsSaved);                         // Current user‑save flag

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/CalendarQueryDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;


/// <summary>
/// Query used by the front‑end to request a calendar slice.
/// The start/end represent the **UTC** window the UI wishes to display
/// (e.g. the current day, a 3‑day window, or a whole week).
/// </summary>
public sealed record CalendarQueryDto(
    DateTimeOffset WindowStartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset WindowEndUtc,
    string? VenueFilter = null,          // Optional – only events at this venue
    string? MarketFilter = null);       // Optional – restrict to a market (Houston, etc.)


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/CalendarQueryDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------

namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;


/// <summary>
/// Query used by the front‑end to request a calendar slice.

/// (e.g. the current day, a 3‑day window, or a whole week).
/// </summary>

    DateTimeOffset WindowStartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset WindowEndUtc,
    string? VenueFilter = null,          // Optional – only events at this venue
    string? MarketFilter = null);       // Optional – restrict to a market (Houston, etc.)


Service Contract (place under WeUP.Domain/Calendar)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Calendar/ICalendarOverlayService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure read‑only service that returns calendar items for a given query.
    /// No mutation, no side‑effects – suitable for unit testing and deterministic
    /// behaviour.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ICalendarOverlayService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Retrieve all events that intersect the supplied UTC window and satisfy
        /// the optional venue/market filters.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto>> GetCalendarSliceAsync(CalendarQueryDto query);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Calendar/ICalendarOverlayService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure read‑only service that returns calendar items for a given query.
    /// No mutation, no side‑effects – suitable for unit testing and deterministic
    /// behaviour.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ICalendarOverlayService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Retrieve all events that intersect the supplied UTC window and satisfy
        /// the optional venue/market filters.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto>> GetCalendarSliceAsync(CalendarQueryDto query);
    }
}


Reference Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Calendar/CalendarOverlayService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Calendar/CalendarOverlayService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;               // IEventReadOnlyRepository (pure read‑only)


namespace WeUP.Application.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Deterministic implementation that pulls the canonical aggregates,
    /// applies the temporal window, optional filters and maps them to DTOs.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class CalendarOverlayService : ICalendarOverlayService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<CalendarOverlayService> _log;
        private readonly IEventReadOnlyRepository _eventRepo;   // read‑only repository


        public CalendarOverlayService(
            ILogger<CalendarOverlayService> log,
            IEventReadOnlyRepository eventRepo)
        {
            _log = log;
            _eventRepo = eventRepo;
        }


        public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto>> GetCalendarSliceAsync(CalendarQueryDto query)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Load all events that intersect the window (simple DB query could be
            //    optimised later – for the MVP we pull all and filter in‑memory).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var all = await _eventRepo.GetAllAsync(); // returns IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>


            var filtered = all.Where(e =>
                // Temporal overlap (any part of the event falls inside the window)
                e.StartUtc < query.WindowEndUtc &&
                (e.EndUtc?. > query.WindowStartUtc ?? true) &&
                // Optional venue filter
                (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(query.VenueFilter) ||
                 e.VenueName.Equals(query.VenueFilter, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)) &&
                // Optional market filter – assume EventAggregate has a MarketId (omitted here);
                // if the field exists you would add a similar check.
                true);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Map to DTO – the mapper does **no** I/O; the saved flag would be
            //    supplied by a higher‑level service (e.g. a SaveStateProvider) –
            //    for the MVP we default to false.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var result = filtered.Select(e => new CalendarEventItemDto(
                EventId:    e.EventId,
                Title:       e.Title,
                StartUtc:    e.StartUtc,
                EndUtc:      e.EndUtc,
                VenueName:   e.VenueName,
                Latitude:    e.Location.Latitude,
                Longitude:   e.Location.Longitude,
                RiskLevel:   e.RiskLevel,
                IsSaved:    false) // UI will later enrich this per‑user.
                .ToArray();


            _log.LogInformation("Calendar slice requested: {Start} – {End}, {Count} items returned",
                                 query.WindowStartUtc, query.WindowEndUtc, result.Length);


            return result;
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Calendar/CalendarOverlayService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;               // IEventReadOnlyRepository (pure read‑only)


namespace WeUP.Application.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Deterministic implementation that pulls the canonical aggregates,
    /// applies the temporal window, optional filters and maps them to DTOs.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class CalendarOverlayService : ICalendarOverlayService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<CalendarOverlayService> _log;
        private readonly IEventReadOnlyRepository _eventRepo;   // read‑only repository


        public CalendarOverlayService(
            ILogger<CalendarOverlayService> log,
            IEventReadOnlyRepository eventRepo)
        {
            _log = log;
            _eventRepo = eventRepo;
        }


        public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto>> GetCalendarSliceAsync(CalendarQueryDto query)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Load all events that intersect the window (simple DB query could be
            //    optimised later – for the MVP we pull all and filter in‑memory).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var all = await _eventRepo.GetAllAsync(); // returns IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>


            var filtered = all.Where(e =>
                // Temporal overlap (any part of the event falls inside the window)
                e.StartUtc < query.WindowEndUtc &&
                (e.EndUtc?. > query.WindowStartUtc ?? true) &&
                // Optional venue filter
                (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(query.VenueFilter) ||
                 e.VenueName.Equals(query.VenueFilter, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)) &&
                // Optional market filter – assume EventAggregate has a MarketId (omitted here);
                // if the field exists you would add a similar check.
                true);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Map to DTO – the mapper does **no** I/O; the saved flag would be
            //    supplied by a higher‑level service (e.g. a SaveStateProvider) –
            //    for the MVP we default to false.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var result = filtered.Select(e => new CalendarEventItemDto(
                EventId:    e.EventId,
                Title:       e.Title,
                StartUtc:    e.StartUtc,
                EndUtc:      e.EndUtc,
                VenueName:   e.VenueName,
                Latitude:    e.Location.Latitude,
                Longitude:   e.Location.Longitude,
                RiskLevel:   e.RiskLevel,
                IsSaved:    false) // UI will later enrich this per‑user.
                .ToArray();


            _log.LogInformation("Calendar slice requested: {Start} – {End}, {Count} items returned",
                                 query.WindowStartUtc, query.WindowEndUtc, result.Length);


            return result;
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Why
	The service must not write to the database – it is a pure read‑only query.
	Guarantees deterministic behaviour and makes unit‑testing trivial.
	All date‑time arguments are UTC; the service never performs timezone conversion.
	Centralises temporal handling (M8).
	The mapper does not resolve IsSaved; the front‑end will call the Save‑state provider after receiving the slice.
	Keeps the overlay service focused on calendar data only.
	If the query window is invalid (Start ≥ End) the implementation must throw ArgumentException.
	Defensive programming – helps the UI catch bugs early.
	The result set is ordered by StartUtc ascending (optional but useful for UI).
	Guarantees a stable order for the front‑end.
	Acceptance Criteria
* CalendarEventItemDto, CalendarQueryDto and ICalendarOverlayService compile.
* CalendarOverlayService implements the contract, validates the query, filters by temporal overlap and optional venue filter, maps to DTOs and returns them ordered by StartUtc.
* Unit‑test placeholder (WeUP.Tests/Calendar/CalendarOverlayServiceTests.cs) checks:
   1. Correct temporal filtering (event partially overlapping the window is returned).
   2. Venue filter works (events with a non‑matching venue are excluded).
   3. Invalid window throws ArgumentException.
* Service is registered in DI: services.AddTransient<ICalendarOverlayService, CalendarOverlayService>();.

M6‑P27 — Build Masonry Event Layout and Time‑Slot Packing v1.0
markdown
# Context
The calendar overlay shows events in a **vertical time‑grid** (like a day‑view agenda).  Events may overlap in time, so they need to be packed into columns (masonry layout) that minimise horizontal expansion while preserving chronological order.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **layout model** (`MasonryLayoutSlot`) that records the column index, start/end, and the event’s DTO.  
2️⃣ Declare a **layout service** (`IEventMasonryLayoutService`) that, given a list of `CalendarEventItemDto`, returns a collection of slots ready for rendering.  
3️⃣ Provide a **deterministic, O(N log N)** implementation that:  
   - Sorts events by start time.  
   - Packs overlapping events into the smallest number of columns (greedy‑first‑fit).  
   - Calculates the column index (0‑based) for each event.  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that verifies packing for overlapping, nested, and disjoint events.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/Layout`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/Layout/MasonryLayoutSlot.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Layout;


/// <summary>
/// Represents a single rendered slot in the masonry layout.
/// </summary>
public sealed record MasonryLayoutSlot(
    CalendarEventItemDto Event,
    int ColumnIndex,               // 0‑based column in the UI
    int TotalColumns);            // Number of columns needed for the whole slice


# Context
The calendar overlay shows events in a **vertical time‑grid** (like a day‑view agenda).  Events may overlap in time, so they need to be packed into columns (masonry layout) that minimise horizontal expansion while preserving chronological order.

   - Packs overlapping events into the smallest number of columns (greedy‑first‑fit).

4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that verifies packing for overlapping, nested, and disjoint events.

using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Layout;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that takes a flat list of calendar events and returns a
    /// deterministic masonry layout (column index for each event).
    /// No side‑effects – suitable for unit testing.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IEventMasonryLayoutService
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>> ComputeLayoutAsync(
            IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto> events);
    }

using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Layout;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that takes a flat list of calendar events and returns a
    /// deterministic masonry layout (column index for each event).
    /// No side‑effects – suitable for unit testing.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IEventMasonryLayoutService
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>> ComputeLayoutAsync(
            IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto> events);
    }
}


Reference Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Calendar/EventMasonryLayoutService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Calendar/EventMasonryLayoutService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Layout;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;


namespace WeUP.Application.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Greedy‑first‑fit algorithm that packs events into the minimum
    /// number of columns while preserving chronological order.
    /// Deterministic because events are processed in a strict start‑time order.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class EventMasonryLayoutService : IEventMasonryLayoutService
    {
        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>> ComputeLayoutAsync(
            IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto> events)
        {
            // Sort by start time (ascending).  If start times are equal, sort by end time.
            var sorted = events.OrderBy(e => e.StartUtc)
                               .ThenBy(e => e.EndUtc ?? DateTimeOffset.MaxValue)
                               .ToArray();


            // Each column stores the *end* of the last event placed in it.
            var columnEnds = new List<DateTimeOffset>();


            var slots = new List<MasonryLayoutSlot>();


            foreach (var ev in sorted)
            {
                // Find the first column where this event does NOT overlap.
                int columnIdx = -1;
                for (int i = 0; i < columnEnds.Count; i++)
                {
                    if (ev.StartUtc >= columnEnds[i])
                    {
                        columnIdx = i;
                        break;
                    }
                }


                // If none found, create a new column.
                if (columnIdx == -1)
                {
                    columnIdx = columnEnds.Count;
                    columnEnds.Add(DateTimeOffset.MinValue);
                }


                // Update the column's occupancy.
                columnEnds[columnIdx] = ev.EndUtc ?? ev.StartUtc.AddHours(1); // assume 1 h if missing


                var slot = new MasonryLayoutSlot(
                    Event: ev,
                    ColumnIndex: columnIdx,
                    TotalColumns: columnEnds.Count);


                slots.Add(slot);
            }


            // The total columns is the final size of columnEnds.
            var finalSlots = slots.Select(s => s with { TotalColumns = columnEnds.Count })
                                  .ToArray();


            return Task.FromResult<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>>(finalSlots);
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Calendar/EventMasonryLayoutService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Dto;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.Layout;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;


namespace WeUP.Application.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Greedy‑first‑fit algorithm that packs events into the minimum
    /// number of columns while preserving chronological order.
    /// Deterministic because events are processed in a strict start‑time order.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class EventMasonryLayoutService : IEventMasonryLayoutService
    {
        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>> ComputeLayoutAsync(
            IReadOnlyCollection<CalendarEventItemDto> events)
        {
            // Sort by start time (ascending).  If start times are equal, sort by end time.
            var sorted = events.OrderBy(e => e.StartUtc)
                               .ThenBy(e => e.EndUtc ?? DateTimeOffset.MaxValue)
                               .ToArray();


            // Each column stores the *end* of the last event placed in it.
            var columnEnds = new List<DateTimeOffset>();


            var slots = new List<MasonryLayoutSlot>();


            foreach (var ev in sorted)
            {
                // Find the first column where this event does NOT overlap.
                int columnIdx = -1;
                for (int i = 0; i < columnEnds.Count; i++)
                {
                    if (ev.StartUtc >= columnEnds[i])
                    {
                        columnIdx = i;
                        break;
                    }
                }


                // If none found, create a new column.
                if (columnIdx == -1)
                {
                    columnIdx = columnEnds.Count;
                    columnEnds.Add(DateTimeOffset.MinValue);
                }


                // Update the column's occupancy.
                columnEnds[columnIdx] = ev.EndUtc ?? ev.StartUtc.AddHours(1); // assume 1 h if missing


                var slot = new MasonryLayoutSlot(
                    Event: ev,
                    ColumnIndex: columnIdx,
                    TotalColumns: columnEnds.Count);


                slots.Add(slot);
            }


            // The total columns is the final size of columnEnds.
            var finalSlots = slots.Select(s => s with { TotalColumns = columnEnds.Count })
                                  .ToArray();


            return Task.FromResult<IReadOnlyCollection<MasonryLayoutSlot>>(finalSlots);
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The algorithm must be deterministic – given the same input list it always returns the same column indices.
	Guarantees repeatable UI rendering and reliable unit tests.
	No mutation of the original CalendarEventItemDto objects (the service creates new MasonryLayoutSlots).
	Keeps the service pure.
	Over‑lap definition: an event overlaps another if its StartUtc is < the other’s EndUtc (or if the other has no EndUtc, treat it as an hour‑long placeholder).
	Provides a simple, deterministic rule.
	The returned TotalColumns on each slot must be the same for all slots (the final column count).
	UI can compute column width from a single value.
	If an event has a null EndUtc, assume a 1‑hour duration for layout purposes.
	Avoids indefinite columns for open‑ended events.
	Acceptance Criteria
* MasonryLayoutSlot record, IEventMasonryLayoutService interface and EventMasonryLayoutService implementation compile.
* The implementation sorts events, packs them into columns using the greedy first‑fit algorithm, and returns slots with correct ColumnIndex and final TotalColumns.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Calendar/EventMasonryLayoutServiceTests.cs covers:
   1. Two non‑overlapping events → both in column 0, TotalColumns = 1.
   2. Two overlapping events → columns 0 and 1, TotalColumns = 2.
   3. Three events where the middle fully contains the others → columns 0, 1, 0 (greedy result).
   4. Events with null EndUtc are treated as 1 hour and packed correctly.
* Service registration: services.AddTransient<IEventMasonryLayoutService, EventMasonryLayoutService>();.
________________


M6‑P28 — Sync Calendar Overlay with Map State v1.0
markdown
# Context
Users can click a marker on the map and the calendar overlay must automatically scroll to (and optionally highlight) the corresponding time‑slot.  Conversely, selecting an event in the calendar should pan/zoom the map to the associated marker.  Both views share the same **canonical EventId**.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **shared state container** (`DiscoveryState`) that lives in a Pinia store (frontend) and a corresponding **C# model** (`DiscoveryStateDto`) for API payloads.  
2️⃣ Declare a **synchronisation service** (`IDiscoverySyncService`) that:  
   - Accepts a map‑selection (`Guid eventId`) and returns the matching `CalendarQueryDto` window (e.g., the day that contains the event).  
   - Accepts a calendar‑selection (`Guid eventId`) and returns the map viewport (bbox) that contains the event’s location.  
3️⃣ Provide **pure implementations** that query the read‑only event repository for the needed data.  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that verifies both directions (map→calendar, calendar→map).


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Discovery`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Discovery/DiscoveryStateDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Discovery;


/// <summary>
/// Minimal shared state that both the map and calendar can read/write.
/// All timestamps are UTC; the front‑end stores them in a Pinia store.
/// </summary>
public sealed record DiscoveryStateDto(
    Guid? SelectedEventId,          // null → nothing selected
    DateTimeOffset? CalendarWindowStartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset? CalendarWindowEndUtc,
    double? MapViewportMinLat,
    double? MapViewportMaxLat,
    double? MapViewportMinLng,
    double? MapViewportMaxLng);


# Context
Users can click a marker on the map and the calendar overlay must automatically scroll to (and optionally highlight) the corresponding time‑slot.  Conversely, selecting an event in the calendar should pan/zoom the map to the associated marker.  Both views share the same **canonical EventId**.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **shared state container** (`DiscoveryState`) that lives in a Pinia store (frontend) and a corresponding **C# model** (`DiscoveryStateDto`) for API payloads.  
2️⃣ Declare a **synchronisation service** (`IDiscoverySyncService`) that:  
   - Accepts a map‑selection (`Guid eventId`) and returns the matching `CalendarQueryDto` window (e.g., the day that contains the event).  
   - Accepts a calendar‑selection (`Guid eventId`) and returns the map viewport (bbox) that contains the event’s location.  
3️⃣ Provide **pure implementations** that query the read‑only event repository for the needed data.  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that verifies both directions (map→calendar, calendar→map).


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Discovery`)


```csharp

    double? MapViewportMinLat,
    double? MapViewportMaxLat,
    double? MapViewportMinLng,
    double? MapViewportMaxLng);


Service Contract (place under WeUP.Domain/Discovery)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Discovery/IDiscoverySyncService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Discovery;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Discovery
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that maps a selected EventId to the appropriate
    /// calendar window or map viewport.  No mutation – just reads.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IDiscoverySyncService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Given an <paramref name="eventId"/> selected on the map,
        /// return a <see cref="CalendarQueryDto"/> that covers the day (or week)
        /// containing the event.
        /// </summary>
        Task<CalendarQueryDto> ResolveCalendarWindowAsync(Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Given an <paramref name="eventId"/> selected in the calendar,
        /// return a minimal map viewport (bbox) that encloses the event’s location
        /// with a small padding (≈ 0.001°).  All coordinates are UTC‑agnostic.
        /// </summary>
        Task<MapFeedQueryDto> ResolveMapViewportAsync(Guid eventId);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Discovery/IDiscoverySyncService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------

using System.Threading.Tasks;

    /// <summary>

    /// calendar window or map viewport.  No mutation – just reads.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IDiscoverySyncService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Given an <paramref name="eventId"/> selected on the map,
        /// return a <see cref="CalendarQueryDto"/> that covers the day (or week)
        /// containing the event.
        /// </summary>
        Task<CalendarQueryDto> ResolveCalendarWindowAsync(Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Given an <paramref name="eventId"/> selected in the calendar,
        /// return a minimal map viewport (bbox) that encloses the event’s location
        /// with a small padding (≈ 0.001°).  All coordinates are UTC‑agnostic.
        /// </summary>
        Task<MapFeedQueryDto> ResolveMapViewportAsync(Guid eventId);
    }
}


Reference Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Discovery/DiscoverySyncService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Discovery/DiscoverySyncService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Discovery;
using WeUP.Domain.Discovery;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;                     // IEventReadOnlyRepository


namespace WeUP.Application.Discovery
{
    public sealed class DiscoverySyncService : IDiscoverySyncService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<DiscoverySyncService> _log;
        private readonly IEventReadOnlyRepository _eventRepo;


        public DiscoverySyncService(
            ILogger<DiscoverySyncService> log,
            IEventReadOnlyRepository eventRepo)
        {
            _log = log;
            _eventRepo = eventRepo;
        }


        public async Task<CalendarQueryDto> ResolveCalendarWindowAsync(Guid eventId)
        {
            var agg = await _eventRepo.GetByIdAsync(eventId)
                ?? throw new InvalidOperationException($"Event {eventId} not found.");


            // For the MVP we return a single‑day window (midnight‑to‑midnight of the event's day).
            var day = agg.StartUtc.Date;
            var start = new DateTimeOffset(day, TimeSpan.Zero);
            var end   = start.AddDays(1).AddTicks(-1); // 23:59:59.999...


            var query = new CalendarQueryDto(
                WindowStartUtc: start,
                WindowEndUtc:   end,
                VenueFilter:    null,
                MarketFilter:   null);


            _log.LogInformation("Resolved calendar window for Event {EventId}: {Start}‑{End}",
                                 eventId, start, end);
            return query;
        }


        public async Task<MapFeedQueryDto> ResolveMapViewportAsync(Guid eventId)
        {
            var agg = await _eventRepo.GetByIdAsync(eventId)
                ?? throw new InvalidOperationException($"Event {eventId} not found.");


            // Pad the point by ±0.001° (≈ 111 m) to give the map a small viewport.
            const double pad = 0.001;
            var minLat = agg.Location.Latitude  - pad;
            var maxLat = agg.Location.Latitude  + pad;
            var minLng = agg.Location.Longitude - pad;
            var maxLng = agg.Location.Longitude + pad;


            var viewport = new MapFeedQueryDto(
                MinLatitude:  minLat,
                MaxLatitude:  maxLat,
                MinLongitude: minLng,
                MaxLongitude: maxLng,
                TimeWindowStartUtc: null,
                TimeWindowEndUtc:   null,
                MaxResults: 1);   // only the selected point


            _log.LogInformation("Resolved map viewport for Event {EventId}: [{MinLat},{MinLng}]‑[{MaxLat},{MaxLng}]",
                                 eventId, minLat, minLng, maxLat, maxLng);
            return viewport;
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------

                ?? throw new InvalidOperationException($"Event {eventId} not found.");

            const double pad = 0.001;

            var minLng = agg.Location.Longitude - pad;
            var maxLng = agg.Location.Longitude + pad;

                MinLatitude:  minLat,
                MaxLatitude:  maxLat,
                MinLongitude: minLng,
                MaxLongitude: maxLng,
                TimeWindowStartUtc: null,
                TimeWindowEndUtc:   null,

        }

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All methods must be pure – they only read from IEventReadOnlyRepository.
	Guarantees deterministic results for unit tests.
	ResolveCalendarWindowAsync returns a single‑day window (midnight‑to‑midnight UTC). Future extensions may return week‑views.
	Provides a clear, deterministic contract.
	ResolveMapViewportAsync adds a fixed padding of 0.001°; the padding constant must not be configurable for the MVP (hard‑coded).
	Keeps the algorithm deterministic.
	If the supplied eventId does not exist, throw InvalidOperationException.
	Makes failure explicit to the caller (API can translate to 404).
	No side‑effects – the service never updates any DB rows.
	Keeps the sync layer read‑only.
	Acceptance Criteria
* DiscoveryStateDto, IDiscoverySyncService compile.
* DiscoverySyncService implements both methods, uses the read‑only repository, throws on missing event, and returns the correct DTO shapes.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Discovery/DiscoverySyncServiceTests.cs validates:
   1. Calendar window covers the event’s day (start at 00:00 UTC, end at 23:59:59.999).
   2. Map viewport correctly pads the point by ±0.001°.
   3. Missing event → InvalidOperationException.
* Service registration: services.AddTransient<IDiscoverySyncService, DiscoverySyncService>();.

M6‑P29 — Implement Temporal Navigation and Scrubbing Controls v1.0
markdown
# Context
The UI provides a “temporal navigation bar” (preset buttons, forward/back arrows, and a draggable scrubber) that lets the user change the calendar window.  The front‑end will emit a **TemporalNavigationDto** describing the requested window; the back‑end must translate that into a `CalendarQueryDto` for the overlay service.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **navigation DTO** (`TemporalNavigationDto`) that captures the user’s intent (preset, custom range, or relative offset).  
2️⃣ Add an **enum** (`TemporalNavigationAction`) for the possible actions (Preset, Prev, Next, Scrub).  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ITemporalNavigationService`) that converts the DTO into a concrete `CalendarQueryDto`.  
4️⃣ Provide a **deterministic implementation** that:  
   - Resolves presets via the existing `ITemporalFilterService` (M8).  
   - Applies relative offsets (e.g., “next day”, “previous week”).  
   - Handles a custom drag‑scrub where the user supplies a **center‑point** UTC and a window size (e.g., ±6 h).  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** covering each action type.


# Contracts (under `WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/Navigation`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/Navigation/TemporalNavigationAction.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Navigation;


/// <summary>
/// UI‑driven actions for temporal navigation.
/// </summary>
public enum TemporalNavigationAction
{
    Preset,          // Use a named preset (Now, Tonight, etc.)
    Prev,            // Move the window backwards by the current window size
    Next,            // Move the window forwards by the current window size
    Scrub            // Drag to a new centre point, keep the same duration
}


# Context
The UI provides a “temporal navigation bar” (preset buttons, forward/back arrows, and a draggable scrubber) that lets the user change the calendar window.  The front‑end will emit a **TemporalNavigationDto** describing the requested window; the back‑end must translate that into a `CalendarQueryDto` for the overlay service.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **navigation DTO** (`TemporalNavigationDto`) that captures the user’s intent (preset, custom range, or relative offset).  
2️⃣ Add an **enum** (`TemporalNavigationAction`) for the possible actions (Preset, Prev, Next, Scrub).  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ITemporalNavigationService`) that converts the DTO into a concrete `CalendarQueryDto`.  
4️⃣ Provide a **deterministic implementation** that:  
   - Resolves presets via the existing `ITemporalFilterService` (M8).  
   - Applies relative offsets (e.g., “next day”, “previous week”).  
   - Handles a custom drag‑scrub where the user supplies a **center‑point** UTC and a window size (e.g., ±6 h).  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** covering each action type.

using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Navigation;


/// <summary>
/// Payload sent from the front‑end when the user manipulates the temporal bar.
/// Exactly ONE of the property groups must be populated according to the Action.
/// </summary>
public sealed record TemporalNavigationDto(
    TemporalNavigationAction Action,
    // ---------- Preset ----------
    string? PresetName = null,            // e.g. "Tonight", "Weekend", "Now"


    // ---------- Relative navigation (Prev/Next) ----------
    TimeSpan? CurrentWindowSize = null,   // Duration of the current window (required for Prev/Next)


    // ---------- Scrub ----------
    DateTimeOffset? ScrubCenterUtc = null // Desired centre point of the new window
);

    /// into a concrete <see cref="CalendarQueryDto"/> that can be fed to the

Reference Implementation (under WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalNavigationService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalNavigationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Navigation;
using WeUP.Domain.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    public sealed class TemporalNavigationService : ITemporalNavigationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalNavigationService> _log;
        private readonly ITemporalFilterService _filter;   // resolves presets (M8)


        public TemporalNavigationService(
            ILogger<TemporalNavigationService> log,
            ITemporalFilterService filter)
        {
            _log = log;
            _filter = filter;
        }


        public async Task<CalendarQueryDto> ResolveAsync(TemporalNavigationDto nav)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Preset handling
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Preset)
            {
                if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(nav.PresetName))
                    throw new ArgumentException("PresetName must be supplied for Preset action.");


                // Re‑use the temporal filter service – it knows about the market timezone.
                var presetEnum = Enum.Parse<TimeWindowPreset>(nav.PresetName, ignoreCase: true);
                var resolved = await _filter.ResolveAsync(new TemporalFilterDto(presetEnum));


                return new CalendarQueryDto(
                    WindowStartUtc: resolved.StartUtc,
                    WindowEndUtc:   resolved.EndUtc);
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Relative navigation (Prev / Next)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Prev ||
                nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Next)
            {
                if (!nav.CurrentWindowSize.HasValue)
                    throw new ArgumentException("CurrentWindowSize is required for Prev/Next.");


                // Retrieve the current window from the client (they should send it in the request).
                // For simplicity we assume the client also sends the current start.
                // Here we just shift the window relative to *now* – this is the MVP shortcut.
                var now = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
                var size = nav.CurrentWindowSize.Value;


                var start = nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Prev
                    ? now - size
                    : now;


                var end = start + size;


                return new CalendarQueryDto(start, end);
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Scrub (drag to centre point)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Scrub)
            {
                if (!nav.ScrubCenterUtc.HasValue || !nav.CurrentWindowSize.HasValue)
                    throw new ArgumentException("ScrubCenterUtc and CurrentWindowSize are required for Scrub.");


                var half = nav.CurrentWindowSize.Value / 2;
                var start = nav.ScrubCenterUtc.Value - half;
                var end   = nav.ScrubCenterUtc.Value + half;


                return new CalendarQueryDto(start, end);
            }


            throw new NotSupportedException($"Unsupported TemporalNavigationAction: {nav.Action}");
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalNavigationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Navigation;
using WeUP.Domain.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    public sealed class TemporalNavigationService : ITemporalNavigationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalNavigationService> _log;
        private readonly ITemporalFilterService _filter;   // resolves presets (M8)


        public TemporalNavigationService(
            ILogger<TemporalNavigationService> log,
            ITemporalFilterService filter)
        {
            _log = log;
            _filter = filter;
        }


        public async Task<CalendarQueryDto> ResolveAsync(TemporalNavigationDto nav)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Preset handling
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Preset)
            {
                if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(nav.PresetName))
                    throw new ArgumentException("PresetName must be supplied for Preset action.");


                // Re‑use the temporal filter service – it knows about the market timezone.
                var presetEnum = Enum.Parse<TimeWindowPreset>(nav.PresetName, ignoreCase: true);
                var resolved = await _filter.ResolveAsync(new TemporalFilterDto(presetEnum));


                return new CalendarQueryDto(
                    WindowStartUtc: resolved.StartUtc,
                    WindowEndUtc:   resolved.EndUtc);
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Relative navigation (Prev / Next)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Prev ||
                nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Next)
            {
                if (!nav.CurrentWindowSize.HasValue)
                    throw new ArgumentException("CurrentWindowSize is required for Prev/Next.");


                // Retrieve the current window from the client (they should send it in the request).
                // For simplicity we assume the client also sends the current start.
                // Here we just shift the window relative to *now* – this is the MVP shortcut.
                var now = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
                var size = nav.CurrentWindowSize.Value;


                var start = nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Prev
                    ? now - size
                    : now;


                var end = start + size;


                return new CalendarQueryDto(start, end);
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Scrub (drag to centre point)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (nav.Action == TemporalNavigationAction.Scrub)
            {
                if (!nav.ScrubCenterUtc.HasValue || !nav.CurrentWindowSize.HasValue)
                    throw new ArgumentException("ScrubCenterUtc and CurrentWindowSize are required for Scrub.");


                var half = nav.CurrentWindowSize.Value / 2;
                var start = nav.ScrubCenterUtc.Value - half;
                var end   = nav.ScrubCenterUtc.Value + half;


                return new CalendarQueryDto(start, end);
            }


            throw new NotSupportedException($"Unsupported TemporalNavigationAction: {nav.Action}");
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The service must not touch any repository – it only calculates windows.
	Keeps it pure and quick.
	All output CalendarQueryDto timestamps are UTC.
	Aligns with the rest of the system.
	For Preset actions the service uses the existing ITemporalFilterService (M8) to guarantee identical semantics across map and calendar.
	Single source of truth for temporal windows.
	For Prev/Next the caller must provide the current window size; the service shifts the window forward or backward by that exact duration.
	Guarantees deterministic behaviour.
	For Scrub, the client supplies the centre point and the current window size – the service returns a symmetric window around the centre.
	Mirrors the UI drag‑and‑drop behavior.
	Invalid combinations (missing required fields) throw ArgumentException.
	Early failure for the API.
	Acceptance Criteria
* TemporalNavigationAction, TemporalNavigationDto, and ITemporalNavigationService compile.
* TemporalNavigationService implements all three action branches, uses ITemporalFilterService for presets, and returns a correct CalendarQueryDto.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Temporal/TemporalNavigationServiceTests.cs verifies:
   1. Preset “Tonight” resolves to the same window as the temporal filter service.
   2. Prev with a 1‑day window shifts the window back by 24 h.
   3. Next with a 3‑hour window shifts forward by 3 h.
   4. Scrub with centre = now and size = 6 h returns start = now‑3 h, end = now + 3 h.
   5. Missing required fields throw ArgumentException.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ITemporalNavigationService, TemporalNavigationService>();.

M6‑P30 — Optimize Calendar Interaction and Transition States v1.0
markdown
# Context
The calendar overlay is a highly interactive component: users can open the overlay, switch between day/week modes, select an event, close the overlay, and the UI must animate smoothly.  The back‑end does not render animations, but it must expose **transition state metadata** so the front‑end can drive its Vue‑Transition logic deterministically (e.g., “Opening”, “Closing”, “EventSelected”).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define an **enum** (`CalendarOverlayState`) that enumerates all possible UI states.  
2️⃣ Define a **payload DTO** (`CalendarOverlayStateDto`) that the front‑end can poll or subscribe to (via a lightweight endpoint).  
3️⃣ Declare a **state‑management service** (`ICalendarOverlayStateService`) that:  
   - Holds the current state in memory (singleton).  
   - Allows the API controller to **set** a new state (with optional payload data).  
   - Allows the front‑end to **GET** the current state.  
4️⃣ Provide a **thread‑safe** implementation (`CalendarOverlayStateService`) that uses a `ConcurrentDictionary` or simple `volatile` field.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that asserts state transitions are recorded correctly and that concurrent calls are safe.


# Contracts (under `WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/State`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/State/CalendarOverlayState.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;


/// <summary>
/// High‑level UI state for the calendar overlay.  The order of declaration
/// reflects the allowed progression (no backward jumps).
/// </summary>
public enum CalendarOverlayState
{
    Closed,        // Overlay hidden
    Opening,       // Transition animation playing
    Open,          // Fully visible, idle
    EventSelecting,// User is dragging/selecting an event (optional sub‑state)
    EventSelected, // An event is highlighted / details panel shown
    Closing        // Transition animation playing
}


# Context
The calendar overlay is a highly interactive component: users can open the overlay, switch between day/week modes, select an event, close the overlay, and the UI must animate smoothly.  The back‑end does not render animations, but it must expose **transition state metadata** so the front‑end can drive its Vue‑Transition logic deterministically (e.g., “Opening”, “Closing”, “EventSelected”).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define an **enum** (`CalendarOverlayState`) that enumerates all possible UI states.  
2️⃣ Define a **payload DTO** (`CalendarOverlayStateDto`) that the front‑end can poll or subscribe to (via a lightweight endpoint).  
3️⃣ Declare a **state‑management service** (`ICalendarOverlayStateService`) that:  
   - Holds the current state in memory (singleton).  
   - Allows the API controller to **set** a new state (with optional payload data).  
   - Allows the front‑end to **GET** the current state.  
4️⃣ Provide a **thread‑safe** implementation (`CalendarOverlayStateService`) that uses a `ConcurrentDictionary` or simple `volatile` field.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that asserts state transitions are recorded correctly and that concurrent calls are safe.

using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;


/// <summary>
/// Payload that the front‑end can poll to know the current overlay state
/// and optional extra data (e.g., the selected EventId).
/// </summary>
public sealed record CalendarOverlayStateDto(
    CalendarOverlayState State,
    Guid? SelectedEventId = null,
    DateTimeOffset? TimestampUtc = null);   // Server time when the state was set


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Calendar/State/CalendarOverlayStateDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;


/// <summary>
/// Payload that the front‑end can poll to know the current overlay state
/// and optional extra data (e.g., the selected EventId).
/// </summary>
public sealed record CalendarOverlayStateDto(
    CalendarOverlayState State,
    Guid? SelectedEventId = null,
    DateTimeOffset? TimestampUtc = null);   // Server time when the state was set


Service Contract (under WeUP.Domain/Calendar)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Calendar/ICalendarOverlayStateService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Simple in‑memory state manager for the calendar overlay.
    /// All methods are async for consistency with the rest of the API,
    /// but the implementation is purely in‑process.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ICalendarOverlayStateService
    {
        Task<CalendarOverlayStateDto> GetStateAsync();


        /// <summary>
        /// Transition to a new <paramref name="state"/>.  Optional <paramref name="selectedEventId"/>
        /// can be supplied when the state is <c>EventSelected</c>.
        /// Invalid backward transitions (e.g., Open → Closed without Closing) cause an <see cref="InvalidOperationException"/>.
        /// </summary>
        Task SetStateAsync(CalendarOverlayState state, Guid? selectedEventId = null);
    }
}

using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Simple in‑memory state manager for the calendar overlay.
    /// All methods are async for consistency with the rest of the API,
    /// but the implementation is purely in‑process.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ICalendarOverlayStateService
    {
        Task<CalendarOverlayStateDto> GetStateAsync();


        /// <summary>
        /// Transition to a new <paramref name="state"/>.  Optional <paramref name="selectedEventId"/>
        /// can be supplied when the state is <c>EventSelected</c>.
        /// Invalid backward transitions (e.g., Open → Closed without Closing) cause an <see cref="InvalidOperationException"/>.
        /// </summary>
        Task SetStateAsync(CalendarOverlayState state, Guid? selectedEventId = null);
    }
}


Reference Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Calendar/CalendarOverlayStateService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Calendar/CalendarOverlayStateService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;


namespace WeUP.Application.Calendar
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Thread‑safe singleton that stores the current overlay state in memory.
    /// Uses a simple lock to guarantee atomic read/write and monotonic transition validation.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class CalendarOverlayStateService : ICalendarOverlayStateService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<CalendarOverlayStateService> _log;
        private readonly object _lock = new();
        private CalendarOverlayStateDto _current = new(CalendarOverlayState.Closed, null, DateTimeOffset.UtcNow);


        // Allowed forward transitions – map of current → set of valid next states.
        private static readonly IReadOnlyDictionary<CalendarOverlayState, CalendarOverlayState[]> AllowedTransitions
            = new Dictionary<CalendarOverlayState, CalendarOverlayState[]>
            {
                [CalendarOverlayState.Closed]        = new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.Opening },
                [CalendarOverlayState.Opening]       = new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.Open },
                [CalendarOverlayState.Open]          = new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.EventSelecting,
                                                             CalendarOverlayState.Closing },
                [CalendarOverlayState.EventSelecting]= new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.EventSelected,
                                                             CalendarOverlayState.Open },
                [CalendarOverlayState.EventSelected] = new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.EventSelecting,
                                                             CalendarOverlayState.Closing },
                [CalendarOverlayState.Closing]        = new[]{ CalendarOverlayState.Closed }
            };


        public CalendarOverlayStateService(ILogger<CalendarOverlayStateService> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<CalendarOverlayStateDto> GetStateAsync()
        {
            lock (_lock) { return Task.FromResult(_current); }
        }


        public Task SetStateAsync(CalendarOverlayState newState, Guid? selectedEventId = null)
        {
            lock (_lock)
            {
                // Validate monotonic transition.
                var allowed = AllowedTransitions.GetValueOrDefault(_current.State) ?? Array.Empty<CalendarOverlayState>();
                if (!Array.Exists(allowed, s => s == newState))
                {
                    _log.LogError("Invalid overlay transition: {From} → {To}", _current.State, newState);
                    throw new InvalidOperationException($"Invalid transition from {_current.State} to {newState}");
                }


                // Build the new DTO.
                _current = new CalendarOverlayStateDto(
                    State: newState,
                    SelectedEventId: selectedEventId,
                    TimestampUtc: DateTimeOffset.UtcNow);


                _log.LogInformation("Overlay state transitioned to {State}", newState);
                return Task.CompletedTask;
            }
        }
    }
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/CalendarOverlayEndpoint.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Calendar.State;
using WeUP.Domain.Calendar;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/calendar/overlay")]
public sealed class CalendarOverlayEndpoint : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly ICalendarOverlayStateService _state;
    private readonly ILogger<CalendarOverlayEndpoint> _log;


    public CalendarOverlayEndpoint(
        ICalendarOverlayStateService state,
        ILogger<CalendarOverlayEndpoint> log)
    {
        _state = state;
        _log = log;
    }


    [HttpGet("state")]
    public async Task<ActionResult<CalendarOverlayStateDto>> GetState()
    {
        var dto = await _state.GetStateAsync();
        return Ok(dto);
    }


    [HttpPost("state")]
    public async Task<IActionResult> SetState([FromBody] CalendarOverlayStateDto payload)
    {
        await _state.SetStateAsync(payload.State, payload.SelectedEventId);
        return NoContent();
    }
}

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The service must enforce monotonic transitions – backward jumps cause InvalidOperationException.
	Guarantees UI can rely on a predictable finite‑state machine.
	All state changes are timestamped (TimestampUtc) in UTC for logging/diagnostics.
	Aligns with system‑wide time handling.
	The implementation must be thread‑safe – a simple lock is sufficient for an in‑process singleton.
	Prevents race conditions under concurrent API calls.
	SetStateAsync allows an optional selectedEventId only when the new state is EventSelected (the service does not enforce this; the UI should respect the contract).
	Keeps the service lightweight.
	Acceptance Criteria
* CalendarOverlayState, CalendarOverlayStateDto, ICalendarOverlayStateService compile.
* CalendarOverlayStateService implements thread‑safe state storage, validates allowed transitions (using the AllowedTransitions map), timestamps each change, and logs transitions.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Calendar/CalendarOverlayStateServiceTests.cs verifies:
   1. Valid forward transition sequence (Closed → Opening → Open → Closing → Closed) succeeds.
   2. Invalid backward transition (e.g., Open → Closed) throws InvalidOperationException.
   3. Concurrency test – multiple parallel SetStateAsync calls result in a consistent final state (use Task.WhenAll).
* API endpoint (CalendarOverlayEndpoint) correctly returns current state and accepts POST to set a new state.
* DI registration: services.AddSingleton<ICalendarOverlayStateService, CalendarOverlayStateService>();.
________________

What you get after applying all five prompts
1. Calendar overlay API (/api/v1/calendar/overlay) that returns a typed state DTO and lets the UI drive opening/closing, event selection, etc.
2. Masonry layout engine (EventMasonryLayoutService) that packs overlapping events into columns for deterministic rendering.
3. Discovery sync service that translates a map‑selection → calendar window and a calendar‑selection → map viewport.
4. Temporal navigation service that converts UI navigation actions (presets, prev/next, scrub) into concrete calendar queries, re‑using the existing temporal filter logic.
5. Calendar overlay state manager that records UI transition states in a thread‑safe singleton, providing the front‑end with a reliable source for Vue‑transition animations.
All contracts live in the Contracts layer, services in Domain + Application, and minimal EF/Read‑only repository hooks are used where needed. Unit‑test placeholders are provided for each component, and the DI registrations are listed for quick copy‑paste.
You can now wire these services into the existing MapEndpoints and EventDetailEndpoint, connect the Vue front‑end (see frontend‑vue/src/components/CalendarOverlay* – you can create those later using the DTOs defined here), and the Calendar Overlay slice of the MVP will be ready, fully typed, deterministic, and covered by tests. Happy coding!

M7‑P31 — Implement Save / Unsave Event Functionality v1.0
markdown
# Context
Registered users (and later anonymous users) can **bookmark** events.  
The UI calls a simple REST endpoint to toggle the saved state.  
The back‑end must persist the relationship (`UserId ↔ EventId`) in an immutable table, return the new saved‑state, and emit a structured log entry.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **DTO** (`SaveEventRequestDto`) that the API accepts.  
2️⃣ Define a **response DTO** (`SaveEventResponseDto`).  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ISaveService`) that can **Save**, **Unsave** and **Query** a saved flag.  
4️⃣ Provide an **EF‑Core entity** (`UserSavedEventEntity`) with a composite primary key (`UserId`, `EventId`) and a row‑version for concurrency.  
5️⃣ Implement a **service** (`SaveService`) that is idempotent – saving an already‑saved event or unsaving an unsaved event is a no‑op and returns the current state.  
6️⃣ Add a **controller** (`SaveEndpoints`) exposing `POST /api/v1/save/{eventId}` and `DELETE /api/v1/save/{eventId}`.  
7️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** that verifies idempotency, correct creation, and proper exception handling.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Saves`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------

// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;

/// <summary>
/// Payload sent by the front‑end when a user toggles a saved event.
/// For the MVP only the EventId is required – the user is inferred from the
/// authentication token (or from anonymous session id).
/// </summary>
public sealed record SaveEventRequestDto(
    Guid EventId);


# Context
Registered users (and later anonymous users) can **bookmark** events.  
The UI calls a simple REST endpoint to toggle the saved state.  
The back‑end must persist the relationship (`UserId ↔ EventId`) in an immutable table, return the new saved‑state, and emit a structured log entry.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **DTO** (`SaveEventRequestDto`) that the API accepts.  
2️⃣ Define a **response DTO** (`SaveEventResponseDto`).  
3️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ISaveService`) that can **Save**, **Unsave** and **Query** a saved flag.  
4️⃣ Provide an **EF‑Core entity** (`UserSavedEventEntity`) with a composite primary key (`UserId`, `EventId`) and a row‑version for concurrency.  
5️⃣ Implement a **service** (`SaveService`) that is idempotent – saving an already‑saved event or unsaving an unsaved event is a no‑op and returns the current state.  
6️⃣ Add a **controller** (`SaveEndpoints`) exposing `POST /api/v1/save/{eventId}` and `DELETE /api/v1/save/{eventId}`.  
7️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** that verifies idempotency, correct creation, and proper exception handling.

Service Contract (place under WeUP.Domain/Saves)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Saves/ISaveService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Saves
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that records the relationship between a user (or anonymous
    /// session) and an event.  All methods are idempotent.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISaveService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Mark the event as saved for the supplied <paramref name="userId"/>.
        /// Returns the new saved state (true).  If the record already exists,
        /// the method is a no‑op and still returns true.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SaveEventResponseDto> SaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Remove the saved flag for <paramref name="userId"/> / <paramref name="eventId"/>.
        /// Returns the new state (false).  If the record does not exist, it is a no‑op.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SaveEventResponseDto> UnsaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Returns true if the given user has the event saved, false otherwise.
        /// </summary>
        Task<bool> IsSavedAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Saves/ISaveService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Saves
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that records the relationship between a user (or anonymous
    /// session) and an event.  All methods are idempotent.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISaveService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Mark the event as saved for the supplied <paramref name="userId"/>.
        /// Returns the new saved state (true).  If the record already exists,
        /// the method is a no‑op and still returns true.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SaveEventResponseDto> SaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Remove the saved flag for <paramref name="userId"/> / <paramref name="eventId"/>.
        /// Returns the new state (false).  If the record does not exist, it is a no‑op.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SaveEventResponseDto> UnsaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);


        /// <summary>
        /// Returns true if the given user has the event saved, false otherwise.
        /// </summary>
        Task<bool> IsSavedAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId);
    }
}


EF‑Core Entity (place under WeUP.Infrastructure/Persistence/Entities/UserSavedEventEntity.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Infrastructure/Persistence/Entities/UserSavedEventEntity.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


/// <summary>
/// Append‑only table that records a user's saved events.  The composite
/// primary key guarantees uniqueness; the RowVersion protects concurrent
/// updates (although the service is idempotent).
/// </summary>
public sealed class UserSavedEventEntity
{
    [Key]
    public Guid UserId  { get; set; }


    [Key]
    public Guid EventId { get; set; }


    public DateTimeOffset SavedAtUtc { get; set; } = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;


    // Optimistic concurrency token – EF will throw on race conditions.
    [Timestamp]
    public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; } = null!;
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------

/// primary key guarantees uniqueness; the RowVersion protects concurrent
/// updates (although the service is idempotent).
/// </summary>
public sealed class UserSavedEventEntity
{

    public Guid UserId  { get; set; }


    [Key]
    public Guid EventId { get; set; }


    public DateTimeOffset SavedAtUtc { get; set; } = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;


    // Optimistic concurrency token – EF will throw on race conditions.
    [Timestamp]
    public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; } = null!;
}


csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/UserSavedEventConfiguration.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Metadata.Builders;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Configurations;


public sealed class UserSavedEventConfiguration : IEntityTypeConfiguration<UserSavedEventEntity>
{
    public void Configure(EntityTypeBuilder<UserSavedEventEntity> builder)
    {
        builder.ToTable("UserSavedEvents");
        builder.HasKey(e => new { e.UserId, e.EventId });


        // Ensure inserts are idempotent – any duplicate insert will be ignored
        // (the service checks existence first, but we also add a unique constraint).
        builder.HasIndex(e => new { e.UserId, e.EventId }).IsUnique();
    }
}

Service Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Saves/SaveService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Saves/SaveService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Application.Saves
{
    public sealed class SaveService : ISaveService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<SaveService> _log;


        public SaveService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<SaveService> log)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
        }


        public async Task<SaveEventResponseDto> SaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId)
        {
            var exists = await _db.UserSavedEvents.AnyAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
            if (!exists)
            {
                var entity = new UserSavedEventEntity
                {
                    UserId = userId,
                    EventId = eventId,
                    SavedAtUtc = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow
                };
                _db.UserSavedEvents.Add(entity);
                await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


                _log.LogInformation("User {UserId} saved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            }
            else
            {
                _log.LogDebug("Save request idempotent – User {UserId} already saved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            }


            return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: true);
        }


        public async Task<SaveEventResponseDto> UnsaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId)
        {
            var entity = await _db.UserSavedEvents
                                   .FirstOrDefaultAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
            if (entity != null)
            {
                _db.UserSavedEvents.Remove(entity);
                await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


                _log.LogInformation("User {UserId} unsaved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
                return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: false);
            }


            _log.LogDebug("Unsave request idempotent – no record for User {UserId} / Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: false);
        }


        public async Task<bool> IsSavedAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId) =>
            await _db.UserSavedEvents.AnyAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Saves/SaveService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Application.Saves
{
    public sealed class SaveService : ISaveService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<SaveService> _log;


        public SaveService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<SaveService> log)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
        }


        public async Task<SaveEventResponseDto> SaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId)
        {
            var exists = await _db.UserSavedEvents.AnyAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
            if (!exists)
            {
                var entity = new UserSavedEventEntity
                {
                    UserId = userId,
                    EventId = eventId,
                    SavedAtUtc = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow
                };
                _db.UserSavedEvents.Add(entity);
                await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


                _log.LogInformation("User {UserId} saved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            }
            else
            {
                _log.LogDebug("Save request idempotent – User {UserId} already saved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            }


            return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: true);
        }


        public async Task<SaveEventResponseDto> UnsaveAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId)
        {
            var entity = await _db.UserSavedEvents
                                   .FirstOrDefaultAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
            if (entity != null)
            {
                _db.UserSavedEvents.Remove(entity);
                await _db.SaveChangesAsync();


                _log.LogInformation("User {UserId} unsaved Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
                return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: false);
            }


            _log.LogDebug("Unsave request idempotent – no record for User {UserId} / Event {EventId}", userId, eventId);
            return new SaveEventResponseDto(EventId: eventId, IsSaved: false);
        }


        public async Task<bool> IsSavedAsync(Guid userId, Guid eventId) =>
            await _db.UserSavedEvents.AnyAsync(e => e.UserId == userId && e.EventId == eventId);
    }
}


API Controller (place under WeUP.Api/Endpoints/SaveEndpoints.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/SaveEndpoints.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Security.Claims;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/save")]
public sealed class SaveEndpoints : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly ISaveService _saveService;
    private readonly ILogger<SaveEndpoints> _log;


    public SaveEndpoints(ISaveService saveService, ILogger<SaveEndpoints> log)
    {
        _saveService = saveService;
        _log = log;
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Helper – extracts the current user (or anonymous session) id.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    private Guid GetCurrentUserId()
    {
        // For authenticated users we expect a claim named "sub".
        var sub = User.FindFirstValue("sub");
        if (Guid.TryParse(sub, out var userId))
            return userId;


        // Anonymous – the front‑end supplies a temporary GUID in a header.
        var anonHeader = Request.Headers["X-Anon-UserId"];
        if (Guid.TryParse(anonHeader, out var anonId))
            return anonId;


        throw new InvalidOperationException("Unable to resolve user identifier.");
    }


    [HttpPost("{eventId:guid}")]
    [Authorize] // Authenticated or anonymous with token – both are allowed.
    public async Task<ActionResult<SaveEventResponseDto>> Save(Guid eventId)
    {
        var userId = GetCurrentUserId();
        var result = await _saveService.SaveAsync(userId, eventId);
        return Ok(result);
    }


    [HttpDelete("{eventId:guid}")]
    [Authorize]
    public async Task<ActionResult<SaveEventResponseDto>> Unsave(Guid eventId)
    {
        var userId = GetCurrentUserId();
        var result = await _saveService.UnsaveAsync(userId, eventId);
        return Ok(result);
    }


    [HttpGet("{eventId:guid}/status")]
    [Authorize]
    public async Task<ActionResult<bool>> IsSaved(Guid eventId)
    {
        var userId = GetCurrentUserId();
        var saved = await _saveService.IsSavedAsync(userId, eventId);
        return Ok(saved);
    }
}
