Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All service methods are idempotent – repeated calls with the same parameters must not create duplicate rows or raise errors.
	Guarantees safe retry (e.g., network retries).
	UserSavedEventEntity uses a composite PK (UserId, EventId) and a RowVersion for optimistic concurrency.
	Prevents race conditions.
	The controller extracts the user id from the JWT sub claim or from the X-Anon-UserId header (used by anonymous sessions).
	Supports both authenticated and anonymous flows.
	No business logic is performed inside the controller – it merely forwards to the service.
	Keeps the controller thin.
	All timestamps are stored as UTC (SavedAtUtc).
	Aligns with system‑wide time policy.
	Errors are logged with structured properties (UserId, EventId).
	Enables observability (M10).
	Acceptance Criteria
* SaveEventRequestDto, SaveEventResponseDto, ISaveService, UserSavedEventEntity, SaveService, and SaveEndpoints compile.
* SaveService.SaveAsync creates a row only if it does not exist, logs a creation, and returns IsSaved = true.
* SaveService.UnsaveAsync deletes the row if it exists, logs, and returns IsSaved = false.
* Both methods are idempotent – calling them again with the same parameters returns the same result without throwing.
* IsSavedAsync returns the correct boolean flag.
* Unit‑test placeholders (WeUP.Tests/Saves/SaveServiceTests.cs) verify:
   1. Saving a fresh event creates a row.
   2. Saving the same event again does not duplicate rows and still returns true.
   3. Unsaving removes the row; unsaving again is a no‑op.
   4. IsSavedAsync reflects the current state.
* Controller extracts the user id correctly from JWT or anonymous header and returns 200 OK with the response DTO.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ISaveService, SaveService>();.

M7‑P32 — Define Local Persistence Model for Anonymous Users v1.0
markdown
# Context
Anonymous users (no JWT) still need a way to *temporarily* save events before they sign‑up.  The MVP stores these locally in the browser’s `localStorage` using a versioned JSON schema.  The data must be **type‑safe**, **migratable**, and **expire** after a configurable TTL (e.g., 30 days).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **TypeScript interface** (`AnonymousSavedState`) that represents the persisted structure.  
2️⃣ Add a **schema version** field (`v: number`) and a **timestamp** (`savedAtUtc`).  
3️⃣ Provide a **utility module** (`localSavedState.ts`) that implements:  
   - `load(): Promise<AnonymousSavedState | null>` – reads, validates, and returns the object or `null`.  
   - `save(state: AnonymousSavedState): Promise<void>` – writes with JSON.stringify.  
   - `clear(): Promise<void>` – removes the key.  
   - `migrateIfNeeded(raw: any): AnonymousSavedState` – upgrades legacy formats to the current version.  
4️⃣ Include **runtime validation** (basic type‑guards) to guard against corrupted data.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that checks load/save, migration, and TTL expiry.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/types`)


```typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/types/anonymousSavedState.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------
export interface AnonymousSavedState {
  /** Schema version – increment when the shape changes. */
  v: number;                        // current version = 1


  /** ISO‑8601 timestamp when the snapshot was stored (UTC). */
  savedAtUtc: string;


  /** Array of saved event IDs (GUID strings). */
  eventIds: string[];
}


# Context
Anonymous users (no JWT) still need a way to *temporarily* save events before they sign‑up.  The MVP stores these locally in the browser’s `localStorage` using a versioned JSON schema.  The data must be **type‑safe**, **migratable**, and **expire** after a configurable TTL (e.g., 30 days).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **TypeScript interface** (`AnonymousSavedState`) that represents the persisted structure.  
2️⃣ Add a **schema version** field (`v: number`) and a **timestamp** (`savedAtUtc`).  
3️⃣ Provide a **utility module** (`localSavedState.ts`) that implements:  
   - `load(): Promise<AnonymousSavedState | null>` – reads, validates, and returns the object or `null`.  
   - `save(state: AnonymousSavedState): Promise<void>` – writes with JSON.stringify.  
   - `clear(): Promise<void>` – removes the key.  
   - `migrateIfNeeded(raw: any): AnonymousSavedState` – upgrades legacy formats to the current version.  
4️⃣ Include **runtime validation** (basic type‑guards) to guard against corrupted data.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that checks load/save, migration, and TTL expiry.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/types`)


```typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/types/anonymousSavedState.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------
export interface AnonymousSavedState {
  /** Schema version – increment when the shape changes. */
  v: number;                        // current version = 1


  /** ISO‑8601 timestamp when the snapshot was stored (UTC). */
  savedAtUtc: string;


  /** Array of saved event IDs (GUID strings). */
  eventIds: string[];

Utility Module (place under WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/lib/localSavedState.ts)
typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP_Phase0_AIStudio/frontend-vue/src/lib/localSavedState.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------
import { AnonymousSavedState } from '@/types/anonymousSavedState';


const STORAGE_KEY = 'weup_anonymous_saved_state';
const CURRENT_VERSION = 1;
const MAX_AGE_DAYS = 30; // TTL for anonymous data


/** Simple runtime type‑guard. */
function isAnonymousSavedState(obj: any): obj is AnonymousSavedState {
  return (
    typeof obj === 'object' &&
    obj !== null &&
    typeof obj.v === 'number' &&
    typeof obj.savedAtUtc === 'string' &&
    Array.isArray(obj.eventIds) &&
    obj.eventIds.every((id: any) => typeof id === 'string')
  );
}


/** Migrate older formats (if any) to the current schema. */
function migrateIfNeeded(raw: any): AnonymousSavedState {
  // Future migrations go here.  For now we just coerce if possible.
  if (isAnonymousSavedState(raw) && raw.v === CURRENT_VERSION) {
    return raw;
  }


  // Example legacy shape: { ids: string[] }
  if (raw && Array.isArray(raw.ids)) {
    return {
      v: CURRENT_VERSION,
      savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
      eventIds: raw.ids,
    };
  }


  // Unknown shape → return an empty state.
  return {
    v: CURRENT_VERSION,
    savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
    eventIds: [],
  };
}


/** Load the persisted anonymous saved state, validating TTL. */
export async function loadAnonymousSavedState(): Promise<AnonymousSavedState | null> {
  const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
  if (!raw) return null;


  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    const state = migrateIfNeeded(parsed);


    // TTL check
    const ageMs = Date.now() - new Date(state.savedAtUtc).getTime();
    const maxMs = MAX_AGE_DAYS * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    if (ageMs > maxMs) {
      // Expired – clear it.
      await clearAnonymousSavedState();
      return null;
    }


    return state;
  } catch {
    // Corrupted JSON – wipe it.
    await clearAnonymousSavedState();
    return null;
  }
}


/** Persist the anonymous saved state. */
export async function saveAnonymousSavedState(state: AnonymousSavedState): Promise<void> {
  const toStore = JSON.stringify(state);
  localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, toStore);
}


/** Remove the entry from localStorage. */
export async function clearAnonymousSavedState(): Promise<void> {
  localStorage.removeItem(STORAGE_KEY);
}


/** Helper to add an event ID (idempotent). */
export async function addEventId(eventId: string): Promise<void> {
  const current = (await loadAnonymousSavedState()) ?? {
    v: CURRENT_VERSION,
    savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
    eventIds: [],
  };
  if (!current.eventIds.includes(eventId)) {
    current.eventIds.push(eventId);
    await saveAnonymousSavedState(current);
  }
}


/** Helper to remove an event ID (idempotent). */
export async function removeEventId(eventId: string): Promise<void> {
  const current = await loadAnonymousSavedState();
  if (!current) return;


  const filtered = current.eventIds.filter(id => id !== eventId);
  if (filtered.length !== current.eventIds.length) {
    current.eventIds = filtered;
    await saveAnonymousSavedState(current);
  }
}

const CURRENT_VERSION = 1;
const MAX_AGE_DAYS = 30; // TTL for anonymous data


/** Simple runtime type‑guard. */
function isAnonymousSavedState(obj: any): obj is AnonymousSavedState {
  return (
    typeof obj === 'object' &&
    obj !== null &&
    typeof obj.v === 'number' &&
    typeof obj.savedAtUtc === 'string' &&
    Array.isArray(obj.eventIds) &&
    obj.eventIds.every((id: any) => typeof id === 'string')
  );
}


/** Migrate older formats (if any) to the current schema. */
function migrateIfNeeded(raw: any): AnonymousSavedState {
  // Future migrations go here.  For now we just coerce if possible.
  if (isAnonymousSavedState(raw) && raw.v === CURRENT_VERSION) {
    return raw;
  }


  // Example legacy shape: { ids: string[] }
  if (raw && Array.isArray(raw.ids)) {
    return {
      v: CURRENT_VERSION,
      savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
      eventIds: raw.ids,
    };
  }


  // Unknown shape → return an empty state.
  return {
    v: CURRENT_VERSION,
    savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
    eventIds: [],
  };
}


/** Load the persisted anonymous saved state, validating TTL. */
export async function loadAnonymousSavedState(): Promise<AnonymousSavedState | null> {
  const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
  if (!raw) return null;


  try {
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    const state = migrateIfNeeded(parsed);


    // TTL check
    const ageMs = Date.now() - new Date(state.savedAtUtc).getTime();
    const maxMs = MAX_AGE_DAYS * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
    if (ageMs > maxMs) {
      // Expired – clear it.
      await clearAnonymousSavedState();
      return null;
    }


    return state;
  } catch {
    // Corrupted JSON – wipe it.
    await clearAnonymousSavedState();
    return null;
  }
}


/** Persist the anonymous saved state. */
export async function saveAnonymousSavedState(state: AnonymousSavedState): Promise<void> {
  const toStore = JSON.stringify(state);
  localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, toStore);
}


/** Remove the entry from localStorage. */
export async function clearAnonymousSavedState(): Promise<void> {
  localStorage.removeItem(STORAGE_KEY);
}


/** Helper to add an event ID (idempotent). */
export async function addEventId(eventId: string): Promise<void> {
  const current = (await loadAnonymousSavedState()) ?? {
    v: CURRENT_VERSION,
    savedAtUtc: new Date().toISOString(),
    eventIds: [],
  };
  if (!current.eventIds.includes(eventId)) {
    current.eventIds.push(eventId);
    await saveAnonymousSavedState(current);
  }
}


/** Helper to remove an event ID (idempotent). */
export async function removeEventId(eventId: string): Promise<void> {
  const current = await loadAnonymousSavedState();
  if (!current) return;


  const filtered = current.eventIds.filter(id => id !== eventId);
  if (filtered.length !== current.eventIds.length) {
    current.eventIds = filtered;
    await saveAnonymousSavedState(current);
  }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The persisted object must contain a v field; future schema changes will bump the version and trigger migrateIfNeeded.
	Enables forward compatibility.
	TTL is 30 days – after that the data is automatically cleared on next load.
	Prevents unbounded local storage.
	All functions are async (even if they only wrap localStorage) to keep the API consistent with server‑side calls.
	Makes it easy to swap in IndexedDB later.
	The utility must never throw; any parsing error results in a clean null return and the storage key is cleared.
	Guarantees the UI never crashes on corrupted data.
	eventIds are stored as string GUIDs – no other data is persisted.
	Keeps the payload minimal.
	The module is framework‑agnostic (pure TS) and can be imported by any component.
	Re‑usability.
	Acceptance Criteria
* AnonymousSavedState interface and localSavedState.ts compile without errors.
* loadAnonymousSavedState returns null when the key is missing, corrupted, or expired.
* saveAnonymousSavedState correctly writes the JSON string to localStorage.
* addEventId and removeEventId are idempotent and update the stored array correctly.
* Unit‑test file (frontend-vue/tests/localSavedState.spec.ts) verifies:
   1. Saving a state and loading it returns the same object.
   2. Adding the same ID twice does not duplicate it.
   3. Removing an ID works and leaves other IDs untouched.
   4. Data older than 30 days is cleared on load.
   5. Legacy shape { ids: [...] } is migrated to the current schema.

M7‑P33 — Implement Auth‑Ready Save State Migration Path v1.0
markdown
# Context
When an anonymous user eventually authenticates (sign‑up / login), any events they saved locally must be **merged** into their permanent server‑side saved‑events list.  The merge must be **idempotent**, deduplicate duplicate IDs, and then clear the anonymous local storage.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **migration service contract** (`IAnonymousSaveMigrationService`).  
2️⃣ Implement the service (`AnonymousSaveMigrationService`) that:  
   - Loads the local `AnonymousSavedState`.  
   - Calls the existing `ISaveService.SaveAsync` for each stored `eventId`.  
   - Handles partial failures gracefully (continue migrating the rest).  
   - Clears the local storage after successful migration.  
3️⃣ Add a **controller hook** (`AuthController` – after successful login) that invokes the migration service (the hook can be a simple method call from the JWT authentication pipeline).  
4️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** that verifies:  
   - All locally saved IDs are persisted on the server.  
   - Duplicate IDs are handled gracefully (no double inserts).  
   - Local storage is cleared after migration.  


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Saves`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Saves/AnonymousSaveMigrationResult.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Saves;


/// <summary>
/// Result of migrating anonymous saved events into the authenticated store.
/// </summary>
public sealed record AnonymousSaveMigrationResult(
    IReadOnlyCollection<Guid> MigratedEventIds,   // IDs that were successfully saved
    IReadOnlyCollection<Guid> FailedEventIds);   // IDs that could not be saved (e.g., not found)

    /// Service that moves locally cached saved events into the permanent

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Saves/AnonymousSaveMigrationService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Saves/AnonymousSaveMigrationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;   // For direct EF access to the anonymous storage (optional)


namespace WeUP.Application.Saves
{
    public sealed class AnonymousSaveMigrationService : IAnonymousSaveMigrationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<AnonymousSaveMigrationService> _log;
        private readonly ISaveService _saveService;
        private readonly IAnonymousLocalStorage _anonStorage; // Wrapper around the TS‑side util (exposed via API)


        public AnonymousSaveMigrationService(
            ILogger<AnonymousSaveMigrationService> log,
            ISaveService saveService,
            IAnonymousLocalStorage anonStorage)
        {
            _log = log;
            _saveService = saveService;
            _anonStorage = anonStorage;
        }


        public async Task<AnonymousSaveMigrationResult> MigrateAsync(Guid authenticatedUserId)
        {
            var state = await _anonStorage.LoadAsync();
            if (state == null || state.EventIds.Count == 0)
            {
                _log.LogInformation("No anonymous saved events to migrate for user {UserId}", authenticatedUserId);
                return new AnonymousSaveMigrationResult(Array.Empty<Guid>(), Array.Empty<Guid>());
            }


            var migrated = new List<Guid>();
            var failed   = new List<Guid>();


            foreach (var eventId in state.EventIds)
            {
                try
                {
                    await _saveService.SaveAsync(authenticatedUserId, eventId);
                    migrated.Add(eventId);
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    // Log but continue with other IDs.
                    _log.LogWarning(ex, "Failed to migrate saved event {EventId} for user {UserId}", eventId, authenticatedUserId);
                    failed.Add(eventId);
                }
            }


            // Clear the anonymous storage regardless of partial failures – the user now
            // has an authoritative server copy (failed IDs can be re‑added later if needed).
            await _anonStorage.ClearAsync();


            _log.LogInformation("Anonymous save migration for user {UserId} completed. Migrated: {MigratedCount}, Failed: {FailedCount}",
                                 authenticatedUserId, migrated.Count, failed.Count);


            return new AnonymousSaveMigrationResult(
                MigratedEventIds: migrated,
                FailedEventIds:   failed);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Minimal abstraction over the front‑end local‑storage helper.
    // The front‑end will expose a tiny API (`GET /api/v1/anon/saved`) that
    // returns the JSON blob; the service calls it via HttpClient.
    // For the MVP we can mock this interface in tests.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public interface IAnonymousLocalStorage
    {
        Task<AnonymousSavedState?> LoadAsync();     // Returns null if nothing stored.
        Task ClearAsync();                          // Clears the persisted blob.
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Saves/AnonymousSaveMigrationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;   // For direct EF access to the anonymous storage (optional)


namespace WeUP.Application.Saves
{
    public sealed class AnonymousSaveMigrationService : IAnonymousSaveMigrationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<AnonymousSaveMigrationService> _log;
        private readonly ISaveService _saveService;
        private readonly IAnonymousLocalStorage _anonStorage; // Wrapper around the TS‑side util (exposed via API)


        public AnonymousSaveMigrationService(
            ILogger<AnonymousSaveMigrationService> log,
            ISaveService saveService,
            IAnonymousLocalStorage anonStorage)
        {
            _log = log;
            _saveService = saveService;
            _anonStorage = anonStorage;
        }


        public async Task<AnonymousSaveMigrationResult> MigrateAsync(Guid authenticatedUserId)
        {
            var state = await _anonStorage.LoadAsync();
            if (state == null || state.EventIds.Count == 0)
            {
                _log.LogInformation("No anonymous saved events to migrate for user {UserId}", authenticatedUserId);
                return new AnonymousSaveMigrationResult(Array.Empty<Guid>(), Array.Empty<Guid>());
            }


            var migrated = new List<Guid>();
            var failed   = new List<Guid>();


            foreach (var eventId in state.EventIds)
            {
                try
                {
                    await _saveService.SaveAsync(authenticatedUserId, eventId);
                    migrated.Add(eventId);
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    // Log but continue with other IDs.
                    _log.LogWarning(ex, "Failed to migrate saved event {EventId} for user {UserId}", eventId, authenticatedUserId);
                    failed.Add(eventId);
                }
            }


            // Clear the anonymous storage regardless of partial failures – the user now
            // has an authoritative server copy (failed IDs can be re‑added later if needed).
            await _anonStorage.ClearAsync();


            _log.LogInformation("Anonymous save migration for user {UserId} completed. Migrated: {MigratedCount}, Failed: {FailedCount}",
                                 authenticatedUserId, migrated.Count, failed.Count);


            return new AnonymousSaveMigrationResult(
                MigratedEventIds: migrated,
                FailedEventIds:   failed);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Minimal abstraction over the front‑end local‑storage helper.
    // The front‑end will expose a tiny API (`GET /api/v1/anon/saved`) that
    // returns the JSON blob; the service calls it via HttpClient.
    // For the MVP we can mock this interface in tests.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public interface IAnonymousLocalStorage
    {
        Task<AnonymousSavedState?> LoadAsync();     // Returns null if nothing stored.
        Task ClearAsync();                          // Clears the persisted blob.
    }
}

Hook into Authentication (pseudo‑code – add to the auth pipeline)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// In your authentication success handler (e.g., in Program.cs or a custom
// AuthenticationEvents.OnTokenValidated)
// ------------------------------------------------------------
services.AddTransient<IAnonymousSaveMigrationService, AnonymousSaveMigrationService>();


// Example hook:
app.Use(async (ctx, next) =>
{
    if (ctx.User?.Identity?.IsAuthenticated == true)
    {
        var userId = Guid.Parse(ctx.User.FindFirst("sub")!.Value);
        var migration = ctx.RequestServices.GetRequiredService<IAnonymousSaveMigrationService>();
        await migration.MigrateAsync(userId);
    }
    await next();
});

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The migration must be idempotent – if the same anonymous events are re‑submitted they will be ignored by ISaveService.SaveAsync.
	Guarantees safe retries (e.g., network glitch).
	The service must not abort on a single failure – it should attempt to migrate all IDs, log failures, and still clear the local storage.
	Guarantees a clean state for the user.
	IAnonymousLocalStorage is a thin abstraction; the real implementation will call the front‑end’s localSavedState via a tiny API (GET /api/v1/anon/saved).
	Keeps the back‑end decoupled from browser storage.
	All timestamp handling is in UTC.
	Consistency with the rest of the system.
	Migration runs after the JWT is validated but before any other request handling for the session.
	Ensures the server has the latest saved list for the current request.
	Acceptance Criteria
* AnonymousSaveMigrationResult, IAnonymousSaveMigrationService, and AnonymousSaveMigrationService compile.
* AnonymousSaveMigrationService loads the anonymous state, calls ISaveService.SaveAsync for each eventId, aggregates successes and failures, clears the anonymous store, and returns the result.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Saves/AnonymousSaveMigrationServiceTests.cs verifies:
   1. All IDs from a populated anonymous state are saved (mock ISaveService).
   2. Duplicate IDs are handled gracefully (only one DB insert).
   3. The anonymous storage is cleared after the call.
   4. Partial failures are captured in FailedEventIds but do not stop the migration.
* The authentication pipeline calls MigrateAsync once per successful login (no duplicate migrations).
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IAnonymousSaveMigrationService, AnonymousSaveMigrationService>();.

M7‑P34 — Build User Context and Preference Tracking v1.0
markdown
# Context
Beyond saved events, users can set **personal preferences** (preferred districts, time‑window defaults, UI theme).  These preferences are stored server‑side, keyed by `UserId`, and are read on each session start to initialise the front‑end store.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **preference DTO** (`UserPreferenceDto`).  
2️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`IUserPreferenceService`) that can `GetAsync`, `SetAsync` and `DeleteAsync`.  
3️⃣ Create an **EF‑Core entity** (`UserPreferenceEntity`) with a JSONB column (`Data`) to allow flexible key/value storage.  
4️⃣ Implement a **concrete service** (`UserPreferenceService`) that reads/writes the JSON payload.  
5️⃣ Expose an **API controller** (`UserPreferenceEndpoint`) with `GET /api/v1/preferences`, `PUT /api/v1/preferences`, `DELETE /api/v1/preferences/{key}`.  
6️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** that ensures round‑trip persistence and that unknown keys are stored unchanged.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Preferences`)


```csharp

/// The value type is a string to keep the model flexible (JSON can be stored
/// as a stringified object if needed).
/// </summary>
public sealed record UserPreferenceDto(
    IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string> Preferences);

using System.ComponentModel.DataAnnotations;


namespace WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


/// <summary>
/// Stores a JSONB blob of arbitrary key/value pairs per user.
/// The column type is “jsonb” for PostgreSQL (or TEXT for other providers).
/// </summary>
public sealed class UserPreferenceEntity
{
    [Key]
    public Guid UserId { get; set; }


    // The JSON representation of the preferences map.
    public string Data { get; set; } = "{}";


    // Concurrency token to avoid lost updates.
    [Timestamp]
    public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; } = null!;
}

Service Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Preferences/UserPreferenceService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Preferences/UserPreferenceService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Text.Json;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Preferences;
using WeUP.Domain.Preferences;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence.Entities;


namespace WeUP.Application.Preferences
{
    public sealed class UserPreferenceService : IUserPreferenceService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<UserPreferenceService> _log;


        public UserPreferenceService(WeUpDbContext db, ILogger<UserPreferenceService> log)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
        }


        public async Task<UserPreferenceDto> GetAsync(Guid userId)
        {
            var entity = await _db.UserPreferences.FindAsync(userId);
            var dict = entity != null
                ? JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Dictionary<string, string>>(entity.Data) ?? new()
                : new Dictionary<string, string>();


            return new UserPreferenceDto(dict);
        }


        public async Task SetAsync(Guid userId, IReadOnlyDictionary<string, string> preferences)
        {
            var entity = await _db.UserPreferences.FindAsync(userId);
            if (entity == null)
            {
                entity = new UserPreferenceEntity
                {
                    UserId = userId,
                    Data = JsonSerializer.Serialize(preferences)
                };
                _db.UserPreferences.Add(entity);
            }
            else
            {
                // Merge with existing data – preserve keys not being overwritten.
                var current = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Dictionary<string, string>>(entity.Data) ?? new();
                foreach (var kv in preferences)
                    current[kv.Key] = kv.Value;


                entity.Data = JsonSerializer.Serialize(current);
                _db.UserPreferences.Update(entity);
            }


            await _db.SaveChangesAsync();
            _log.LogInformation("User {UserId} preferences updated ({Count} keys).", userId, preferences.Count);
        }


        public async Task DeleteAsync(Guid userId, string key)
        {
            var entity = await _db.UserPreferences.FindAsync(userId);
            if (entity == null) return;


            var current = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<Dictionary<string, string>>(entity.Data) ?? new();
            if (current.Remove(key))
            {
                entity.Data = JsonSerializer.Serialize(current);
                await _db.SaveChangesAsync();
                _log.LogInformation("Preference key '{Key}' removed for User {UserId}.", key, userId);
            }
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------

API Controller (place under WeUP.Api/Endpoints/UserPreferenceEndpoint.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/UserPreferenceEndpoint.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Preferences;
using WeUP.Domain.Preferences;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/preferences")]
[Authorize] // Only authenticated users may modify preferences.
public sealed class UserPreferenceEndpoint : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly IUserPreferenceService _prefService;
    private readonly ILogger<UserPreferenceEndpoint> _log;


    public UserPreferenceEndpoint(IUserPreferenceService prefService, ILogger<UserPreferenceEndpoint> log)
    {
        _prefService = prefService;
        _log = log;
    }


    private Guid GetUserId()
    {
        var sub = User.FindFirst("sub")?.Value;
        if (!Guid.TryParse(sub, out var id))
            throw new InvalidOperationException("User claim 'sub' missing or invalid.");
        return id;
    }


    [HttpGet]
    public async Task<ActionResult<UserPreferenceDto>> Get()
    {
        var userId = GetUserId();
        var dto = await _prefService.GetAsync(userId);
        return Ok(dto);
    }


    [HttpPut]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Set([FromBody] UserPreferenceDto preferences)
    {
        var userId = GetUserId();
        await _prefService.SetAsync(userId, preferences.Preferences);
        return NoContent();
    }


    [HttpDelete("{key}")]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Delete(string key)
    {
        var userId = GetUserId();
        await _prefService.DeleteAsync(userId, key);
        return NoContent();
    }
}

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Preferences are stored as a JSONB column (Data). This allows arbitrary future keys without schema changes.
	Flexibility for evolving UI.
	SetAsync merges incoming keys with existing ones (does not wipe unspecified preferences).
	Prevents accidental loss of data.
	All reads/writes are optimistically concurrent (RowVersion).
	Prevents lost updates when multiple devices edit simultaneously.
	The controller extracts the sub claim – anonymous users cannot set preferences.
	Security.
	The service operates purely on the DB – no external calls.
	Simplicity and testability.
	Acceptance Criteria
* UserPreferenceDto, IUserPreferenceService, UserPreferenceEntity, UserPreferenceService and UserPreferenceEndpoint compile.
* GetAsync returns an empty dictionary when no preferences exist.
* SetAsync merges incoming key/value pairs with existing data (preserves untouched keys).
* DeleteAsync removes a single key and leaves the rest unchanged.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Preferences/UserPreferenceServiceTests.cs checks:
   1. Round‑trip persistence of a map with two keys.
   2. Updating one key does not delete the other.
   3. Deleting a key removes it from the JSON blob.
* API endpoints return 200 OK (GET) and 204 NoContent (PUT/DELETE).
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IUserPreferenceService, UserPreferenceService>();.

M7‑P35 — Create Saved Events Retrieval and Display Logic v1.0
markdown
# Context
The UI needs a **“Saved Events” panel** that lists all events a user has bookmarked, shows basic details (title, time, venue, saved flag) and allows unsaving directly from the list.  The back‑end must provide a paginated endpoint that returns the saved events as `EventDto`s (the same DTO used by the map).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **paginated request DTO** (`SavedEventsQueryDto`).  
2️⃣ Define a **paginated response DTO** (`SavedEventsResponseDto`).  
3️⃣ Extend `ISaveService` (or add a new contract `ISavedEventsQueryService`) with a method `GetSavedEventsAsync(Guid userId, SavedEventsQueryDto query)`.  
4️⃣ Provide an **implementation** (`SavedEventsQueryService`) that joins `UserSavedEventEntity` with `EventAggregateEntity`, maps to `EventDto` using the existing `IEventMapper`.  
5️⃣ Add an **API controller** (`SavedEventsEndpoint`) exposing `GET /api/v1/saved?page=1&pageSize=20`.  
6️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** verifying pagination, correct ordering (most recent saved first), and that the `IsSaved` flag in the returned DTOs is always `true`.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Saves/Dto`)

# Context
The UI needs a **“Saved Events” panel** that lists all events a user has bookmarked, shows basic details (title, time, venue, saved flag) and allows unsaving directly from the list.  The back‑end must provide a paginated endpoint that returns the saved events as `EventDto`s (the same DTO used by the map).


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **paginated request DTO** (`SavedEventsQueryDto`).  
2️⃣ Define a **paginated response DTO** (`SavedEventsResponseDto`).  
3️⃣ Extend `ISaveService` (or add a new contract `ISavedEventsQueryService`) with a method `GetSavedEventsAsync(Guid userId, SavedEventsQueryDto query)`.  
4️⃣ Provide an **implementation** (`SavedEventsQueryService`) that joins `UserSavedEventEntity` with `EventAggregateEntity`, maps to `EventDto` using the existing `IEventMapper`.  
5️⃣ Add an **API controller** (`SavedEventsEndpoint`) exposing `GET /api/v1/saved?page=1&pageSize=20`.  
6️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** verifying pagination, correct ordering (most recent saved first), and that the `IsSaved` flag in the returned DTOs is always `true`.

Service Contract (place under WeUP.Domain/Saves)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Saves/ISavedEventsQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Saves
{
    public interface ISavedEventsQueryService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Returns a paginated list of the user's saved events, ordered by
        /// <c>SavedAtUtc</c> descending (most recent first).  The returned
        /// <see cref="EventDto"/> always has <c>IsSaved = true</c>.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SavedEventsResponseDto> GetSavedEventsAsync(Guid userId, SavedEventsQueryDto query);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Saves/ISavedEventsQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Saves
{
    public interface ISavedEventsQueryService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Returns a paginated list of the user's saved events, ordered by
        /// <c>SavedAtUtc</c> descending (most recent first).  The returned
        /// <see cref="EventDto"/> always has <c>IsSaved = true</c>.
        /// </summary>
        Task<SavedEventsResponseDto> GetSavedEventsAsync(Guid userId, SavedEventsQueryDto query);
    }
}


Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Saves/SavedEventsQueryService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Saves/SavedEventsQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events.Dto;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Events;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Saves
{
    public sealed class SavedEventsQueryService : ISavedEventsQueryService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly IEventMapper _mapper;
        private readonly ILogger<SavedEventsQueryService> _log;


        public SavedEventsQueryService(
            WeUpDbContext db,
            IEventMapper mapper,
            ILogger<SavedEventsQueryService> log)
        {
            _db = db;
            _mapper = mapper;
            _log = log;
        }


        public async Task<SavedEventsResponseDto> GetSavedEventsAsync(Guid userId, SavedEventsQueryDto query)
        {
            // Clamp page size to a reasonable maximum.
            int pageSize = Math.Min(query.PageSize, 100);
            int skip = (Math.Max(query.Page, 1) - 1) * pageSize;


            // Join saved rows with the canonical events.
            var queryable = from saved in _db.UserSavedEvents
                            join agg in _db.EventAggregates
                                on saved.EventId equals agg.EventId
                            where saved.UserId == userId
                            orderby saved.SavedAtUtc descending
                            select agg;


            var total = await queryable.CountAsync();


            var aggregates = await queryable
                .Skip(skip)
                .Take(pageSize)
                .ToListAsync();


            // Map each aggregate to the public DTO (IsSaved = true).
            var items = new List<EventDto>();
            foreach (var agg in aggregates)
            {
                var dto = await _mapper.ToDtoAsync(agg);
                // Override the IsSaved flag – the mapper does not know about the user.
                var savedDto = dto with { IsSaved = true };
                items.Add(savedDto);
            }


            var response = new SavedEventsResponseDto(
                Items: items,
                Page: query.Page,
                PageSize: pageSize,
                TotalCount: total);


            _log.LogInformation("Returned {Count}/{Total} saved events for User {UserId} (Page {Page})",
                                 items.Count, total, userId, query.Page);


            return response;
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------

API Controller (place under WeUP.Api/Endpoints/SavedEventsEndpoint.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/SavedEventsEndpoint.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Saves.Dto;
using WeUP.Domain.Saves;


namespace WeUP.Api.Endpoints;


[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/saved")]
[Authorize]
public sealed class SavedEventsEndpoint : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly ISavedEventsQueryService _query;
    private readonly ILogger<SavedEventsEndpoint> _log;


    public SavedEventsEndpoint(ISavedEventsQueryService query, ILogger<SavedEventsEndpoint> log)
    {
        _query = query;
        _log = log;
    }


    private Guid GetUserId()
    {
        var sub = User.FindFirst("sub")?.Value;
        return Guid.TryParse(sub, out var id) ? id : throw new InvalidOperationException("Invalid user claim.");
    }


    [HttpGet]
    public async Task<ActionResult<SavedEventsResponseDto>> Get([FromQuery] SavedEventsQueryDto query)
    {
        var userId = GetUserId();
        var result = await _query.GetSavedEventsAsync(userId, query);
        return Ok(result);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Phase0_AIStudio/backend/WeUP.Api/Endpoints/SavedEventsEndpoint.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The pagination must be 1‑based (Page = 1 = first page).
	Aligns with typical UI expectations.
	PageSize is capped at 100 to prevent accidental huge responses.
	Protects the API from abuse.
	The returned EventDto.IsSaved is forced to true regardless of the mapper's default.
	Guarantees UI consistency.
	The query orders by UserSavedEventEntity.SavedAtUtc descending – newest saved first.
	Natural UI ordering.
	The service is read‑only – no modifications to saves here; separate endpoints handle Save/Unsave (M7‑P31).
	Separation of concerns.
	All timestamps are in UTC.
	System‑wide time policy.
	The controller extracts the user ID from the JWT sub claim; anonymous requests are rejected.
	Security.
	Acceptance Criteria
* SavedEventsQueryDto, SavedEventsResponseDto, ISavedEventsQueryService, SavedEventsQueryService, and SavedEventsEndpoint compile.
* SavedEventsQueryService correctly:
   1. Counts total saved events (TotalCount).
   2. Applies pagination (skip, take).
   3. Returns EventDtos with IsSaved = true.
   4. Orders by saved timestamp descending.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Saves/SavedEventsQueryServiceTests.cs validates:
   1. Pagination – page 2 returns the correct slice.
   2. Total count matches the number of rows in the DB.
   3. All returned DTOs have IsSaved = true.
   4. Empty result when the user has no saved events.
* API endpoint returns 200 OK with the paginated DTO, respects page/pageSize query parameters.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ISavedEventsQueryService, SavedEventsQueryService>();.

M8‑P36 — Define Canonical Time‑Window Semantics v1.0
markdown
# Context
All temporal logic in WeUP (map feed, calendar, ingestion, moderation) must be based on a **single, versioned definition of a time window**.  The window is always expressed in UTC, but the *semantic* presets (Now, Tonight, ThisWeekend, Rolling windows, Custom) are market‑local concepts.


# Objective
1️⃣ Create an **enum** that lists the canonical presets (`TimeWindowPreset`).  
2️⃣ Create a **record** that represents a resolved UTC window (`ResolvedTimeWindow`).  
3️⃣ Create a **DTO** (`TemporalFilterDto`) that the API receives from the front‑end (preset + optional start/end for Custom).  
4️⃣ Place the contracts in the *Contracts* layer so that both back‑end services and front‑end TypeScript models can be generated from them.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Temporal`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/TimeWindowPreset.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


/// <summary>
/// Canonical, versioned temporal presets used across the system.
/// The enum names are the exact strings the front‑end will send
/// (case‑insensitive when parsed).
/// </summary>
public enum TimeWindowPreset
{
    /// <summary>Current hour (rounded down to the hour).</summary>
    Now,


    /// <summary>Today 6 pm → midnight (local market time).</summary>
    Tonight,


    /// <summary>Saturday 00:00 → Sunday 23:59 (local market time).</summary>
    ThisWeekend,


    /// <summary>Now → now + 24 h.</summary>
    Next24Hours,


    /// <summary>Now → now + 48 h.</summary>
    Next48Hours,


    /// <summary>Explicit start / end supplied by the caller.</summary>
    Custom
}


# Context
All temporal logic in WeUP (map feed, calendar, ingestion, moderation) must be based on a **single, versioned definition of a time window**.  The window is always expressed in UTC, but the *semantic* presets (Now, Tonight, ThisWeekend, Rolling windows, Custom) are market‑local concepts.


# Objective
1️⃣ Create an **enum** that lists the canonical presets (`TimeWindowPreset`).  
2️⃣ Create a **record** that represents a resolved UTC window (`ResolvedTimeWindow`).  
3️⃣ Create a **DTO** (`TemporalFilterDto`) that the API receives from the front‑end (preset + optional start/end for Custom).  
4️⃣ Place the contracts in the *Contracts* layer so that both back‑end services and front‑end TypeScript models can be generated from them.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Temporal`)

using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


/// <summary>
/// Concrete UTC window produced by the resolver.  All downstream services
/// must consume this immutable record (no time‑zone information attached).
/// </summary>
public sealed record ResolvedTimeWindow(
    DateTimeOffset StartUtc,
    DateTimeOffset EndUtc);

    DateTimeOffset StartUtc,

csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/TemporalFilterDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


/// <summary>
/// Input contract used by the API to request a window.  If <see cref="Preset"/>
/// is <c>Custom</c>, <see cref="CustomStartUtc"/> and <see cref="CustomEndUtc"/>
/// must be supplied; otherwise they are ignored and the preset is resolved
/// using market‑local rules (see M8‑P37).
/// </summary>
public sealed record TemporalFilterDto(
    TimeWindowPreset Preset,
    DateTimeOffset? CustomStartUtc = null,
    DateTimeOffset? CustomEndUtc   = null);


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/TemporalFilterDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


/// <summary>
/// Input contract used by the API to request a window.  If <see cref="Preset"/>
/// is <c>Custom</c>, <see cref="CustomStartUtc"/> and <see cref="CustomEndUtc"/>
/// must be supplied; otherwise they are ignored and the preset is resolved
/// using market‑local rules (see M8‑P37).
/// </summary>
public sealed record TemporalFilterDto(
    TimeWindowPreset Preset,
    DateTimeOffset? CustomStartUtc = null,
    DateTimeOffset? CustomEndUtc   = null);


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Why
	All enum values are stable – never rename or reorder; only add new members (future versions get a new DTO file).
	Guarantees backward compatibility.
	ResolvedTimeWindow.StartUtc must be ≤ EndUtc. The resolver will enforce this.
	Prevents invalid windows downstream.
	The DTO is immutable (record) – no setters.
	Guarantees that once deserialized the data cannot be mutated.
	Custom preset requires both CustomStartUtc and CustomEndUtc (otherwise the resolver throws ArgumentException).
	Defensive programming.
	All timestamps are UTC (DateTimeOffset with Offset = TimeSpan.Zero).
	Uniform time handling across the system.
	Acceptance Criteria
* TimeWindowPreset, ResolvedTimeWindow, and TemporalFilterDto compile under WeUP.Contracts/Temporal.
* The enum contains exactly the six members shown, each with XML doc comments.
* No additional fields (e.g., timezone) are part of the DTO – the resolver adds them.
* Unit‑test placeholder can be added later (WeUP.Tests/Temporal/TemporalContractsTests.cs) that verifies JSON serialization round‑trip for each enum value.

M8‑P37 — Implement “Tonight”, “Weekend”, and Rolling Window Logic v1.0
markdown
# Context
The *preset* values defined in M8‑P36 need concrete, deterministic logic that converts them into UTC start/end timestamps **according to the market’s local time zone** (Houston = America/Chicago).  The implementation must handle DST transitions, midnight roll‑over, and edge‑cases (e.g., “Tonight” after 23:30 should still resolve to the same night).


# Objective
1️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ITemporalWindowResolver`).  
2️⃣ Provide a **pure implementation** (`TemporalWindowResolver`) that:
   - Uses `TimeZoneInfo` for the market (hard‑coded to *America/Chicago* for MVP).  
   - Resolves every `TimeWindowPreset` to a `ResolvedTimeWindow` as per the definitions.  
   - Handles DST correctly (by using `TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime` both ways).  
   - Throws `ArgumentException` for malformed `TemporalFilterDto` (e.g. missing custom bounds).  
3️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that checks each preset for a known fixed “now” (using a deterministic `IClock` abstraction to freeze time).  


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Domain/Temporal`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Temporal/ITemporalWindowResolver.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Temporal
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that resolves a <see cref="TemporalFilterDto"/> into a concrete
    /// <see cref="ResolvedTimeWindow"/> expressed in UTC.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ITemporalWindowResolver
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Resolve the supplied <paramref name="filter"/> into UTC bounds.
        /// </summary>
        Task<ResolvedTimeWindow> ResolveAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter);
    }
}


# Context
The *preset* values defined in M8‑P36 need concrete, deterministic logic that converts them into UTC start/end timestamps **according to the market’s local time zone** (Houston = America/Chicago).  The implementation must handle DST transitions, midnight roll‑over, and edge‑cases (e.g., “Tonight” after 23:30 should still resolve to the same night).


# Objective
1️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ITemporalWindowResolver`).  
2️⃣ Provide a **pure implementation** (`TemporalWindowResolver`) that:
   - Uses `TimeZoneInfo` for the market (hard‑coded to *America/Chicago* for MVP).  
   - Resolves every `TimeWindowPreset` to a `ResolvedTimeWindow` as per the definitions.  
   - Handles DST correctly (by using `TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime` both ways).  
   - Throws `ArgumentException` for malformed `TemporalFilterDto` (e.g. missing custom bounds).  
3️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that checks each preset for a known fixed “now” (using a deterministic `IClock` abstraction to freeze time).  


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Domain/Temporal`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Temporal/ITemporalWindowResolver.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Temporal
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that resolves a <see cref="TemporalFilterDto"/> into a concrete
    /// <see cref="ResolvedTimeWindow"/> expressed in UTC.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ITemporalWindowResolver
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Resolve the supplied <paramref name="filter"/> into UTC bounds.
        /// </summary>
        Task<ResolvedTimeWindow> ResolveAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter);
    }
}


Reference Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalWindowResolver.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalWindowResolver.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Deterministic resolver for temporal presets.  All calculations are
    /// performed in the *market* time zone (America/Chicago) and the result
    /// is converted back to UTC.
    ///
    /// The class receives an <see cref="IClock"/> abstraction so that unit
    /// tests can freeze time.  In production the default clock returns
    /// <c>DateTimeOffset.UtcNow</c>.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class TemporalWindowResolver : ITemporalWindowResolver
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalWindowResolver> _log;
        private readonly IClock _clock;


        // Market time zone – Houston (Central Time).  Use the IANA / Windows identifier that works on the host OS.
        private static readonly TimeZoneInfo MarketTz = RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform(OSPlatform.Windows)
            ? TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("Central Standard Time")      // Windows identifier
            : TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("America/Chicago");          // IANA identifier


        public TemporalWindowResolver(ILogger<TemporalWindowResolver> log, IClock? clock = null)
        {
            _log = log;
            _clock = clock ?? new SystemClock();   // SystemClock implements IClock → returns UtcNow
        }


        public Task<ResolvedTimeWindow> ResolveAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Convert “now” to the market’s local time (preserves DST).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var nowUtc = _clock.UtcNow;
            var nowLocal = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(nowUtc, MarketTz);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Resolve based on the preset.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            DateTimeOffset startLocal, endLocal;


            switch (filter.Preset)
            {
                case TimeWindowPreset.Now:
                    // Current hour, truncated (e.g., 14:37 → 14:00)
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(
                        nowLocal.Year,
                        nowLocal.Month,
                        nowLocal.Day,
                        nowLocal.Hour,
                        0,
                        0,
                        nowLocal.Offset);
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(1);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Tonight:
                    // Tonight = today 18:00 → 23:59:59 (local)
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(
                        nowLocal.Year,
                        nowLocal.Month,
                        nowLocal.Day,
                        18, 0, 0, nowLocal.Offset);
                    // End at the *end* of the same day (23:59:59.999)
                    endLocal = startLocal
                        .AddHours(5)                 // 18 → 23
                        .AddMinutes(59)
                        .AddSeconds(59)
                        .AddMilliseconds(999);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.ThisWeekend:
                    // Find the upcoming Saturday (or today if already Saturday)
                    int daysToSat = ((int)DayOfWeek.Saturday - (int)nowLocal.DayOfWeek + 7) % 7;
                    var satDate = nowLocal.Date.AddDays(daysToSat);
                    var sunDate = satDate.AddDays(1);
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(satDate, nowLocal.Offset); // 00:00 Saturday
                    // End at 23:59:59.999 on Sunday
                    endLocal = new DateTimeOffset(sunDate.AddDays(1).AddMilliseconds(-1), nowLocal.Offset);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Next24Hours:
                    startLocal = nowLocal;
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(24);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Next48Hours:
                    startLocal = nowLocal;
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(48);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Custom:
                    if (!filter.CustomStartUtc.HasValue || !filter.CustomEndUtc.HasValue)
                        throw new ArgumentException("Custom preset requires both CustomStartUtc and CustomEndUtc.");


                    // Custom values are already UTC – we just forward them after validation.
                    var customStart = filter.CustomStartUtc.Value;
                    var customEnd   = filter.CustomEndUtc.Value;


                    if (customStart >= customEnd)
                        throw new ArgumentException("CustomStartUtc must be before CustomEndUtc.");


                    // Convert to market local just to verify it is a sensible range, then back to UTC.
                    startLocal = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(customStart, MarketTz);
                    endLocal   = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(customEnd,   MarketTz);
                    break;


                default:
                    throw new NotSupportedException($"Unsupported preset: {filter.Preset}");
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Convert the resolved local timestamps back to UTC.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var startUtc = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(startLocal, TimeZoneInfo.Utc);
            var endUtc   = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(endLocal,   TimeZoneInfo.Utc);


            // Defensive sanity check.
            if (startUtc >= endUtc)
                throw new InvalidOperationException("Resolved time window is invalid (Start >= End).");


            _log.LogDebug("Temporal window resolved: Preset={Preset}, StartUtc={Start:u}, EndUtc={End:u}",
                           filter.Preset, startUtc, endUtc);


            return Task.FromResult(new ResolvedTimeWindow(startUtc, endUtc));
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Very small clock abstraction – makes the resolver testable.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public interface IClock
    {
        DateTimeOffset UtcNow { get; }
    }


    public sealed class SystemClock : IClock
    {
        public DateTimeOffset UtcNow => DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalWindowResolver.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Deterministic resolver for temporal presets.  All calculations are
    /// performed in the *market* time zone (America/Chicago) and the result
    /// is converted back to UTC.
    ///
    /// The class receives an <see cref="IClock"/> abstraction so that unit
    /// tests can freeze time.  In production the default clock returns
    /// <c>DateTimeOffset.UtcNow</c>.
    /// </summary>
    public sealed class TemporalWindowResolver : ITemporalWindowResolver
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalWindowResolver> _log;
        private readonly IClock _clock;


        // Market time zone – Houston (Central Time).  Use the IANA / Windows identifier that works on the host OS.
        private static readonly TimeZoneInfo MarketTz = RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform(OSPlatform.Windows)
            ? TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("Central Standard Time")      // Windows identifier
            : TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("America/Chicago");          // IANA identifier


        public TemporalWindowResolver(ILogger<TemporalWindowResolver> log, IClock? clock = null)
        {
            _log = log;
            _clock = clock ?? new SystemClock();   // SystemClock implements IClock → returns UtcNow
        }


        public Task<ResolvedTimeWindow> ResolveAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Convert “now” to the market’s local time (preserves DST).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var nowUtc = _clock.UtcNow;
            var nowLocal = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(nowUtc, MarketTz);


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Resolve based on the preset.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            DateTimeOffset startLocal, endLocal;


            switch (filter.Preset)
            {
                case TimeWindowPreset.Now:
                    // Current hour, truncated (e.g., 14:37 → 14:00)
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(
                        nowLocal.Year,
                        nowLocal.Month,
                        nowLocal.Day,
                        nowLocal.Hour,
                        0,
                        0,
                        nowLocal.Offset);
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(1);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Tonight:
                    // Tonight = today 18:00 → 23:59:59 (local)
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(
                        nowLocal.Year,
                        nowLocal.Month,
                        nowLocal.Day,
                        18, 0, 0, nowLocal.Offset);
                    // End at the *end* of the same day (23:59:59.999)
                    endLocal = startLocal
                        .AddHours(5)                 // 18 → 23
                        .AddMinutes(59)
                        .AddSeconds(59)
                        .AddMilliseconds(999);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.ThisWeekend:
                    // Find the upcoming Saturday (or today if already Saturday)
                    int daysToSat = ((int)DayOfWeek.Saturday - (int)nowLocal.DayOfWeek + 7) % 7;
                    var satDate = nowLocal.Date.AddDays(daysToSat);
                    var sunDate = satDate.AddDays(1);
                    startLocal = new DateTimeOffset(satDate, nowLocal.Offset); // 00:00 Saturday
                    // End at 23:59:59.999 on Sunday
                    endLocal = new DateTimeOffset(sunDate.AddDays(1).AddMilliseconds(-1), nowLocal.Offset);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Next24Hours:
                    startLocal = nowLocal;
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(24);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Next48Hours:
                    startLocal = nowLocal;
                    endLocal   = startLocal.AddHours(48);
                    break;


                case TimeWindowPreset.Custom:
                    if (!filter.CustomStartUtc.HasValue || !filter.CustomEndUtc.HasValue)
                        throw new ArgumentException("Custom preset requires both CustomStartUtc and CustomEndUtc.");


                    // Custom values are already UTC – we just forward them after validation.
                    var customStart = filter.CustomStartUtc.Value;
                    var customEnd   = filter.CustomEndUtc.Value;


                    if (customStart >= customEnd)
                        throw new ArgumentException("CustomStartUtc must be before CustomEndUtc.");


                    // Convert to market local just to verify it is a sensible range, then back to UTC.
                    startLocal = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(customStart, MarketTz);
                    endLocal   = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(customEnd,   MarketTz);
                    break;


                default:
                    throw new NotSupportedException($"Unsupported preset: {filter.Preset}");
            }


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Convert the resolved local timestamps back to UTC.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var startUtc = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(startLocal, TimeZoneInfo.Utc);
            var endUtc   = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(endLocal,   TimeZoneInfo.Utc);


            // Defensive sanity check.
            if (startUtc >= endUtc)
                throw new InvalidOperationException("Resolved time window is invalid (Start >= End).");


            _log.LogDebug("Temporal window resolved: Preset={Preset}, StartUtc={Start:u}, EndUtc={End:u}",
                           filter.Preset, startUtc, endUtc);


            return Task.FromResult(new ResolvedTimeWindow(startUtc, endUtc));
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Very small clock abstraction – makes the resolver testable.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public interface IClock
    {
        DateTimeOffset UtcNow { get; }
    }


    public sealed class SystemClock : IClock
    {
        public DateTimeOffset UtcNow => DateTimeOffset.UtcNow;
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The resolver must not perform any I/O (no DB, no network). All calculations are pure arithmetic using TimeZoneInfo.
	Guarantees determinism for unit tests.
	All results are UTC (DateTimeOffset with Offset = TimeSpan.Zero).
	Aligns with system‑wide time policy.
	DST transitions are handled automatically by TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime.
	Prevents off‑by‑hour bugs on the spring/fall change.
	For Custom the service validates that CustomStartUtc < CustomEndUtc; otherwise it throws ArgumentException.
	Early failure on malformed input.
	The market time zone is hard‑coded to “America/Chicago”. If the business ever needs a different market, only this constant changes.
	Centralises the market definition.
	The service logs the resolved window at Debug level (helps troubleshooting).
	Observability.
	Acceptance Criteria
* ITemporalWindowResolver interface compiles.
* TemporalWindowResolver implements the contract, resolves all six presets according to the definitions in this block, handles DST, and returns a ResolvedTimeWindow with StartUtc < EndUtc.
* The implementation uses an IClock abstraction (default = SystemClock).
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Temporal/TemporalWindowResolverTests.cs (using a mock IClock set to a fixed point, e.g., 2025‑03‑13 12:34 UTC) validates:
   1. Now → start = 12:00 UTC (hour‑truncated) and end = 13:00 UTC (adjusted for market offset).
   2. Tonight → correct 18:00 local to midnight‑local conversion, then to UTC (including DST).
   3. ThisWeekend → Saturday‑Sunday window in UTC.
   4. Next24Hours and Next48Hours → exact hour spans from the frozen “now”.
   5. Custom → passes through when start < end, throws when missing or reversed.
* The resolver is registered in DI: services.AddTransient<ITemporalWindowResolver, TemporalWindowResolver>();.
________________


M8‑P38 — Normalize Timezone Handling Across System Layers v1.0
markdown
# Context
Various layers (ingestion timestamp parsing, UI date‑pickers, map feed queries, calendar UI) all need to **convert between the market’s local time zone and UTC** consistently.  Duplicated conversion logic leads to drift and hard‑to‑debug bugs.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **timezone helper contract** (`ITimezoneAdapter`) that provides:  
   - `ToMarketLocal(DateTimeOffset utc) → DateTimeOffset`  
   - `ToUtc(DateTimeOffset marketLocal) → DateTimeOffset`  
2️⃣ Provide a **concrete implementation** (`TimezoneAdapter`) that uses the same market TZ (`America/Chicago`) and caches the `TimeZoneInfo` instance.  
3️⃣ Extend the **existing TemporalWindowResolver** to depend on this adapter (instead of directly calling `TimeZoneInfo`).  This enforces a single source of truth for the market TZ.  
4️⃣ Add a **utility extension method** (`DateTimeOffsetExtensions`) for easy conversion in any layer (e.g., `utc.ToMarketLocal()`), implemented once in the `WeUP.Domain` layer.  
5️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** that proves round‑trip conversion works for a date that falls on a DST transition (spring forward/backward).


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Domain/Timezone`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Timezone/ITimezoneAdapter.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Timezone
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Centralised adapter for converting between UTC and the market’s
    /// local time zone (America/Chicago for MVP).  All services must
    /// use this adapter to guarantee consistent behaviour.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ITimezoneAdapter
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Convert a UTC timestamp to market‑local time (preserving the instant).
        /// </summary>
        DateTimeOffset ToMarketLocal(DateTimeOffset utc);


        /// <summary>
        /// Convert a market‑local timestamp back to UTC.
        /// </summary>
        DateTimeOffset ToUtc(DateTimeOffset marketLocal);
    }
}


# Context
Various layers (ingestion timestamp parsing, UI date‑pickers, map feed queries, calendar UI) all need to **convert between the market’s local time zone and UTC** consistently.  Duplicated conversion logic leads to drift and hard‑to‑debug bugs.


# Objective

   - `ToUtc(DateTimeOffset marketLocal) → DateTimeOffset`  
2️⃣ Provide a **concrete implementation** (`TimezoneAdapter`) that uses the same market TZ (`America/Chicago`) and caches the `TimeZoneInfo` instance.  
3️⃣ Extend the **existing TemporalWindowResolver** to depend on this adapter (instead of directly calling `TimeZoneInfo`).  This enforces a single source of truth for the market TZ.  
4️⃣ Add a **utility extension method** (`DateTimeOffsetExtensions`) for easy conversion in any layer (e.g., `utc.ToMarketLocal()`), implemented once in the `WeUP.Domain` layer.  
5️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** that proves round‑trip conversion works for a date that falls on a DST transition (spring forward/backward).

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Timezone/TimezoneAdapter.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Timezone/TimezoneAdapter.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Domain.Timezone;


namespace WeUP.Application.Timezone
{
    public sealed class TimezoneAdapter : ITimezoneAdapter
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TimezoneAdapter> _log;
        // Market time zone – cached for performance.
        private static readonly TimeZoneInfo MarketTz = RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform(OSPlatform.Windows)
            ? TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("Central Standard Time")
            : TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("America/Chicago");


        public TimezoneAdapter(ILogger<TimezoneAdapter> log) => _log = log;


        public DateTimeOffset ToMarketLocal(DateTimeOffset utc)
        {
            var local = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(utc, MarketTz);
            _log?.LogDebug("Converted UTC {Utc} → MarketLocal {Local}", utc, local);
            return local;
        }


        public DateTimeOffset ToUtc(DateTimeOffset marketLocal)
        {
            var utc = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(marketLocal, TimeZoneInfo.Utc);
            _log?.LogDebug("Converted MarketLocal {Local} → UTC {Utc}", marketLocal, utc);
            return utc;
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension methods – convenient for any code‑base.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class DateTimeOffsetExtensions
    {
        public static DateTimeOffset ToMarketLocal(this DateTimeOffset utc, ITimezoneAdapter adapter) =>
            adapter.ToMarketLocal(utc);


        public static DateTimeOffset ToUtc(this DateTimeOffset marketLocal, ITimezoneAdapter adapter) =>
            adapter.ToUtc(marketLocal);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Timezone/TimezoneAdapter.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Domain.Timezone;


namespace WeUP.Application.Timezone
{
    public sealed class TimezoneAdapter : ITimezoneAdapter
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TimezoneAdapter> _log;
        // Market time zone – cached for performance.
        private static readonly TimeZoneInfo MarketTz = RuntimeInformation.IsOSPlatform(OSPlatform.Windows)
            ? TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("Central Standard Time")
            : TimeZoneInfo.FindSystemTimeZoneById("America/Chicago");


        public TimezoneAdapter(ILogger<TimezoneAdapter> log) => _log = log;


        public DateTimeOffset ToMarketLocal(DateTimeOffset utc)
        {
            var local = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(utc, MarketTz);
            _log?.LogDebug("Converted UTC {Utc} → MarketLocal {Local}", utc, local);
            return local;
        }


        public DateTimeOffset ToUtc(DateTimeOffset marketLocal)
        {
            var utc = TimeZoneInfo.ConvertTime(marketLocal, TimeZoneInfo.Utc);
            _log?.LogDebug("Converted MarketLocal {Local} → UTC {Utc}", marketLocal, utc);
            return utc;
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension methods – convenient for any code‑base.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class DateTimeOffsetExtensions
    {
        public static DateTimeOffset ToMarketLocal(this DateTimeOffset utc, ITimezoneAdapter adapter) =>
            adapter.ToMarketLocal(utc);


        public static DateTimeOffset ToUtc(this DateTimeOffset marketLocal, ITimezoneAdapter adapter) =>
            adapter.ToUtc(marketLocal);
    }
}

csharp
// In TemporalWindowResolver.cs (replace direct TimeZoneInfo calls):

public TemporalWindowResolver(ILogger<TemporalWindowResolver> log, IClock? clock = null, ITimezoneAdapter? tzAdapter = null)
{
    _log = log;
    _clock = clock ?? new SystemClock();
    _tzAdapter = tzAdapter ?? new TimezoneAdapter(logFactory); // resolve via DI

var endLocal   = customEnd.ToMarketLocal(_tzAdapter);

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All conversion must go through ITimezoneAdapter – no direct TimeZoneInfo calls elsewhere.
	Guarantees a single source of truth for the market TZ.
	The adapter is stateless and thread‑safe (caches a readonly TimeZoneInfo).
	Safe for DI singleton usage.
	Extension methods (ToMarketLocal, ToUtc) are optional helpers; they must receive the adapter instance (no hidden static state).
	Prevents hidden dependencies.
	All services that need time‑zone conversion (e.g., TemporalWindowResolver, map‑feed throttling, ingestion timestamp normalisation) must be updated to use the adapter.
	System‑wide consistency.
	The adapter logs conversions at Debug level (useful for troubleshooting).
	Observability.
	Acceptance Criteria
* ITimezoneAdapter and TimezoneAdapter compile under the indicated namespaces.
* TimezoneAdapter correctly converts UTC ↔ market‑local for dates that fall on normal days, DST start, and DST end (unit‑test verifies a spring‑forward date, e.g., 2024‑03‑10 02:30 UTC → 20:30 local, and back).
* Extension methods compile and delegate to the injected adapter.
* TemporalWindowResolver now depends on ITimezoneAdapter and uses it for all conversions.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Timezone/TimezoneAdapterTests.cs covers round‑trip conversion and DST edge cases.
* DI registration: services.AddSingleton<ITimezoneAdapter, TimezoneAdapter>();.
