M8‑P39 — Synchronize Temporal Logic Between Front‑End and Back‑End v1.0
markdown
# Context
The front‑end (Vue 3) already ships a `TemporalFilterDto` TypeScript interface generated from the C# contract (M8‑P36).  However, the front‑end also performs its own **preset calculations** (e.g., `Tonight` → local 6 pm–midnight) for immediate UI feedback.  To avoid drift we must **share the same algorithm** between front‑end and back‑end.


# Objective
1️⃣ Export the **same preset logic** to a JavaScript/TypeScript module (`temporalResolver.ts`).  
2️⃣ Make the module **import the compiled C# enum** via a generated type definition (`@types/weup-temporal` generated from the C# `TimeWindowPreset`).  
3️⃣ Provide a **wrapper function** `resolveTemporalWindow(preset: TimeWindowPreset, customStart?: Date, customEnd?: Date): ResolvedTimeWindow` that mirrors the C# resolver (using the same market TZ and DST handling).  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** in the front‑end (`temporalResolver.spec.ts`) that checks the same edge cases as the back‑end tests (e.g., “Tonight” on a DST‑transition day).  
5️⃣ Document the **contract‑sync process** (run `npm run generate-contracts` to copy the C# enum to TypeScript).


# Front‑End Type Definitions (place under `frontend‑vue/src/types/temporal.d.ts`)


```typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: frontend-vue/src/types/temporal.d.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------


declare module 'weup-temporal' {
  export enum TimeWindowPreset {
    Now = 'Now',
    Tonight = 'Tonight',
    ThisWeekend = 'ThisWeekend',
    Next24Hours = 'Next24Hours',
    Next48Hours = 'Next48Hours',
    Custom = 'Custom',
  }


  /** Same shape as the C# ResolvedTimeWindow (UTC). */
  export interface ResolvedTimeWindow {
    startUtc: string;   // ISO‑8601 string (always Z)
    endUtc: string;    // ISO‑8601 string (always Z)
  }
}


# Context
The front‑end (Vue 3) already ships a `TemporalFilterDto` TypeScript interface generated from the C# contract (M8‑P36).  However, the front‑end also performs its own **preset calculations** (e.g., `Tonight` → local 6 pm–midnight) for immediate UI feedback.  To avoid drift we must **share the same algorithm** between front‑end and back‑end.


# Objective
1️⃣ Export the **same preset logic** to a JavaScript/TypeScript module (`temporalResolver.ts`).  
2️⃣ Make the module **import the compiled C# enum** via a generated type definition (`@types/weup-temporal` generated from the C# `TimeWindowPreset`).  
3️⃣ Provide a **wrapper function** `resolveTemporalWindow(preset: TimeWindowPreset, customStart?: Date, customEnd?: Date): ResolvedTimeWindow` that mirrors the C# resolver (using the same market TZ and DST handling).  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** in the front‑end (`temporalResolver.spec.ts`) that checks the same edge cases as the back‑end tests (e.g., “Tonight” on a DST‑transition day).  
5️⃣ Document the **contract‑sync process** (run `npm run generate-contracts` to copy the C# enum to TypeScript).


# Front‑End Type Definitions (place under `frontend‑vue/src/types/temporal.d.ts`)


```typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: frontend-vue/src/types/temporal.d.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------


declare module 'weup-temporal' {
  export enum TimeWindowPreset {
    Now = 'Now',
    Tonight = 'Tonight',
    ThisWeekend = 'ThisWeekend',
    Next24Hours = 'Next24Hours',
    Next48Hours = 'Next48Hours',
    Custom = 'Custom',
  }


  /** Same shape as the C# ResolvedTimeWindow (UTC). */
  export interface ResolvedTimeWindow {
    startUtc: string;   // ISO‑8601 string (always Z)
    endUtc: string;    // ISO‑8601 string (always Z)
  }
}


Temporal Resolver (place under frontend‑vue/src/lib/temporalResolver.ts)
typescript
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: frontend-vue/src/lib/temporalResolver.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------
import { TimeWindowPreset, ResolvedTimeWindow } from 'weup-temporal';


/**
 * Market time zone identifier for Houston (America/Chicago).
 * In the browser we use the Intl API to apply DST rules.
 */
const MARKET_TIMEZONE = 'America/Chicago';


/**
 * Convert a UTC Date to the market’s local Date (preserves the instant).
 */
function utcToMarketLocal(utc: Date): Date {
  // Intl.DateTimeFormat can be used to get the offset for a given time zone.
  const tz = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {
    timeZone: MARKET_TIMEZONE,
    hour12: false,
  });
  const parts = tz.formatToParts(utc);
  // Re‑assemble a Date that has the same wall‑clock values as the market time.
  const year   = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'year')!.value);
  const month  = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'month')!.value) - 1; // 0‑based
  const day    = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'day')!.value);
  const hour   = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'hour')!.value);
  const minute = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'minute')!.value);
  const second = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'second')!.value);
  return new Date(Date.UTC(year, month, day, hour, minute, second));
}


/**
 * Convert a market‑local Date back to UTC.
 */
function marketLocalToUtc(local: Date): Date {
  // The reverse of utcToMarketLocal – we create a Date as if it were in the market
  // time zone, then ask the browser for its UTC representation.
  const iso = `${local.getUTCFullYear()}-${pad(local.getUTCMonth() + 1)}-${pad(local.getUTCDate())
    }T${pad(local.getUTCHours())}:${pad(local.getUTCMinutes())}:${pad(local.getUTCSeconds())}.000Z`;
  return new Date(iso);
}


function pad(n: number): string => n.toString().padStart(2, '0');


/**
 * Resolve a preset (or custom) into a UTC window.
 * Mirrors the C# TemporalWindowResolver logic.
 */
export function resolveTemporalWindow(
  preset: TimeWindowPreset,
  customStart?: Date,
  customEnd?: Date
): ResolvedTimeWindow {
  const nowUtc = new Date();               // current instant (UTC)
  const nowLocal = utcToMarketLocal(nowUtc); // market‑local representation


  let startLocal: Date;
  let endLocal: Date;


  switch (preset) {
    case TimeWindowPreset.Now:
      startLocal = new Date(nowLocal);
      startLocal.setMinutes(0, 0, 0);                // truncate minutes
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal);
      endLocal.setHours(endLocal.getHours() + 1);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Tonight:
      startLocal = new Date(nowLocal);
      startLocal.setHours(18, 0, 0, 0);               // 18:00 local
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal);
      endLocal.setHours(23, 59, 59, 999);           // end of day
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.ThisWeekend: {
      const dayOfWeek = nowLocal.getUTCDay(); // 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday
      const daysToSaturday = (6 - dayOfWeek + 7) % 7;
      const sat = new Date(nowLocal);
      sat.setDate(sat.getUTCDate() + daysToSaturday);
      sat.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);
      startLocal = sat;


      const sun = new Date(sat);
      sun.setDate(sat.getUTCDate() + 2); // Move to Monday 00:00 then subtract 1 ms
      endLocal = new Date(sun.getTime() - 1);
      break;
    }


    case TimeWindowPreset.Next24Hours:
      startLocal = nowLocal;
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal.getTime() + 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Next48Hours:
      startLocal = nowLocal;
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal.getTime() + 48 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Custom:
      if (!customStart || !customEnd) {
        throw new Error('Custom preset requires both start and end dates.');
      }
      // Custom inputs are already UTC – just use them.
      return {
        startUtc: customStart.toISOString(),
        endUtc: customEnd.toISOString(),
      };


    default:
      throw new Error(`Unsupported preset: ${preset}`);
  }


  // Convert the local window back to UTC.
  const startUtc = marketLocalToUtc(startLocal);
  const endUtc   = marketLocalToUtc(endLocal);


  if (startUtc >= endUtc) {
    throw new Error('Resolved window is invalid (start >= end).');
  }


  return {
    startUtc: startUtc.toISOString(),
    endUtc: endUtc.toISOString(),
  };
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: frontend-vue/src/lib/temporalResolver.ts
// ------------------------------------------------------------
import { TimeWindowPreset, ResolvedTimeWindow } from 'weup-temporal';


/**
 * Market time zone identifier for Houston (America/Chicago).
 * In the browser we use the Intl API to apply DST rules.
 */
const MARKET_TIMEZONE = 'America/Chicago';


/**
 * Convert a UTC Date to the market’s local Date (preserves the instant).
 */
function utcToMarketLocal(utc: Date): Date {
  // Intl.DateTimeFormat can be used to get the offset for a given time zone.
  const tz = new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', {
    timeZone: MARKET_TIMEZONE,
    hour12: false,
  });
  const parts = tz.formatToParts(utc);
  // Re‑assemble a Date that has the same wall‑clock values as the market time.
  const year   = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'year')!.value);
  const month  = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'month')!.value) - 1; // 0‑based
  const day    = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'day')!.value);
  const hour   = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'hour')!.value);
  const minute = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'minute')!.value);
  const second = Number(parts.find(p => p.type === 'second')!.value);
  return new Date(Date.UTC(year, month, day, hour, minute, second));
}


/**
 * Convert a market‑local Date back to UTC.
 */
function marketLocalToUtc(local: Date): Date {
  // The reverse of utcToMarketLocal – we create a Date as if it were in the market
  // time zone, then ask the browser for its UTC representation.
  const iso = `${local.getUTCFullYear()}-${pad(local.getUTCMonth() + 1)}-${pad(local.getUTCDate())
    }T${pad(local.getUTCHours())}:${pad(local.getUTCMinutes())}:${pad(local.getUTCSeconds())}.000Z`;
  return new Date(iso);
}


function pad(n: number): string => n.toString().padStart(2, '0');


/**
 * Resolve a preset (or custom) into a UTC window.
 * Mirrors the C# TemporalWindowResolver logic.
 */
export function resolveTemporalWindow(
  preset: TimeWindowPreset,
  customStart?: Date,
  customEnd?: Date
): ResolvedTimeWindow {
  const nowUtc = new Date();               // current instant (UTC)
  const nowLocal = utcToMarketLocal(nowUtc); // market‑local representation


  let startLocal: Date;
  let endLocal: Date;


  switch (preset) {
    case TimeWindowPreset.Now:
      startLocal = new Date(nowLocal);
      startLocal.setMinutes(0, 0, 0);                // truncate minutes
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal);
      endLocal.setHours(endLocal.getHours() + 1);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Tonight:
      startLocal = new Date(nowLocal);
      startLocal.setHours(18, 0, 0, 0);               // 18:00 local
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal);
      endLocal.setHours(23, 59, 59, 999);           // end of day
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.ThisWeekend: {
      const dayOfWeek = nowLocal.getUTCDay(); // 0 = Sunday … 6 = Saturday
      const daysToSaturday = (6 - dayOfWeek + 7) % 7;
      const sat = new Date(nowLocal);
      sat.setDate(sat.getUTCDate() + daysToSaturday);
      sat.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);
      startLocal = sat;


      const sun = new Date(sat);
      sun.setDate(sat.getUTCDate() + 2); // Move to Monday 00:00 then subtract 1 ms
      endLocal = new Date(sun.getTime() - 1);
      break;
    }


    case TimeWindowPreset.Next24Hours:
      startLocal = nowLocal;
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal.getTime() + 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Next48Hours:
      startLocal = nowLocal;
      endLocal = new Date(startLocal.getTime() + 48 * 60 * 60 * 1000);
      break;


    case TimeWindowPreset.Custom:
      if (!customStart || !customEnd) {
        throw new Error('Custom preset requires both start and end dates.');
      }
      // Custom inputs are already UTC – just use them.
      return {
        startUtc: customStart.toISOString(),
        endUtc: customEnd.toISOString(),
      };


    default:
      throw new Error(`Unsupported preset: ${preset}`);
  }


  // Convert the local window back to UTC.
  const startUtc = marketLocalToUtc(startLocal);
  const endUtc   = marketLocalToUtc(endLocal);


  if (startUtc >= endUtc) {
    throw new Error('Resolved window is invalid (start >= end).');
  }


  return {
    startUtc: startUtc.toISOString(),
    endUtc: endUtc.toISOString(),
  };
}


Unit‑Test (place under frontend‑vue/tests/unit/temporalResolver.spec.ts)
typescript
import { TimeWindowPreset } from 'weup-temporal';
import { resolveTemporalWindow } from '@/lib/temporalResolver';


describe('TemporalResolver (frontend)', () => {
  // Freeze time using jest‑date‑mock or similar.  For illustration we set a fixed date.
  const FIXED_NOW = new Date('2025-03-13T12:34:00Z'); // DST is active in Chicago


  beforeAll(() => {
    jest.useFakeTimers();
    jest.setSystemTime(FIXED_NOW);
  });


  afterAll(() => {
    jest.useRealTimers();
  });


  it('resolves Now correctly', () => {
    const result = resolveTemporalWindow(TimeWindowPreset.Now);
    // 2025‑03‑13 12:34 UTC = 07:34 local (CST/CDT offset = -5h)
    // Truncated hour = 07:00 local => 12:00 UTC
    expect(result.startUtc).toBe('2025-03-13T12:00:00.000Z');
    expect(result.endUtc).toBe('2025-03-13T13:00:00.000Z');
  });


  it('resolves Tonight correctly', () => {
    const result = resolveTemporalWindow(TimeWindowPreset.Tonight);
    // Tonight 18:00‑23:59 local on 2025‑03‑13 => in UTC:
    // 18:00 CDT = 23:00 UTC, 23:59 CDT = 04:59 UTC (next day)
    expect(result.startUtc).toBe('2025-03-13T23:00:00.000Z');
    expect(result.endUtc).toBe('2025-03-14T04:59:59.999Z');
  });


  it('resolves ThisWeekend correctly', () => {
    const result = resolveTemporalWindow(TimeWindowPreset.ThisWeekend);
    // 2025‑03‑13 is a Thursday, Saturday is 2025‑03‑15.
    // Start = 2025‑03‑15T00:00 CDT = 2025‑03‑15T05:00Z
    // End   = 2025‑03‑16T23:59:59.999 CDT = 2025‑03‑17T04:59:59.999Z
    expect(result.startUtc).toBe('2025-03-15T05:00:00.000Z');
    expect(result.endUtc).toBe('2025-03-17T04:59:59.999Z');
  });


  it('passes through Custom window unchanged', () => {
    const start = new Date('2025-04-01T08:00:00Z');
    const end   = new Date('2025-04-01T10:00:00Z');
    const result = resolveTemporalWindow(TimeWindowPreset.Custom, start, end);
    expect(result.startUtc).toBe(start.toISOString());
    expect(result.endUtc).toBe(end.toISOString());
  });
});

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The front‑end resolver must import the same enum (TimeWindowPreset) from a generated TypeScript definition that mirrors the C# enum.
	Guarantees identical spelling/casing.
	The algorithm must not rely on the browser’s local time zone – it must force the market TZ (America/Chicago) via the Intl API.
	Prevents user‑side TZ leakage.
	All returned timestamps must be ISO‑8601 UTC strings (Z suffix) to match the back‑end ResolvedTimeWindow.
	Allows easy JSON round‑trip.
	The resolver should throw on missing custom dates (mirroring the back‑end).
	Consistent error handling.
	Unit‑tests must freeze the system clock to guarantee deterministic output.
	Guarantees repeatable test results.
	Acceptance Criteria
* TypeScript declaration weup-temporal with enum and ResolvedTimeWindow interface compiles.
* temporalResolver.ts implements resolveTemporalWindow that mirrors the C# logic, using the market TZ and handling DST correctly.
* The resolver is pure (no external I/O).
* Unit‑test (temporalResolver.spec.ts) passes for all presets, including DST edge cases, and validates the custom window passthrough.
* The front‑end builds without TypeScript errors, and the generated DTO can be imported by any component that needs to request a temporal window (e.g., the map feed service).
________________


M8‑P40 — Validate Temporal Edge Cases and Boundary Conditions v1.0
markdown
# Context
All services that accept a `TemporalFilterDto` (map feed, calendar overlay, moderation) must enforce **validation** before the window is resolved.  Invalid requests (e.g., End < Start, windows longer than 30 days, custom windows that cross DST boundaries incorrectly) should be rejected early with a clear error message.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **validation result** (`TemporalValidationResult`).  
2️⃣ Declare a **validation service contract** (`ITemporalValidator`).  
3️⃣ Implement a **deterministic validator** (`TemporalValidator`) that checks:
   - Preset enum is defined.
   - For `Custom`, both `CustomStartUtc` and `CustomEndUtc` are present, `Start < End`, and the duration does not exceed a configurable **max‑duration** (default 30 days).  
   - For non‑custom presets, after resolution the duration is within allowed limits (e.g., “Tonight” always ≤ 6 h, “Next48Hours” ≤ 48 h).  
   - The resulting window does **not** span more than one DST transition (optional strictness – for MVP we just ensure the duration ≤ max).  
4️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** covering every failure mode and a successful case.  
5️⃣ Wire the validator into the **MapFeed** and **CalendarOverlay** services (call `ValidateAsync` before `ResolveAsync`).  


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/Validation`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/Validation/TemporalValidationResult.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation;


/// <summary>
/// Result of validating a <see cref="TemporalFilterDto"/>.  If <c>IsValid</c> is
/// false, <c>Errors</c> contains one or more human‑readable messages.
/// </summary>
public sealed record TemporalValidationResult(
    bool IsValid,
    IReadOnlyList<string> Errors);


# Context
All services that accept a `TemporalFilterDto` (map feed, calendar overlay, moderation) must enforce **validation** before the window is resolved.  Invalid requests (e.g., End < Start, windows longer than 30 days, custom windows that cross DST boundaries incorrectly) should be rejected early with a clear error message.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **validation result** (`TemporalValidationResult`).  
2️⃣ Declare a **validation service contract** (`ITemporalValidator`).  
3️⃣ Implement a **deterministic validator** (`TemporalValidator`) that checks:
   - Preset enum is defined.
   - For `Custom`, both `CustomStartUtc` and `CustomEndUtc` are present, `Start < End`, and the duration does not exceed a configurable **max‑duration** (default 30 days).  
   - For non‑custom presets, after resolution the duration is within allowed limits (e.g., “Tonight” always ≤ 6 h, “Next48Hours” ≤ 48 h).  
   - The resulting window does **not** span more than one DST transition (optional strictness – for MVP we just ensure the duration ≤ max).  
4️⃣ Provide a **unit‑test** covering every failure mode and a successful case.  
5️⃣ Wire the validator into the **MapFeed** and **CalendarOverlay** services (call `ValidateAsync` before `ResolveAsync`).

using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure validator that checks a <see cref="TemporalFilterDto"/> for
    /// structural and business‑rule correctness before any resolution occurs.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ITemporalValidator
    {
        Task<TemporalValidationResult> ValidateAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter);
    }
}

// File: WeUP.Contracts/Temporal/Validation/ITemporalValidator.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure validator that checks a <see cref="TemporalFilterDto"/> for
    /// structural and business‑rule correctness before any resolution occurs.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ITemporalValidator
    {
        Task<TemporalValidationResult> ValidateAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter);
    }
}


Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalValidator.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalValidator.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    public sealed class TemporalValidator : ITemporalValidator
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalValidator> _log;
        private readonly ITemporalWindowResolver _resolver;


        // Maximum allowed duration for any window (configurable, default 30 days).
        private readonly TimeSpan _maxDuration = TimeSpan.FromDays(30);


        public TemporalValidator(ILogger<TemporalValidator> log, ITemporalWindowResolver resolver)
        {
            _log = log;
            _resolver = resolver;
        }


        public async Task<TemporalValidationResult> ValidateAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Ensure the preset value is defined (Enum.Parse already guarantees it
            //    when deserialized from JSON, but guard against unknown values).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (!Enum.IsDefined(typeof(TimeWindowPreset), filter.Preset))
                errors.Add($"Unsupported temporal preset: {filter.Preset}");


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Custom preset validation.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (filter.Preset == TimeWindowPreset.Custom)
            {
                if (!filter.CustomStartUtc.HasValue || !filter.CustomEndUtc.HasValue)
                {
                    errors.Add("Custom preset requires both CustomStartUtc and CustomEndUtc.");
                }
                else
                {
                    var start = filter.CustomStartUtc.Value;
                    var end   = filter.CustomEndUtc.Value;


                    if (start >= end)
                        errors.Add("CustomStartUtc must be earlier than CustomEndUtc.");


                    var duration = end - start;
                    if (duration > _maxDuration)
                        errors.Add($"Custom window duration ({duration.TotalDays:F1} days) exceeds the maximum allowed ({_maxDuration.TotalDays} days).");
                }
            }
            else
            {
                // -----------------------------------------------------------------
                // 3️⃣ For built‑in presets we resolve the window first
                //     so we can check the derived duration against the max.
                // -----------------------------------------------------------------
                try
                {
                    var resolved = await _resolver.ResolveAsync(filter);
                    var duration = resolved.EndUtc - resolved.StartUtc;


                    if (duration > _maxDuration)
                        errors.Add($"Resolved window ({duration.TotalDays:F1} days) exceeds the allowed maximum of {_maxDuration.TotalDays} days.");
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    // The resolver throws for malformed input (e.g., missing custom dates).
                    errors.Add($"Error while resolving preset: {ex.Message}");
                }
            }


            var result = new TemporalValidationResult(
                IsValid: errors.Count == 0,
                Errors: errors);


            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Temporal validation failed: {Count} errors.", errors.Count);


            return result;
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Temporal/TemporalValidator.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal;
using WeUP.Contracts.Temporal.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Temporal
{
    public sealed class TemporalValidator : ITemporalValidator
    {
        private readonly ILogger<TemporalValidator> _log;
        private readonly ITemporalWindowResolver _resolver;


        // Maximum allowed duration for any window (configurable, default 30 days).
        private readonly TimeSpan _maxDuration = TimeSpan.FromDays(30);


        public TemporalValidator(ILogger<TemporalValidator> log, ITemporalWindowResolver resolver)
        {
            _log = log;
            _resolver = resolver;
        }


        public async Task<TemporalValidationResult> ValidateAsync(TemporalFilterDto filter)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Ensure the preset value is defined (Enum.Parse already guarantees it
            //    when deserialized from JSON, but guard against unknown values).
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (!Enum.IsDefined(typeof(TimeWindowPreset), filter.Preset))
                errors.Add($"Unsupported temporal preset: {filter.Preset}");


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Custom preset validation.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            if (filter.Preset == TimeWindowPreset.Custom)
            {
                if (!filter.CustomStartUtc.HasValue || !filter.CustomEndUtc.HasValue)
                {
                    errors.Add("Custom preset requires both CustomStartUtc and CustomEndUtc.");
                }
                else
                {
                    var start = filter.CustomStartUtc.Value;
                    var end   = filter.CustomEndUtc.Value;


                    if (start >= end)
                        errors.Add("CustomStartUtc must be earlier than CustomEndUtc.");


                    var duration = end - start;
                    if (duration > _maxDuration)
                        errors.Add($"Custom window duration ({duration.TotalDays:F1} days) exceeds the maximum allowed ({_maxDuration.TotalDays} days).");
                }
            }
            else
            {
                // -----------------------------------------------------------------
                // 3️⃣ For built‑in presets we resolve the window first
                //     so we can check the derived duration against the max.
                // -----------------------------------------------------------------
                try
                {
                    var resolved = await _resolver.ResolveAsync(filter);
                    var duration = resolved.EndUtc - resolved.StartUtc;


                    if (duration > _maxDuration)
                        errors.Add($"Resolved window ({duration.TotalDays:F1} days) exceeds the allowed maximum of {_maxDuration.TotalDays} days.");
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    // The resolver throws for malformed input (e.g., missing custom dates).
                    errors.Add($"Error while resolving preset: {ex.Message}");
                }
            }


            var result = new TemporalValidationResult(
                IsValid: errors.Count == 0,
                Errors: errors);


            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Temporal validation failed: {Count} errors.", errors.Count);


            return result;
        }
    }
}


Integration Example (Map feed service)
csharp
public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
    MapFeedQueryDto query, bool enableClustering = true)
{
    // Validate temporal part of the query before proceeding.
    var temporalDto = new TemporalFilterDto(filter.Preset, filter.CustomStartUtc, filter.CustomEndUtc);
    var validation = await _temporalValidator.ValidateAsync(temporalDto);
    if (!validation.IsValid)
        throw new ArgumentException($"Temporal filter invalid: {string.Join("; ", validation.Errors)}");


    // Resolve to concrete UTC window...
    var window = await _temporalResolver.ResolveAsync(temporalDto);
    // Use window.StartUtc / window.EndUtc in the spatial query...
}


public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterMapItemDto>> GetMapFeedAsync(
    MapFeedQueryDto query, bool enableClustering = true)
{
    // Validate temporal part of the query before proceeding.
    var temporalDto = new TemporalFilterDto(filter.Preset, filter.CustomStartUtc, filter.CustomEndUtc);
    var validation = await _temporalValidator.ValidateAsync(temporalDto);
    if (!validation.IsValid)
        throw new ArgumentException($"Temporal filter invalid: {string.Join("; ", validation.Errors)}");


    // Resolve to concrete UTC window...
    var window = await _temporalResolver.ResolveAsync(temporalDto);
    // Use window.StartUtc / window.EndUtc in the spatial query...
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Validation must be pure – no DB access, only arithmetic and the resolver (which itself is pure).
	Guarantees deterministic outcomes.
	Errors are human‑readable strings collected in TemporalValidationResult.Errors.
	Allows the API to surface a clear 400 response.
	The maximum duration (_maxDuration) is configurable (e.g., via IOptions<TemporalOptions> in a real system). For the MVP it is hard‑coded to 30 days.
	Prevents excessively large queries.
	The validator must be called before any heavy‑weight query execution (e.g., in map feed, calendar overlay).
	Saves resources.
	For non‑custom presets the validator resolves the window (via ITemporalWindowResolver) to check the derived duration – this guarantees that the same DST rules are applied as in production.
	Consistency.
	The validator never throws; it always returns a TemporalValidationResult. The caller decides whether to turn a failure into a 400 Bad Request.
	Clear separation of concerns.
	Acceptance Criteria
* TemporalValidationResult and ITemporalValidator compile.
* TemporalValidator validates all cases (custom missing dates, start ≥ end, duration > 30 days, unknown preset).
* The validator uses the same ITemporalWindowResolver implementation, ensuring identical DST handling.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Temporal/TemporalValidatorTests.cs covers:
   1. Valid preset “Now” → IsValid = true.
   2. Custom window with missing dates → error.
   3. Custom window where Start >= End → error.
   4. Custom window longer than 30 days → error.
   5. Preset that resolves to > 30 days (e.g., artificially set a max 1 day and test the guard).
* The map feed or calendar overlay services are updated to call ValidateAsync before resolution (no compile errors).
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ITemporalValidator, TemporalValidator>();.

With these five prompt‑blocks applied you now have a fully‑typed, deterministic, and validated temporal engine that:
* defines a single source of truth for preset semantics,
* resolves them to UTC windows respecting DST and market localisation,
* normalises all time‑zone conversions through a shared adapter,
* exposes the same logic to the front‑end via a generated TypeScript module, and
* validates every incoming temporal request before any heavy processing occurs.
All components are pure, unit‑testable, and ready to be wired into the map‑feed, calendar overlay, ingestion, and moderation pipelines of the WeUP MVP. Happy coding!

M9‑P41 — Define Market, District, and Neighborhood Model v1.0
markdown
# Context
WeUP currently supports **only the Houston market** (Phase 0).  All spatial queries must be scoped to a market → district → neighborhood hierarchy so that the UI can filter, the API can validate, and the database can enforce referential integrity.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define three **value‑objects** (`MarketId`, `DistrictId`, `NeighborhoodId`).  
2️⃣ Define three **record types** (`Market`, `District`, `Neighborhood`) that capture the canonical hierarchy (ids, display name, parent id).  
3️⃣ Create an **enumeration** (`MarketRegion`) that can be expanded in future phases.  
4️⃣ Add a **repository interface** (`ISpatialHierarchyRepository`) that can read the static taxonomy (read‑only – no writes in MVP).  
5️⃣ Provide a **seed‑data POCO** (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs`) that populates the taxonomy for the MVP (hard‑coded collections).  
6️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** that validates the hierarchy (district belongs to market, neighborhood belongs to district).


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Spatial`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/MarketRegion.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
namespace WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;


/// <summary>
/// High‑level market region identifiers.  For the MVP only Houston exists.
/// </summary>
public enum MarketRegion
{
    Houston   // Future markets (e.g., Dallas, Austin) can be added later.
}

Repository Contract (place under WeUP.Domain/Spatial)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Spatial/ISpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Spatial
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Read‑only repository for the static market → district → neighborhood taxonomy.
    /// All methods are async for API consistency, but the implementation reads
    /// in‑memory collections seeded at startup.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync();
        Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId);
        Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId);
        Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId);
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Spatial/ISpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------

namespace WeUP.Domain.Spatial

    /// All methods are async for API consistency, but the implementation reads
    /// in‑memory collections seeded at startup.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync();
        Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId);
        Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId);
        Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId);
    }
}

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepository.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialHierarchyRepository : ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        // In‑memory store – populated from the seed at startup.
        private static readonly IReadOnlyCollection<Market> _markets = new[]
        {
            HoustonSpatialSeed.HoustonMarket
        };


        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync() =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets);


        public Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets.FirstOrDefault(m => m.Id == marketId));


        public Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == districtId));


        public Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .SelectMany(d => d.Neighborhoods)
                .FirstOrDefault(n => n.Id == neighborhoodId));
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialHierarchyRepository : ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        // In‑memory store – populated from the seed at startup.
        private static readonly IReadOnlyCollection<Market> _markets = new[]
        {
            HoustonSpatialSeed.HoustonMarket
        };


        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync() =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets);


        public Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets.FirstOrDefault(m => m.Id == marketId));


        public Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == districtId));


        public Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .SelectMany(d => d.Neighborhoods)
                .FirstOrDefault(n => n.Id == neighborhoodId));
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All identifier structs (MarketId, DistrictId, NeighborhoodId) are readonly records wrapping a Guid.
	Guarantees type safety and immutability.
	The hierarchy is read‑only – no CRUD endpoints are exposed for the MVP.
	Prevents accidental mutation of the taxonomy.
	The seed data must be consistent (each district’s ParentMarketId matches the market’s Id; each neighborhood’s ParentDistrictId matches its district).
	Enables simple validation tests.
	The repository methods are async (return Task) to keep a consistent API surface, even though the data is in memory.
	Allows future swap‑out with a DB implementation without breaking callers.
	Acceptance Criteria
* MarketRegion, MarketId, DistrictId, NeighborhoodId, Market, District, Neighborhood compile.
* ISpatialHierarchyRepository interface exists with the four async methods.
* HoustonSpatialSeed defines a market with at least two districts and one neighborhood per district (as shown).
* SpatialHierarchyRepository correctly implements the look‑ups (unit‑tests confirm hierarchy integrity).
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepositoryTests.cs verifies:
   1. GetAllMarketsAsync returns exactly one market (Houston).
   2. GetDistrictAsync returns the correct district for a known DistrictId.
   3. GetNeighborhoodAsync returns the correct neighborhood and its parent linkage.
   4. Invalid IDs return null.
* DI registration: services.AddSingleton<ISpatialHierarchyRepository, SpatialHierarchyRepository>();.

M9‑P42 — Implement Geo Validation and Coordinate Integrity v1.0
markdown
# Context
All events must have a **valid geographic point** (latitude ∈ [-90, 90], longitude ∈ [-180, 180]).  Invalid coordinates must be rejected early (ingestion, flyer upload, UI).  The validation logic must be reusable across back‑end services and front‑end TS helpers.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **validation result** (`GeoValidationResult`).  
2️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`IGeoValidationService`) that validates a `GeoPoint`.  
3️⃣ Create a **value‑object** (`GeoPoint`) – already defined in the spatial contracts (M9‑P41).  
4️⃣ Implement a **pure validator** (`GeoValidationService`) that:  
   - Checks latitude/longitude ranges.  
   - Optionally checks for “zero‑coordinates” (0,0) as a sentinel for missing data.  
   - Returns a result with `IsValid` and a list of human‑readable error messages.  
5️⃣ Add **extension methods** on `GeoPoint` (`IsValid(this GeoPoint pt)`) for convenient in‑line checks.  
6️⃣ Scaffold a **unit‑test** covering the full range of valid/invalid cases.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/Validation`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/Validation/GeoValidationResult.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Validation;


/// <summary>
/// Result of a geographic point validation.
/// </summary>
public sealed record GeoValidationResult(
    bool IsValid,
    IReadOnlyList<string> Errors);

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial/GeoValidationService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/GeoValidationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class GeoValidationService : IGeoValidationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<GeoValidationService> _log;
        private const double MinLat = -90.0;
        private const double MaxLat = 90.0;
        private const double MinLng = -180.0;
        private const double MaxLng = 180.0;


        public GeoValidationService(ILogger<GeoValidationService> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<GeoValidationResult> ValidateAsync(GeoPoint point)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            if (point.Latitude < MinLat || point.Latitude > MaxLat)
                errors.Add($"Latitude {point.Latitude} is out of bounds ({MinLat} to {MaxLat}).");


            if (point.Longitude < MinLng || point.Longitude > MaxLng)
                errors.Add($"Longitude {point.Longitude} is out of bounds ({MinLng} to {MaxLng}).");


            // Treat (0,0) as a sentinel for “missing” coordinates – optional rule.
            if (Math.Abs(point.Latitude) < 1e-9 && Math.Abs(point.Longitude) < 1e-9)
                errors.Add("Coordinates (0,0) are not allowed – they indicate missing location data.");


            var result = new GeoValidationResult(errors.Count == 0, errors);
            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Geo validation failed: {Errors}", string.Join("; ", errors));


            return Task.FromResult(result);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension method for inline checks – used by domain code.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class GeoPointExtensions
    {
        public static async Task<bool> IsValidAsync(this GeoPoint point, IGeoValidationService validator) =>
            (await validator.ValidateAsync(point)).IsValid;
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/GeoValidationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class GeoValidationService : IGeoValidationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<GeoValidationService> _log;
        private const double MinLat = -90.0;
        private const double MaxLat = 90.0;
        private const double MinLng = -180.0;
        private const double MaxLng = 180.0;


        public GeoValidationService(ILogger<GeoValidationService> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<GeoValidationResult> ValidateAsync(GeoPoint point)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            if (point.Latitude < MinLat || point.Latitude > MaxLat)
                errors.Add($"Latitude {point.Latitude} is out of bounds ({MinLat} to {MaxLat}).");


            if (point.Longitude < MinLng || point.Longitude > MaxLng)
                errors.Add($"Longitude {point.Longitude} is out of bounds ({MinLng} to {MaxLng}).");


            // Treat (0,0) as a sentinel for “missing” coordinates – optional rule.
            if (Math.Abs(point.Latitude) < 1e-9 && Math.Abs(point.Longitude) < 1e-9)
                errors.Add("Coordinates (0,0) are not allowed – they indicate missing location data.");


            var result = new GeoValidationResult(errors.Count == 0, errors);
            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Geo validation failed: {Errors}", string.Join("; ", errors));


            return Task.FromResult(result);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension method for inline checks – used by domain code.
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class GeoPointExtensions
    {
        public static async Task<bool> IsValidAsync(this GeoPoint point, IGeoValidationService validator) =>
            (await validator.ValidateAsync(point)).IsValid;
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Why
	Validation is pure – no DB or external calls.
	Guarantees deterministic unit tests.
	Latitude range ‑90 ≤ lat ≤ 90, longitude ‑180 ≤ lng ≤ 180 (hard‑coded).
	Aligns with geographic standards.
	(0,0) is considered invalid for the MVP (helps catch missing data).
	Prevents silent acceptance of placeholder coordinates.
	Errors are human‑readable strings collected in Errors.
	Allows API to surface a clear 400 response.
	The service logs any validation failures at Warning level.
	Observability.
	The extension method (IsValidAsync) enables concise code: if (!await point.IsValidAsync(_geoValidator)) ….
	Improves readability.
	Acceptance Criteria
* GeoValidationResult, IGeoValidationService, GeoValidationService, and GeoPointExtensions compile.
* GeoValidationService.ValidateAsync returns IsValid = true for a point within range (e.g., (29.7604, ‑95.3698)).
* Returns IsValid = false with appropriate error messages for: latitude out of range, longitude out of range, and (0,0).
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Spatial/GeoValidationServiceTests.cs covers:
   1. Valid point → success.
   2. Latitude > 90 → error.
   3. Longitude < ‑180 → error.
   4. (0,0) → error.
   5. Multiple simultaneous errors (e.g., lat = 200, lng = ‑200) → both messages present.
* Extension method works (await point.IsValidAsync(_geoValidator)).
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IGeoValidationService, GeoValidationService>();.

M9‑P43 — Build Spatial Query Semantics and Filtering Logic v1.0
markdown
# Context
The map feed endpoint receives a **spatial query** (bounding box, optional market/district/neighbor filters).  The back‑end must translate this into an EF‑Core query that returns only events whose `GeoPoint` lies inside the box and whose taxonomy matches the supplied filters.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **query DTO** (`SpatialQueryDto`) that captures the allowed filters:  
   - Bounding box (`MinLat`, `MaxLat`, `MinLng`, `MaxLng`).  
   - Optional `MarketId`, `DistrictId`, `NeighborhoodId`.  
2️⃣ Declare a **service contract** (`ISpatialQueryService`) that:  
   - Validates the query (using the geo validator from M9‑P42).  
   - Returns a **filtered collection of `EventAggregate`** (or a lightweight DTO).  
3️⃣ Provide a **reference EF‑Core implementation** (`SpatialQueryService`) that builds the query with proper `WHERE` clauses:  
   - `Latitude BETWEEN MinLat AND MaxLat` and `Longitude BETWEEN MinLng AND MaxLng`.  
   - Joins to the spatial hierarchy tables (via FK relationships) if filter IDs are present.  
4️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that verifies:  
   - Bounding‑box filtering works.  
   - Taxonomy filtering (market/district/neighborhood) works.  
   - Invalid query (e.g., min > max) returns a validation error.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/Query`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/Query/SpatialQueryDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;

namespace WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Query;


/// <summary>
/// Input contract for spatial queries (map feed, calendar slice, etc.).
/// All coordinates are expressed in **decimal degrees** (WGS‑84) and are UTC‑agnostic.
/// </summary>
public sealed record SpatialQueryDto(
    double MinLatitude,
    double MaxLatitude,
    double MinLongitude,
    double MaxLongitude,
    MarketId? MarketFilter = null,
    DistrictId? DistrictFilter = null,
    NeighborhoodId? NeighborhoodFilter = null);

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Spatial/ISpatialQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Query;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Spatial
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Pure service that applies a spatial filter to the event store and
    /// returns a collection of matching <see cref="EventAggregate"/>s.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISpatialQueryService
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Execute the spatial query.  Returns an empty collection if no events match.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>> QueryAsync(SpatialQueryDto query);
    }
}

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialQueryService.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Query;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialQueryService : ISpatialQueryService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<SpatialQueryService> _log;
        private readonly IGeoValidationService _geoValidator;


        public SpatialQueryService(
            WeUpDbContext db,
            ILogger<SpatialQueryService> log,
            IGeoValidationService geoValidator)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
            _geoValidator = geoValidator;
        }


        public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>> QueryAsync(SpatialQueryDto query)
        {
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Validate the bounding box (min ≤ max, ranges)
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var boxResult = await _geoValidator.ValidateAsync(
                new GeoPoint(query.MinLatitude, query.MinLongitude)); // Only checks range, not box logic.
            if (!boxResult.IsValid)
                throw new ArgumentException(string.Join("; ", boxResult.Errors));


            if (query.MinLatitude > query.MaxLatitude ||
                query.MinLongitude > query.MaxLongitude)
                throw new ArgumentException("Bounding box min values must be <= max values.");


            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Build the EF Core query.
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var q = _db.EventAggregates.AsQueryable();


            // Spatial bounding box
            q = q.Where(e =>
                e.Latitude  >= query.MinLatitude &&
                e.Latitude  <= query.MaxLatitude &&
                e.Longitude >= query.MinLongitude &&
                e.Longitude <= query.MaxLongitude);


            // Optional taxonomy filters – join with the static hierarchy tables.
            if (query.MarketFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.MarketId == query.MarketFilter.Value);
            }


            if (query.DistrictFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.DistrictId == query.DistrictFilter.Value);
            }


            if (query.NeighborhoodFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.NeighborhoodId == query.NeighborhoodFilter.Value);
            }


            var entities = await q.ToListAsync();


            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Map EF entities -> domain aggregates (pure mapping)
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var result = entities.Select(e => new EventAggregate(
                EventId: e.EventId,
                Title: e.Title,
                StartUtc: e.StartUtc,
                EndUtc: e.EndUtc,
                VenueName: e.VenueName,
                Location: new GeoPoint(e.Latitude, e.Longitude),
                Status: e.Status,
                RiskLevel: e.RiskLevel,
                Version: e.Version,
                ConfidenceScore: e.ConfidenceScore,
                FlyerImageUrl: e.FlyerImageUrl,
                CreatedAtUtc: e.CreatedAtUtc,
                UpdatedAtUtc: e.UpdatedAtUtc)).ToArray();


            _log.LogInformation("Spatial query returned {Count} events.", result.Length);
            return result;
        }
    }
}

using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Query;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialQueryService : ISpatialQueryService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<SpatialQueryService> _log;
        private readonly IGeoValidationService _geoValidator;


        public SpatialQueryService(
            WeUpDbContext db,
            ILogger<SpatialQueryService> log,
            IGeoValidationService geoValidator)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
            _geoValidator = geoValidator;
        }


        public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>> QueryAsync(SpatialQueryDto query)
        {
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Validate the bounding box (min ≤ max, ranges)
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var boxResult = await _geoValidator.ValidateAsync(
                new GeoPoint(query.MinLatitude, query.MinLongitude)); // Only checks range, not box logic.
            if (!boxResult.IsValid)
                throw new ArgumentException(string.Join("; ", boxResult.Errors));


            if (query.MinLatitude > query.MaxLatitude ||
                query.MinLongitude > query.MaxLongitude)
                throw new ArgumentException("Bounding box min values must be <= max values.");


            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Build the EF Core query.
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var q = _db.EventAggregates.AsQueryable();


            // Spatial bounding box
            q = q.Where(e =>
                e.Latitude  >= query.MinLatitude &&
                e.Latitude  <= query.MaxLatitude &&
                e.Longitude >= query.MinLongitude &&
                e.Longitude <= query.MaxLongitude);


            // Optional taxonomy filters – join with the static hierarchy tables.
            if (query.MarketFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.MarketId == query.MarketFilter.Value);
            }


            if (query.DistrictFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.DistrictId == query.DistrictFilter.Value);
            }


            if (query.NeighborhoodFilter.HasValue)
            {
                q = q.Where(e => e.NeighborhoodId == query.NeighborhoodFilter.Value);
            }


            var entities = await q.ToListAsync();


            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Map EF entities -> domain aggregates (pure mapping)
            // -------------------------------------------------------------
            var result = entities.Select(e => new EventAggregate(
                EventId: e.EventId,
                Title: e.Title,
                StartUtc: e.StartUtc,
                EndUtc: e.EndUtc,
                VenueName: e.VenueName,
                Location: new GeoPoint(e.Latitude, e.Longitude),
                Status: e.Status,
                RiskLevel: e.RiskLevel,
                Version: e.Version,
                ConfidenceScore: e.ConfidenceScore,
                FlyerImageUrl: e.FlyerImageUrl,
                CreatedAtUtc: e.CreatedAtUtc,
                UpdatedAtUtc: e.UpdatedAtUtc)).ToArray();


            _log.LogInformation("Spatial query returned {Count} events.", result.Length);
            return result;
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The query must validate the bounding‑box ranges before issuing the DB call (throw ArgumentException on invalid input).
	Prevents malformed SQL and gives the API a clear 400 response.
	All coordinate checks reuse IGeoValidationService (single source of truth).
	Consistency.
	The EF query uses range predicates (BETWEEN) – these are index‑friendly if spatial indexes are created on Latitude/Longitude.
	Performance.
	Optional taxonomy filters are applied only when supplied; missing filters mean “no restriction”.
	Flexibility.
	The service is pure aside from the DB call; mapping to EventAggregate does not modify any entity.
	Testability.
	The method returns empty collection when nothing matches (never null).
	API contract.
	Acceptance Criteria
* SpatialQueryDto, ISpatialQueryService, and SpatialQueryService compile.
* SpatialQueryService.QueryAsync validates the bbox, applies all filter predicates, and returns a list of EventAggregate objects.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Spatial/SpatialQueryServiceTests.cs verifies:
   1. Bounding‑box filter returns only events inside the box.
   2. Market filter limits results to the specified market.
   3. District and Neighborhood filters behave similarly.
   4. Invalid bbox (min > max) throws ArgumentException.
   5. Empty result when no events satisfy the criteria.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ISpatialQueryService, SpatialQueryService>();.

M9‑P44 — Design Cluster‑Ready Spatial Response Structures v1.0
markdown
# Context
The map feed can optionally return **pre‑clustered** results (to reduce client‑side work).  The response must contain either a single marker or a cluster payload that includes the geographic centre, the number of items, and an array of the contained `EventMapItemDto`s (from M5‑P21).  The shape must be stable, versioned, and serialisable as JSON.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **cluster DTO** (`ClusterSpatialItemDto`) that contains a list of `EventMapItemDto`s, the centre point, and the total count.  
2️⃣ Define a **union‑style response** (`SpatialFeedResponseDto`) that contains either a flat list of `EventMapItemDto` (when clustering disabled) or a list of `ClusterSpatialItemDto`.  
3️⃣ Add **JSON‑serialization attributes** (`[JsonPropertyName]`) to guarantee stable field names.  
4️⃣ Provide a **factory method** (`SpatialResponseFactory.CreateClusteredResponse`) that the map service can call after clustering.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that asserts correct JSON shape for both clustered and non‑clustered responses.


# Contracts (place under `WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto`)


```csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/ClusterSpatialItemDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Payload sent when the server returns a pre‑clustered set of map items.
/// </summary>
public sealed record ClusterSpatialItemDto(
    IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Items,   // 1‑N markers in this cluster
    double CenterLat,                               // Geographic centre (average of points)
    double CenterLng,
    int Count);                                     // Redundant – Items.Count for convenience


# Context
The map feed can optionally return **pre‑clustered** results (to reduce client‑side work).  The response must contain either a single marker or a cluster payload that includes the geographic centre, the number of items, and an array of the contained `EventMapItemDto`s (from M5‑P21).  The shape must be stable, versioned, and serialisable as JSON.


# Objective
1️⃣ Define a **cluster DTO** (`ClusterSpatialItemDto`) that contains a list of `EventMapItemDto`s, the centre point, and the total count.  
2️⃣ Define a **union‑style response** (`SpatialFeedResponseDto`) that contains either a flat list of `EventMapItemDto` (when clustering disabled) or a list of `ClusterSpatialItemDto`.  
3️⃣ Add **JSON‑serialization attributes** (`[JsonPropertyName]`) to guarantee stable field names.  
4️⃣ Provide a **factory method** (`SpatialResponseFactory.CreateClusteredResponse`) that the map service can call after clustering.  
5️⃣ Add a **unit‑test** that asserts correct JSON shape for both clustered and non‑clustered responses.

/// Root object returned by the map‑feed endpoint.  Exactly one of the
/// collections will be populated – the other will be empty.  The client can
/// decide whether to render individual markers or clustered aggregates.
/// </summary>
public sealed record SpatialFeedResponseDto(
    [property: JsonPropertyName("markers")] IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Markers,
    [property: JsonPropertyName("clusters")] IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterSpatialItemDto> Clusters);

/// </summary>
public sealed record SpatialFeedResponseDto(

Factory (place under WeUP.Application/Map/SpatialResponseFactory.cs)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/SpatialResponseFactory.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map
{
    public static class SpatialResponseFactory
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Build a response DTO either as a flat list of markers or as pre‑clustered data.
        /// </summary>
        /// <param name="markers">All markers that match the query (pre‑clustering).</param>
        /// <param name="clustered">If true, the method groups the markers into clusters
        ///   using a simple grid (same algorithm as the Map service’s clustering).</param>
        public static SpatialFeedResponseDto CreateResponse(
            IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> markers,
            bool clustered)
        {
            if (!clustered)
            {
                // No clustering – return markers directly.
                return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                    Markers: markers,
                    Clusters: Array.Empty<ClusterSpatialItemDto>());
            }


            // Simple deterministic grid clustering (reuse the same logic as the service).
            var gridSize = 0.01; // ~1 km cells – for MVP.
            var clusters = markers
                .GroupBy(m =>
                {
                    var latIdx = (int)Math.Floor(m.Latitude  / gridSize);
                    var lngIdx = (int)Math.Floor(m.Longitude / gridSize);
                    return (latIdx, lngIdx);
                })
                .Select(g =>
                {
                    var items = g.ToArray();
                    var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
                    var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
                    return new ClusterSpatialItemDto(
                        Items: items,
                        CenterLat: centerLat,
                        CenterLng: centerLng,
                        Count: items.Length);
                })
                .ToArray();


            return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                Markers: Array.Empty<EventMapItemDto>(),
                Clusters: clusters);
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/SpatialResponseFactory.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map
{
    public static class SpatialResponseFactory
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Build a response DTO either as a flat list of markers or as pre‑clustered data.
        /// </summary>
        /// <param name="markers">All markers that match the query (pre‑clustering).</param>
        /// <param name="clustered">If true, the method groups the markers into clusters
        ///   using a simple grid (same algorithm as the Map service’s clustering).</param>
        public static SpatialFeedResponseDto CreateResponse(
            IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> markers,
            bool clustered)
        {
            if (!clustered)
            {
                // No clustering – return markers directly.
                return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                    Markers: markers,
                    Clusters: Array.Empty<ClusterSpatialItemDto>());
            }


            // Simple deterministic grid clustering (reuse the same logic as the service).
            var gridSize = 0.01; // ~1 km cells – for MVP.
            var clusters = markers
                .GroupBy(m =>
                {
                    var latIdx = (int)Math.Floor(m.Latitude  / gridSize);
                    var lngIdx = (int)Math.Floor(m.Longitude / gridSize);
                    return (latIdx, lngIdx);
                })
                .Select(g =>
                {
                    var items = g.ToArray();
                    var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
                    var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
                    return new ClusterSpatialItemDto(
                        Items: items,
                        CenterLat: centerLat,
                        CenterLng: centerLng,
                        Count: items.Length);
                })
                .ToArray();


            return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                Markers: Array.Empty<EventMapItemDto>(),
                Clusters: clusters);
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The response must contain exactly one of Markers or Clusters populated – the other must be an empty array.
	Simplifies client logic.
	All numeric fields (Latitude, Longitude, CenterLat, CenterLng) are double with full precision; the JSON serializer will emit them as numbers.
	No loss of precision.
	The factory’s clustering algorithm must be deterministic (grid‑based with fixed cell size).
	Guarantees repeatable responses for the same input.
	The DTOs are immutable records – no setters.
	Guarantees thread‑safety and easy testing.
	The factory does not perform any DB I/O – it works on the in‑memory marker collection supplied by the map service.
	Separation of concerns.
	Acceptance Criteria
* ClusterSpatialItemDto and SpatialFeedResponseDto compile with the JSON property attributes.
* SpatialResponseFactory.CreateResponse returns a response with only Markers populated when clustered = false.
* When clustered = true, the method groups markers into clusters, calculates centre points, and populates the Clusters collection.
* Unit‑test WeUP.Tests/Map/SpatialResponseFactoryTests.cs verifies:
   1. Non‑clustered response contains the original list of markers, empty clusters.
   2. Clustered response groups markers correctly (e.g., two markers in the same grid cell appear in a single cluster).
   3. JSON serialization (using System.Text.Json.JsonSerializer) yields the expected field names (markers, clusters).
* The map feed service can call SpatialResponseFactory.CreateResponse after its own clustering logic.
________________

# Context
Stakeholders (product, QA, future developers) need an authoritative **markdown** document that describes the static spatial taxonomy for the Houston Phase 0 MVP: market → districts → neighborhoods, coordinate validation rules, and how the data is persisted.


# Objective
Create a **README‑style markdown file** (`HU_SPATIAL_MODEL.md`) that contains:
1. Overview of the hierarchy (diagram‑style list).  
2. Table of **Districts** (Id, Name).  
3. Table of **Neighborhoods** per district (Id, Name, ParentDistrictId).  
4. Explanation of **Geo validation rules** (lat/lng ranges, zero‑coordinate rule).  
5. Description of the **static seed data** (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs`).  
6. Instructions for developers on how to extend the model (add new markets, districts, neighborhoods).  
7. Links to the corresponding contract files (e.g., `WeUP.Contracts/Spatial/Market.cs`).

# Houston Phase 0 Spatial Model (v1.0)


## 1️⃣ Overview




Market (Houston) │ ├─ District: Midtown │ ├─ Neighborhood: Midtown West │ └─ Neighborhood: Midtown East │ └─ District: Downtown └─ Neighborhood: The Heights


*All identifiers are GUID‑based `readonly record struct`s defined in `WeUP.Contracts.Spatial`.*


## 2️⃣ District Table


| DistrictId (GUID)                              | DisplayName |
|-----------------------------------------------|-------------|
| `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` | Midtown |
| `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` | Downtown |


## 3️⃣ Neighborhood Table


| NeighborhoodId (GUID)                           | DisplayName   | ParentDistrictId |
|------------------------------------------------|---------------|------------------|
| `33333333-3333-3333-3333-333333333333` | Midtown West | `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` |
| `33333334-3333-3333-3333-333333333334` | Midtown East | `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` |
| `33333335-3333-3333-3333-333333333335` | The Heights | `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` |


## 4️⃣ Geo‑Validation Rules (M9‑P42)


- **Latitude** must be between **‑90 °** and **+90 °**.  
- **Longitude** must be between **‑180 °** and **+180 °**.  
- Coordinates **(0, 0)** are **invalid** – they indicate missing location data.  
- Validation is performed by `IGeoValidationService.ValidateAsync`.  


## 5️⃣ Seed Data (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs`)


The static data lives in `WeUP.Application.Spatial.HoustonSpatialSeed`.  
The class constructs a single `Market` instance with the GUID `11111111‑1111‑1111‑1111‑111111111111` and embeds the districts & neighborhoods shown above.


## 6️⃣ Extending the Model


1. **Add a new `Market`**: create a new GUID, set `Region = MarketRegion.<YourRegion>`, and add it to the collection in `SpatialHierarchyRepository`.  
2. **Add districts**: create `District` records with a new `DistrictId` and reference the parent `MarketId`.  
3. **Add neighborhoods**: create `Neighborhood` records with a new `NeighborhoodId` and reference the parent `DistrictId`.  
4. Update `HoustonSpatialSeed.cs` (or create a new seed class) and register the updated hierarchy in `SpatialHierarchyRepository`.  


## 7️⃣ References


- Contracts: `WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.*` (Market, District, Neighborhood, Id structs)  
- Validation Service: `WeUP.Application.Spatial.GeoValidationService`  
- Repository: `WeUP.Domain.Spatial.ISpatialHierarchyRepository` & `WeUP.Application.Spatial.SpatialHierarchyRepository`  


---  


*End of document.*  




*All identifiers are GUID‑based `readonly record struct`s defined in `WeUP.Contracts.Spatial`.*


## 2️⃣ District Table

|-----------------------------------------------|-------------|
| `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` | Midtown |
| `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` | Downtown |


## 3️⃣ Neighborhood Table

| `33333334-3333-3333-3333-333333333334` | Midtown East | `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` |
| `33333335-3333-3333-3333-333333333335` | The Heights | `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` |


## 4️⃣ Geo‑Validation Rules (M9‑P42)

## 5️⃣ Seed Data (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs`)

📦 M9‑P41 — Define Market, District, and Neighborhood Model v1.0
Context
WeUP currently supports only the Houston market (Phase 0). All spatial queries must be scoped to a market → district → neighborhood hierarchy so that the UI can filter, the API can validate, and the database can enforce referential integrity.

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Domain/Spatial/ISpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Domain.Spatial
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Read‑only repository for the static market → district → neighborhood taxonomy.
    /// All methods are async for API consistency, but the current implementation
    /// reads in‑memory collections seeded at startup.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync();
        Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId);
        Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId);
        Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId);
    }
}

namespace WeUP.Domain.Spatial

    /// reads in‑memory collections seeded at startup.
    /// </summary>
    public interface ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync();
        Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId);
        Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId);
        Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId);

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialHierarchyRepository : ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        // In‑memory store populated from the seed at application start.
        private static readonly IReadOnlyCollection<Market> _markets = new[]
        {
            HoustonSpatialSeed.HoustonMarket
        };


        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync() =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets);


        public Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets.FirstOrDefault(m => m.Id == marketId));


        public Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == districtId));


        public Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .SelectMany(d => d.Neighborhoods)
                .FirstOrDefault(n => n.Id == neighborhoodId));
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialHierarchyRepository.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialHierarchyRepository : ISpatialHierarchyRepository
    {
        // In‑memory store populated from the seed at application start.
        private static readonly IReadOnlyCollection<Market> _markets = new[]
        {
            HoustonSpatialSeed.HoustonMarket
        };


        public Task<IReadOnlyCollection<Market>> GetAllMarketsAsync() =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets);


        public Task<Market?> GetMarketAsync(MarketId marketId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets.FirstOrDefault(m => m.Id == marketId));


        public Task<District?> GetDistrictAsync(DistrictId districtId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .FirstOrDefault(d => d.Id == districtId));


        public Task<Neighborhood?> GetNeighborhoodAsync(NeighborhoodId neighborhoodId) =>
            Task.FromResult(_markets
                .SelectMany(m => m.Districts)
                .SelectMany(d => d.Neighborhoods)
                .FirstOrDefault(n => n.Id == neighborhoodId));
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Identifier structs (MarketId, DistrictId, NeighborhoodId) are readonly record struct wrappers around Guid.
	Compile‑time safety and immutability.
	The hierarchy is read‑only – no write endpoints for the MVP.
	Prevent accidental mutation of taxonomy.
	Seed data must be internally consistent (district‑parent IDs, neighborhood‑parent IDs).
	Guarantees meaningful look‑ups; unit‑tests will enforce it.
	Repository methods are async (return Task) to keep a stable API surface for future DB‑backed implementations.
	Allows seamless swap‑out later.
	All collections are exposed as IReadOnlyCollection<T>.
	Prevent callers from mutating the in‑memory seed.
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* MarketRegion, MarketId, DistrictId, NeighborhoodId, Market, District, Neighborhood compile under WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.
* ISpatialHierarchyRepository exists with the four async methods.
* HoustonSpatialSeed defines a market with at least two districts and one neighborhood per district (as shown).
* SpatialHierarchyRepository correctly implements the look‑ups (unit‑tests validate hierarchy integrity).
* DI registration (e.g. services.AddSingleton<ISpatialHierarchyRepository, SpatialHierarchyRepository>();) is ready.
________________


📦 M9‑P42 — Implement Geo Validation and Coordinate Integrity v1.0
Context
Every event must have a valid geographic point (latitude ∈ [‑90, 90], longitude ∈ [‑180, 180]). Invalid coordinates must be rejected early (ingestion, flyer upload, UI). The validation logic must be reusable across back‑end services and front‑end TypeScript helpers.
