Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/GeoValidationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class GeoValidationService : IGeoValidationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<GeoValidationService> _log;
        private const double MinLat = -90.0;
        private const double MaxLat =  90.0;
        private const double MinLng = -180.0;
        private const double MaxLng =  180.0;


        public GeoValidationService(ILogger<GeoValidationService> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<GeoValidationResult> ValidateAsync(GeoPoint point)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            if (point.Latitude < MinLat || point.Latitude > MaxLat)
                errors.Add($"Latitude {point.Latitude} is out of bounds ({MinLat}‑{MaxLat}).");


            if (point.Longitude < MinLng || point.Longitude > MaxLng)
                errors.Add($"Longitude {point.Longitude} is out of bounds ({MinLng}‑{MaxLng}).");


            // (0,0) is a sentinel for “missing” – treat as invalid for the MVP.
            if (Math.Abs(point.Latitude) < 1e-9 && Math.Abs(point.Longitude) < 1e-9)
                errors.Add("Coordinates (0,0) are not allowed – they indicate missing location data.");


            var result = new GeoValidationResult(errors.Count == 0, errors);
            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Geo validation failed: {Errors}", string.Join("; ", errors));


            return Task.FromResult(result);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension method for inline validation (optional convenience).
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class GeoPointExtensions
    {
        public static async Task<bool> IsValidAsync(this GeoPoint point, IGeoValidationService validator) =>
            (await validator.ValidateAsync(point)).IsValid;
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/GeoValidationService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Validation;


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class GeoValidationService : IGeoValidationService
    {
        private readonly ILogger<GeoValidationService> _log;
        private const double MinLat = -90.0;
        private const double MaxLat =  90.0;
        private const double MinLng = -180.0;
        private const double MaxLng =  180.0;


        public GeoValidationService(ILogger<GeoValidationService> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<GeoValidationResult> ValidateAsync(GeoPoint point)
        {
            var errors = new List<string>();


            if (point.Latitude < MinLat || point.Latitude > MaxLat)
                errors.Add($"Latitude {point.Latitude} is out of bounds ({MinLat}‑{MaxLat}).");


            if (point.Longitude < MinLng || point.Longitude > MaxLng)
                errors.Add($"Longitude {point.Longitude} is out of bounds ({MinLng}‑{MaxLng}).");


            // (0,0) is a sentinel for “missing” – treat as invalid for the MVP.
            if (Math.Abs(point.Latitude) < 1e-9 && Math.Abs(point.Longitude) < 1e-9)
                errors.Add("Coordinates (0,0) are not allowed – they indicate missing location data.");


            var result = new GeoValidationResult(errors.Count == 0, errors);
            if (!result.IsValid)
                _log.LogWarning("Geo validation failed: {Errors}", string.Join("; ", errors));


            return Task.FromResult(result);
        }
    }


    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    // Extension method for inline validation (optional convenience).
    // -----------------------------------------------------------------
    public static class GeoPointExtensions
    {
        public static async Task<bool> IsValidAsync(this GeoPoint point, IGeoValidationService validator) =>
            (await validator.ValidateAsync(point)).IsValid;
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Validation must be pure – no DB, no network calls.
	Guarantees deterministic unit tests.
	Latitude must be in ‑90 ≤ lat ≤ 90, Longitude in ‑180 ≤ lng ≤ 180.
	Aligns with geographic standards.
	(0,0) is considered invalid for the MVP (sentinel for missing data).
	Prevents silent acceptance of placeholder coordinates.
	Errors are human‑readable strings collected in Errors.
	Allows API to surface a clear 400 Bad Request.
	The service logs failures at Warning level.
	Provides observability (M10).
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* GeoPoint, GeoValidationResult, IGeoValidationService, and the extension method compile.
* GeoValidationService.ValidateAsync returns IsValid = true for a point inside the allowed ranges (e.g., (29.7604, ‑95.3698)).
* Returns IsValid = false with appropriate error messages for out‑of‑range latitude, out‑of‑range longitude, and (0,0).
* Unit‑test (WeUP.Tests/Spatial/GeoValidationServiceTests.cs) covers:
   * Valid point.
   * Latitude > 90.
   * Longitude < ‑180.
   * Zero‑coordinate sentinel.
   * Multiple simultaneous errors.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<IGeoValidationService, GeoValidationService>();.
________________


📦 M9‑P43 — Build Spatial Query Semantics and Filtering Logic v1.0
Context
The map‑feed endpoint receives a spatial query (bounding box + optional market/district/neighborhood filters). The backend must translate this into an EF‑Core query that returns only events whose GeoPoint lies inside the box and whose taxonomy matches the supplied filters.

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Spatial)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Spatial/SpatialQueryService.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using WeUP.Contracts.Events;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial;
using WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.Query;
using WeUP.Domain.Spatial;
using WeUP.Infrastructure.Persistence;
using WeUP.Application.Spatial; // for GeoValidationService (if needed)


namespace WeUP.Application.Spatial
{
    public sealed class SpatialQueryService : ISpatialQueryService
    {
        private readonly WeUpDbContext _db;
        private readonly ILogger<SpatialQueryService> _log;
        private readonly IGeoValidationService _geoValidator;


        public SpatialQueryService(
            WeUpDbContext db,
            ILogger<SpatialQueryService> log,
            IGeoValidationService geoValidator)
        {
            _db = db;
            _log = log;
            _geoValidator = geoValidator;
        }


        public async Task<IReadOnlyCollection<EventAggregate>> QueryAsync(SpatialQueryDto query)
        {
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 1️⃣ Validate the bounding‑box (min ≤ max, latitude/longitude within world bounds)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var boxCheck = await _geoValidator.ValidateAsync(
                new GeoPoint(query.MinLatitude, query.MinLongitude));
            if (!boxCheck.IsValid)
                throw new ArgumentException($"Invalid bounding‑box min corner: {string.Join("; ", boxCheck.Errors)}");


            if (query.MinLatitude > query.MaxLatitude ||
                query.MinLongitude > query.MaxLongitude)
                throw new ArgumentException("Bounding‑box min values must be <= max values.");


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 2️⃣ Build the EF Core query.
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var q = _db.EventAggregates.AsQueryable();


            // Spatial bounding box
            q = q.Where(e =>
                e.LocationLatitude  >= query.MinLatitude  && e.LocationLatitude  <= query.MaxLatitude &&
                e.LocationLongitude >= query.MinLongitude && e.LocationLongitude <= query.MaxLongitude);


            // Optional taxonomy filters
            if (query.MarketFilter.HasValue)
                q = q.Where(e => e.MarketId == query.MarketFilter.Value);


            if (query.DistrictFilter.HasValue)
                q = q.Where(e => e.DistrictId == query.DistrictFilter.Value);


            if (query.NeighborhoodFilter.HasValue)
                q = q.Where(e => e.NeighborhoodId == query.NeighborhoodFilter.Value);


            var entities = await q.ToListAsync();


            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            // 3️⃣ Map EF entities → domain aggregates (pure, deterministic)
            // -----------------------------------------------------------------
            var result = entities.Select(e => new EventAggregate(
                EventId:           e.EventId,
                Title:              e.Title,
                StartUtc:           e.StartUtc,
                EndUtc:             e.EndUtc,
                VenueName:          e.VenueName,
                Location:           new GeoPoint(e.LocationLatitude, e.LocationLongitude),
                Status:             e.Status,
                RiskLevel:          e.RiskLevel,
                Version:            e.Version,
                ConfidenceScore:    e.ConfidenceScore,
                FlyerImageUrl:      e.FlyerImageUrl,
                CreatedAtUtc:       e.CreatedAtUtc,
                UpdatedAtUtc:       e.UpdatedAtUtc)).ToArray();


            _log.LogInformation("Spatial query returned {Count} events.", result.Length);
            return result;
        }
    }
}

        }
    }

Entity column note – your EventAggregate EF entity must expose LocationLatitude and LocationLongitude columns (or a composite Geography type). Adjust the LINQ predicates accordingly.
Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	The method must validate the bounding‑box before issuing the DB query (throws ArgumentException on bad input).
	Guarantees early failure & clear API error.
	All coordinate checks reuse the single IGeoValidationService to keep validation logic in one place.
	Prevents drift between services.
	Optional taxonomy filters are applied only when supplied; missing filters imply “no restriction”.
	Flexibility for different UI use‑cases.
	The service returns never null – an empty collection signals “no match”.
	Consistent contract.
	Mapping from EF entity to EventAggregate is a pure, side‑effect‑free operation.
	Testability.
	The query uses simple BETWEEN‑style predicates (range filters) – these are index‑friendly if you add spatial indexes later.
	Performance.
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* SpatialQueryDto, ISpatialQueryService, and SpatialQueryService compile.
* SpatialQueryService.QueryAsync validates the bbox, applies latitude/longitude range filters, optional market/district/neighborhood filters, and returns a collection of EventAggregate.
* Unit‑test (WeUP.Tests/Spatial/SpatialQueryServiceTests.cs) verifies:
   * Bounding‑box filtering works (events inside/outside the box).
   * Market, district, and neighborhood filters correctly limit the result set.
   * Invalid bbox (min > max) throws ArgumentException.
   * Empty result when nothing matches.
* DI registration: services.AddTransient<ISpatialQueryService, SpatialQueryService>();.
________________


📦 M9‑P44 — Design Cluster‑Ready Spatial Response Structures v1.0
Context
The map feed can optionally return pre‑clustered results to reduce client‑side work. The response must contain either a flat list of markers or a list of clusters, with stable JSON field names.
Contracts (place under WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/ClusterSpatialItemDto.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Payload sent when the server returns a pre‑clustered set of map items.
/// </summary>
public sealed record ClusterSpatialItemDto(
    IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Items,   // 1‑N markers in this cluster
    double CenterLat,                             // Geographic centre of the cluster
    double CenterLng,
    int Count);                                   // Redundant – Items.Count for convenience

namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Payload sent when the server returns a pre‑clustered set of map items.
/// </summary>
public sealed record ClusterSpatialItemDto(
    IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Items,   // 1‑N markers in this cluster
    double CenterLat,                             // Geographic centre of the cluster
    double CenterLng,
    int Count);                                   // Redundant – Items.Count for convenience


csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/SpatialFeedResponseDto.cs

using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Text.Json.Serialization;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Root object returned by the map‑feed endpoint. Exactly one of the
/// collections will be populated – the other will be empty. The client can
/// decide whether to render individual markers or clustered aggregates.
/// </summary>
public sealed record SpatialFeedResponseDto(
    [property: JsonPropertyName("markers")]  IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Markers,
    [property: JsonPropertyName("clusters")] IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterSpatialItemDto> Clusters);

// File: WeUP.Contracts/Map/Dto/SpatialFeedResponseDto.cs

using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Text.Json.Serialization;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


/// <summary>
/// Root object returned by the map‑feed endpoint. Exactly one of the
/// collections will be populated – the other will be empty. The client can
/// decide whether to render individual markers or clustered aggregates.
/// </summary>
public sealed record SpatialFeedResponseDto(
    [property: JsonPropertyName("markers")]  IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> Markers,
    [property: JsonPropertyName("clusters")] IReadOnlyCollection<ClusterSpatialItemDto> Clusters);


Factory (place under WeUP.Application/Map)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/SpatialResponseFactory.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map
{
    public static class SpatialResponseFactory
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Build a response DTO either as a flat list of markers or as pre‑clustered data.
        /// </summary>
        /// <param name="markers">All matching markers (pre‑filtering applied).</param>
        /// <param name="clustered">If true, the method groups the markers into deterministic clusters.</param>
        public static SpatialFeedResponseDto CreateResponse(
            IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> markers,
            bool clustered)
        {
            if (!clustered)
            {
                // No clustering – return markers directly.
                return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                    Markers:  markers,
                    Clusters: Array.Empty<ClusterSpatialItemDto>());
            }


            // ---------------------------------------------------------
            // Simple deterministic grid clustering (cell size ≈ 0.001° ≈ 100 m).
            // ---------------------------------------------------------
            const double CellSizeDeg = 0.001;
            var clusters = markers
                .GroupBy(m =>
                {
                    var row = (int)Math.Floor(m.Latitude  / CellSizeDeg);
                    var col = (int)Math.Floor(m.Longitude / CellSizeDeg);
                    return (row, col);
                })
                .Select(g =>
                {
                    var items = g.ToArray();
                    var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
                    var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
                    return new ClusterSpatialItemDto(
                        Items:    items,
                        CenterLat: centerLat,
                        CenterLng: centerLng,
                        Count:    items.Length);
                })
                .ToArray();


            return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                Markers:  Array.Empty<EventMapItemDto>(),
                Clusters: clusters);
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Map/SpatialResponseFactory.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using WeUP.Contracts.Map.Dto;


namespace WeUP.Application.Map
{
    public static class SpatialResponseFactory
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Build a response DTO either as a flat list of markers or as pre‑clustered data.
        /// </summary>
        /// <param name="markers">All matching markers (pre‑filtering applied).</param>
        /// <param name="clustered">If true, the method groups the markers into deterministic clusters.</param>
        public static SpatialFeedResponseDto CreateResponse(
            IReadOnlyCollection<EventMapItemDto> markers,
            bool clustered)
        {
            if (!clustered)
            {
                // No clustering – return markers directly.
                return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                    Markers:  markers,
                    Clusters: Array.Empty<ClusterSpatialItemDto>());
            }


            // ---------------------------------------------------------
            // Simple deterministic grid clustering (cell size ≈ 0.001° ≈ 100 m).
            // ---------------------------------------------------------
            const double CellSizeDeg = 0.001;
            var clusters = markers
                .GroupBy(m =>
                {
                    var row = (int)Math.Floor(m.Latitude  / CellSizeDeg);
                    var col = (int)Math.Floor(m.Longitude / CellSizeDeg);
                    return (row, col);
                })
                .Select(g =>
                {
                    var items = g.ToArray();
                    var centerLat = items.Average(i => i.Latitude);
                    var centerLng = items.Average(i => i.Longitude);
                    return new ClusterSpatialItemDto(
                        Items:    items,
                        CenterLat: centerLat,
                        CenterLng: centerLng,
                        Count:    items.Length);
                })
                .ToArray();


            return new SpatialFeedResponseDto(
                Markers:  Array.Empty<EventMapItemDto>(),
                Clusters: clusters);
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Exactly one of Markers or Clusters must be populated; the other is an empty array.
	Simplifies client logic – it can check which list has items.
	All numeric fields (Latitude, Longitude, CenterLat, CenterLng) are double – JSON serializer will emit them as numbers with full precision.
	Prevents loss of location accuracy.
	The clustering algorithm is deterministic (fixed grid size, no randomness).
	Guarantees repeatable responses for the same input.
	The factory does no DB I/O – it works entirely on the in‑memory collection supplied by the calling map‑feed service.
	Separation of concerns.
	DTOs are immutable records – no setters, thread‑safe.
	Guarantees safety in concurrent environments.
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* ClusterSpatialItemDto, SpatialFeedResponseDto, and SpatialResponseFactory compile.
* SpatialResponseFactory.CreateResponse:
   * Returns a response with Markers populated and Clusters empty when clustered == false.
   * Returns a response with deterministic clusters (grid‑based) when clustered == true.
* Unit‑test (WeUP.Tests/Map/SpatialResponseFactoryTests.cs) checks:
   * Non‑clustered response contains exactly the original markers.
   * Clustered response groups markers that fall in the same grid cell, computes correct centre latitude/longitude, and sets Count correctly.
   * JSON serialization of the response produces the field names "markers" and "clusters" (via System.Text.Json).
* The map‑feed service (MapFeedService from M5‑P21) can call SpatialResponseFactory.CreateResponse after its own clustering step (or when clustering is disabled).
* DI registration (no special services needed – the factory is static).
________________

# Houston Phase 0 Spatial Model (v1.0)


## 1️⃣ Overview




# Houston Phase 0 Spatial Model (v1.0)


## 1️⃣ Overview




Market (Houston) ├─ District: Midtown │ ├─ Neighborhood: Midtown West │ └─ Neighborhood: Midtown East └─ District: Downtown └─ Neighborhood: The Heights


*All identifiers are GUID‑based `readonly record struct`s defined in `WeUP.Contracts.Spatial`.*


## 2️⃣ District Table


| DistrictId (GUID)                              | DisplayName |
|-----------------------------------------------|-------------|
| `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` | Midtown |
| `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` | Downtown |


## 3️⃣ Neighborhood Table


| NeighborhoodId (GUID)                           | DisplayName   | ParentDistrictId |
|------------------------------------------------|---------------|------------------|
| `33333333-3333-3333-3333-333333333333` | Midtown West | `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` |
| `33333334-3333-3333-3333-333333333334` | Midtown East | `22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222` |
| `33333335-3333-3333-3333-333333333335` | The Heights  | `22222223-2222-2222-2222-222222222223` |


## 4️⃣ Geo‑Validation Rules (M9‑P42)


- **Latitude** must be between **‑90 °** and **+90 °**.  
- **Longitude** must be between **‑180 °** and **+180 °**.  
- Coordinates **(0, 0)** are **invalid** – they indicate missing location data.  
- Validation is performed by `IGeoValidationService.ValidateAsync`.


## 5️⃣ Seed Data (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs`)


The static taxonomy is defined in `WeUP.Application.Spatial.HoustonSpatialSeed`.  
The class creates a single `Market` instance (`Id = 11111111‑1111‑1111‑1111‑111111111111`) and embeds the districts & neighborhoods listed above.


## 6️⃣ Extending the Model


1. **Add a new `Market`** – generate a new GUID, set `Region = MarketRegion.<YourRegion>`, and give it a display name.  
2. **Add districts** – create `District` records with a new `DistrictId` and reference the parent `MarketId`.  
3. **Add neighborhoods** – create `Neighborhood` records with a new `NeighborhoodId` and reference the parent `DistrictId`.  
4. Update the seed class (`HoustonSpatialSeed.cs` or create a new seed) and ensure `SpatialHierarchyRepository` returns the updated collection.


## 7️⃣ References


- **Contracts**: `WeUP.Contracts.Spatial.*` (Market, District, Neighborhood, Id structs)  
- **Validation Service**: `WeUP.Application.Spatial.GeoValidationService`  
- **Repository**: `WeUP.Domain.Spatial.ISpatialHierarchyRepository` & `WeUP.Application.Spatial.SpatialHierarchyRepository`

🎉 What you now have
1. Strongly‑typed market/district/neighborhood model (M9‑P41).
2. Reusable geographic validation (M9‑P42).
3. A pure spatial query service that filters events by bounding box and taxonomy (M9‑P43).
4. Cluster‑ready response DTOs and a deterministic factory to produce either flat marker lists or pre‑clustered payloads (M9‑P44).
5. A single source of truth documentation for the Houston Phase 0 taxonomy (M9‑P45).
All components are immutable, versioned, unit‑testable, and ready to be wired into the Map feed (M5‑P21) and any other spatial‑aware subsystem. Happy coding!
Below is a complete, copy‑paste‑ready “Bundle M10 – Ingestion Observability & Telemetry v1.0”.
For each prompt you will get:
1. Context / Objective – why the piece exists.
2. Contracts – immutable C# 11 records / interfaces / enums (placed in the Contracts layer).
3. Reference implementation – minimal, deterministic code (pure C# or EF‑Core where required).
4. Rules & Constraints – hard requirements the code must obey.
5. Acceptance‑Criteria checklist – what to verify after the code is generated.
6. Exact file‑path – relative to the repository root (create the folders if they do not exist).
NOTE – All metrics use OpenTelemetry (OpenTelemetry.Metrics).
The concrete metric‑record types are simple DTOs; the actual Meter objects are created once per service (or registered as singletons).
If you already have an OpenTelemetry SDK wired in the solution, just add the services below; otherwise add the OpenTelemetry.Extensions.Hosting package and call builder.Services.AddOpenTelemetryMetrics() in Program.cs.

📦 M10‑P46 — Implement Ingestion Metrics and Success Tracking v1.0
Context
The ingestion pipeline (source‑adapter → OCR → normalization → evidence tracking) must expose high‑level health metrics so that the SRE team can monitor throughput, error rates, and latency.
Contracts (place under WeUP.Contracts/Observability)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Observability/IngestionMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Collections.Generic;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


/// <summary>
/// Simple DTO that aggregates the most important ingestion‑pipeline counters.
/// All values are cumulative since the process started.
/// </summary>
public sealed record IngestionMetricsSnapshot(
    long TotalRequests,          // # of ingestion requests received
    long SuccessfulRequests,     // # that completed without error
    long FailedRequests,         // # that ended in error (OCR, parsing, etc.)
    double AverageLatencyMs,      // arithmetic mean of request latency
    IReadOnlyDictionary<string, long> FailureReasons); // e.g. {"OcrError":123}

csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Observability/IIngestionMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Observability
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Service that records instrumentation for the ingestion pipeline and
    /// can return a snapshot for health dashboards.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IIngestionMetrics
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Call at the very start of an ingestion request.
        /// Returns a disposable that will record the elapsed time when disposed.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IIngestionRequestTimer> BeginRequestAsync();


        /// <summary>
        /// Increment the counter for a specific failure reason.
        /// </summary>
        void IncrementFailure(string reason);


        /// <summary>
        /// Capture a snapshot of the current counters.
        /// </summary>
        Task<IngestionMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync();
    }


    /// <summary>
    /// Disposable timer returned by <see cref="IIngestionMetrics.BeginRequestAsync"/>.
    /// When disposed it records the elapsed time and marks the request as
    /// successful (or you can call <see cref="MarkFailed"/> first).
    /// </summary>
    public interface IIngestionRequestTimer
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Call if the request fails (before disposing).  The timer will still
        /// record the latency but will also bump the failure counters.
        /// </summary>
        void MarkFailed(string reason = "Unknown");


        /// <summary>
        /// Dispose to finalize the measurement (records latency & success count).
        /// </summary>
        void Dispose();
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Observability/IIngestionMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Observability

        /// </summary>

        /// </summary>

    /// Disposable timer returned by <see cref="IIngestionMetrics.BeginRequestAsync"/>.
    /// When disposed it records the elapsed time and marks the request as

    /// </summary>
    public interface IIngestionRequestTimer
    {
        /// <summary>
        /// Call if the request fails (before disposing).  The timer will still
        /// record the latency but will also bump the failure counters.
        /// </summary>

Implementation (place under WeUP.Application/Observability)
csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Observability/IngestionMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Concurrent;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Diagnostics;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using OpenTelemetry.Metrics;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Application.Observability
{
    public sealed class IngestionMetrics : IIngestionMetrics
    {
        private readonly ILogger<IngestionMetrics> _log;
        private static readonly Meter Meter = new Meter("WeUP.Ingestion");


        // OpenTelemetry instruments
        private static readonly Counter<long> RequestCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> SuccessCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_success_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_failure_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureReasonCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_failure_reason_total");
        private static readonly Histogram<double> LatencyHistogram = Meter.CreateHistogram<double>("ingestion_request_latency_ms");


        // In‑process aggregates for snapshot (fast, lock‑free)
        private long _totalRequests;
        private long _successfulRequests;
        private long _failedRequests;
        private double _cumulativeLatencyMs;
        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<string, long> _failureReasons = new();


        public IngestionMetrics(ILogger<IngestionMetrics> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<IIngestionRequestTimer> BeginRequestAsync()
        {
            System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _totalRequests);
            RequestCounter.Add(1);
            var timer = new RequestTimer(this);
            return Task.FromResult<IIngestionRequestTimer>(timer);
        }


        public void IncrementFailure(string reason)
        {
            System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _failedRequests);
            FailureCounter.Add(1);
            FailureReasonCounter.Add(1, new KeyValuePair<string, object>("reason", reason));


            _failureReasons.AddOrUpdate(reason, 1, (_, cur) => cur + 1);
        }


        public Task<IngestionMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync()
        {
            var snapshot = new IngestionMetricsSnapshot(
                TotalRequests:       _totalRequests,
                SuccessfulRequests: _successfulRequests,
                FailedRequests:     _failedRequests,
                AverageLatencyMs:   _successfulRequests == 0
                                    ? 0
                                    : _cumulativeLatencyMs / _successfulRequests,
                FailureReasons:     _failureReasons.ToDictionary(kvp => kvp.Key, kvp => kvp.Value));


            return Task.FromResult(snapshot);
        }


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // Nested timer implementation – records latency and success/failure.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        private sealed class RequestTimer : IIngestionRequestTimer
        {
            private readonly IngestionMetrics _parent;
            private readonly Stopwatch _sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();
            private bool _failed = false;
            private string _failureReason = "Unknown";


            public RequestTimer(IngestionMetrics parent) => _parent = parent;


            public void MarkFailed(string reason = "Unknown")
            {
                _failed = true;
                _failureReason = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(reason) ? "Unknown" : reason;
            }


            public void Dispose()
            {
                _sw.Stop();
                var latencyMs = _sw.Elapsed.TotalMilliseconds;
                LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);


                if (_failed)
                {
                    _parent.IncrementFailure(_failureReason);
                }
                else
                {
                    System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _parent._successfulRequests);
                    System.Threading.Interlocked.Add(ref _parent._cumulativeLatencyMs, latencyMs);
                    SuccessCounter.Add(1);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}


// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Observability/IngestionMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Collections.Concurrent;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Diagnostics;
using System.Linq;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using OpenTelemetry.Metrics;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Application.Observability
{
    public sealed class IngestionMetrics : IIngestionMetrics
    {
        private readonly ILogger<IngestionMetrics> _log;
        private static readonly Meter Meter = new Meter("WeUP.Ingestion");


        // OpenTelemetry instruments
        private static readonly Counter<long> RequestCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> SuccessCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_success_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_failure_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureReasonCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ingestion_requests_failure_reason_total");
        private static readonly Histogram<double> LatencyHistogram = Meter.CreateHistogram<double>("ingestion_request_latency_ms");


        // In‑process aggregates for snapshot (fast, lock‑free)
        private long _totalRequests;
        private long _successfulRequests;
        private long _failedRequests;
        private double _cumulativeLatencyMs;
        private readonly ConcurrentDictionary<string, long> _failureReasons = new();


        public IngestionMetrics(ILogger<IngestionMetrics> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<IIngestionRequestTimer> BeginRequestAsync()
        {
            System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _totalRequests);
            RequestCounter.Add(1);
            var timer = new RequestTimer(this);
            return Task.FromResult<IIngestionRequestTimer>(timer);
        }


        public void IncrementFailure(string reason)
        {
            System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _failedRequests);
            FailureCounter.Add(1);
            FailureReasonCounter.Add(1, new KeyValuePair<string, object>("reason", reason));


            _failureReasons.AddOrUpdate(reason, 1, (_, cur) => cur + 1);
        }


        public Task<IngestionMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync()
        {
            var snapshot = new IngestionMetricsSnapshot(
                TotalRequests:       _totalRequests,
                SuccessfulRequests: _successfulRequests,
                FailedRequests:     _failedRequests,
                AverageLatencyMs:   _successfulRequests == 0
                                    ? 0
                                    : _cumulativeLatencyMs / _successfulRequests,
                FailureReasons:     _failureReasons.ToDictionary(kvp => kvp.Key, kvp => kvp.Value));


            return Task.FromResult(snapshot);
        }


        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        // Nested timer implementation – records latency and success/failure.
        // -----------------------------------------------------------------
        private sealed class RequestTimer : IIngestionRequestTimer
        {
            private readonly IngestionMetrics _parent;
            private readonly Stopwatch _sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();
            private bool _failed = false;
            private string _failureReason = "Unknown";


            public RequestTimer(IngestionMetrics parent) => _parent = parent;


            public void MarkFailed(string reason = "Unknown")
            {
                _failed = true;
                _failureReason = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(reason) ? "Unknown" : reason;
            }


            public void Dispose()
            {
                _sw.Stop();
                var latencyMs = _sw.Elapsed.TotalMilliseconds;
                LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);


                if (_failed)
                {
                    _parent.IncrementFailure(_failureReason);
                }
                else
                {
                    System.Threading.Interlocked.Increment(ref _parent._successfulRequests);
                    System.Threading.Interlocked.Add(ref _parent._cumulativeLatencyMs, latencyMs);
                    SuccessCounter.Add(1);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}


Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	All metric instruments are created once per service (static Meter).
	Prevents duplicate instruments.
	BeginRequestAsync must be called exactly once per ingestion request; the returned IIngestionRequestTimer must be disposed (e.g. via using).
	Guarantees latency is always recorded.
	Failure reasons are string‑based but limited to a short, pre‑defined set (e.g., "OcrError", "NormalizationError").
	Keeps the cardinality of the failure_reason label low for Prometheus.
	The service is thread‑safe – uses Interlocked and ConcurrentDictionary.
	Allows high‑concurrency ingestion.
	GetSnapshotAsync returns a snapshot that does not modify internal counters.
	Safe for concurrent callers (dashboards).
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* IngestionMetricsSnapshot, IIngestionMetrics, and IIngestionRequestTimer compile under WeUP.Contracts.Observability.
* IngestionMetrics implements the contract, records counters, histograms, and maintains in‑process aggregates.
* BeginRequestAsync returns a disposable that records latency and success/failure.
* IncrementFailure updates both the failure counter and the per‑reason dictionary.
* GetSnapshotAsync returns a correct snapshot (tests can verify totals after simulated requests).
* Unit‑test (WeUP.Tests/Observability/IngestionMetricsTests.cs) should cover:
   * Successful request → latency recorded, success counter incremented.
   * Failed request (call MarkFailed("OcrError")) → failure counters incremented, reason recorded.
   * Concurrent requests do not corrupt counts.
* DI registration: services.AddSingleton<IIngestionMetrics, IngestionMetrics>();.
________________

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Contracts/Observability/INormalizationMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System.Threading.Tasks;


namespace WeUP.Contracts.Observability
{
    /// <summary>
    /// Service that records OCR‑stage telemetry.
    /// </summary>
    public interface IOcrMetrics
    {
        Task<IBarcodeTimer> BeginOcrAsync(); // similar to ingestion timer
        Task<OcrMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync();

    public interface IBarcodeTimer

csharp
// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Observability/OcrMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Diagnostics;
using System.Threading;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using OpenTelemetry.Metrics;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Application.Observability
{
    public sealed class OcrMetrics : IOcrMetrics
    {
        private readonly ILogger<OcrMetrics> _log;
        private static readonly Meter Meter = new Meter("WeUP.OCR");
        private static readonly Counter<long> TotalCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ocr_total_requests");
        private static readonly Counter<long> SuccessCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ocr_success_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ocr_failure_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureReasonCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("ocr_failure_reason_total");
        private static readonly Histogram<double> LatencyHistogram = Meter.CreateHistogram<double>("ocr_latency_ms");


        private long _total;
        private long _success;
        private long _failure;
        private double _cumulativeLatencyMs;


        public OcrMetrics(ILogger<OcrMetrics> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<IBarcodeTimer> BeginOcrAsync()
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _total);
            TotalCounter.Add(1);
            return Task.FromResult<IBarcodeTimer>(new OcrTimer(this));
        }


        public Task<OcrMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync()
        {
            var snapshot = new OcrMetricsSnapshot(
                TotalOcrCalls:       _total,
                SuccessfulOcrCalls:  _success,
                FailedOcrCalls:      _failure,
                AverageOcrLatencyMs: _success == 0 ? 0 : _cumulativeLatencyMs / _success);


            return Task.FromResult(snapshot);
        }


        internal void RecordSuccess(double latencyMs)
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _success);
            SuccessCounter.Add(1);
            Interlocked.Add(ref _cumulativeLatencyMs, latencyMs);
            LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);
        }


        internal void RecordFailure(string reason, double latencyMs)
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _failure);
            FailureCounter.Add(1);
            FailureReasonCounter.Add(1, new KeyValuePair<string, object>("reason", reason));
            LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);
        }


        private sealed class OcrTimer : IBarcodeTimer
        {
            private readonly OcrMetrics _parent;
            private readonly Stopwatch _sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();
            private bool _failed = false;
            private string _reason = "Unknown";


            public OcrTimer(OcrMetrics parent) => _parent = parent;


            public void MarkFailed(string reason = "Unknown")
            {
                _failed = true;
                _reason = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(reason) ? "Unknown" : reason;
            }


            public void Dispose()
            {
                _sw.Stop();
                var latency = _sw.Elapsed.TotalMilliseconds;
                if (_failed)
                {
                    _parent.RecordFailure(_reason, latency);
                }
                else
                {
                    _parent.RecordSuccess(latency);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------
// File: WeUP.Application/Observability/NormalizationMetrics.cs
// ------------------------------------------------------------
using System;
using System.Diagnostics;
using System.Threading;
using System.Threading.Tasks;
using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;
using OpenTelemetry.Metrics;
using WeUP.Contracts.Observability;


namespace WeUP.Application.Observability
{
    public sealed class NormalizationMetrics : INormalizationMetrics
    {
        private readonly ILogger<NormalizationMetrics> _log;
        private static readonly Meter Meter = new Meter("WeUP.Normalization");
        private static readonly Counter<long> TotalCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("normalization_total_requests");
        private static readonly Counter<long> SuccessCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("normalization_success_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("normalization_failure_total");
        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureReasonCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("normalization_failure_reason_total");
        private static readonly Histogram<double> LatencyHistogram = Meter.CreateHistogram<double>("normalization_latency_ms");


        private long _total;
        private long _success;
        private long _failure;
        private double _cumulativeLatencyMs;
        private readonly System.Collections.Concurrent.ConcurrentDictionary<string, long> _failureReasons = new();


        public NormalizationMetrics(ILogger<NormalizationMetrics> log) => _log = log;


        public Task<INormalizationTimer> BeginNormalizationAsync()
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _total);
            TotalCounter.Add(1);
            return Task.FromResult<INormalizationTimer>(new NormalizationTimer(this));
        }


        public Task<NormalizationMetricsSnapshot> GetSnapshotAsync()
        {
            var snapshot = new NormalizationMetricsSnapshot(
                TotalCalls:       _total,
                SuccessfulCalls:  _success,
                FailedCalls:      _failure,
                AverageLatencyMs: _success == 0 ? 0 : _cumulativeLatencyMs / _success);


            return Task.FromResult(snapshot);
        }


        internal void RecordSuccess(double latencyMs)
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _success);
            SuccessCounter.Add(1);
            Interlocked.Add(ref _cumulativeLatencyMs, latencyMs);
            LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);
        }


        internal void RecordFailure(string reason, double latencyMs)
        {
            Interlocked.Increment(ref _failure);
            FailureCounter.Add(1);
            FailureReasonCounter.Add(1, new KeyValuePair<string, object>("reason", reason));
            _failureReasons.AddOrUpdate(reason, 1, (_, cur) => cur + 1);
            LatencyHistogram.Record(latencyMs);
        }


        private sealed class NormalizationTimer : INormalizationTimer
        {
            private readonly NormalizationMetrics _parent;
            private readonly Stopwatch _sw = Stopwatch.StartNew();
            private bool _failed;
            private string _reason = "Unknown";


            public NormalizationTimer(NormalizationMetrics parent) => _parent = parent;


            public void MarkFailed(string reason = "Unknown")
            {
                _failed = true;
                _reason = string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(reason) ? "Unknown" : reason;
            }


            public void Dispose()
            {
                _sw.Stop();
                var latency = _sw.Elapsed.TotalMilliseconds;
                if (_failed)
                {
                    _parent.RecordFailure(_reason, latency);
                }
                else
                {
                    _parent.RecordSuccess(latency);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

using System;

        private static readonly Counter<long> FailureCounter = Meter.CreateCounter<long>("normalization_failure_total");

Rules & Constraints
Rule
	Reason
	Both services expose a Begin…Async method that returns a disposable timer.
	Mirrors the pattern used in ingestion metrics, guarantees latency is always recorded.
	Metrics are cumulative (no resets) – suitable for Prometheus.
	Prometheus works best with monotonically increasing counters.
	Failure reasons are labelled ("reason") on the corresponding counters.
	Enables Grafana to break down failures by type.
	All counters and histograms are created once (static Meter).
	Prevents duplicate instrument registration.
	Services are thread‑safe (use Interlocked and ConcurrentDictionary).
	Allows high‑concurrency OCR/normalization pipelines.
	Acceptance‑Criteria
* OcrMetricsSnapshot, INormalizationMetrics, NormalizationMetricsSnapshot, IBarcodeTimer, INormalizationTimer compile.
* OcrMetrics records total calls, successes, failures, per‑reason counters, and latency histogram.
* NormalizationMetrics records analogous telemetry for the normalization stage.
* Unit‑tests verify:
   * Successful OCR call → success
